# Firmware internals

How the scroll-wheel payload hooks the stock Shanling Q2 firmware. For the user-facing behavior it produces, see the [README](../README.md); build and release steps are in [building.md](building.md) and [releasing.md](releasing.md).

`release/bin/demo`, `usr/bin/hciplayer` (the PEQ filter, one branch, [Large MP3s](#large-mp3s), two calls, [MP3 ReplayGain](#mp3-replaygain), and the MQA gate and probe length, [MQA detection](#mqa-detection)), `usr/bin/bluealsa` (AAC capability and Bluetooth DROP handling), its launcher `usr/bin/bluealsa2` ([keep-alive](#bluetooth-keep-alive)), `usr/bin/checkappprocess.sh` (one sleep, [Battery](#battery)), the boot logo, `home_page.bin` (the Coverflow card; the iPod Home list) and the artist page inside `rootfs.squashfs` change, and the stock EQ preset page and the images only the stock EQ pages show (`STOCK_EQ` in `tools/build.py`) are removed. iPod additionally changes the other audited assets in `patch/ipod.json`: local browsing, settings and streaming pages, the equalizer page, the status bar, Now Playing and the theme (`styles/default.bin`). It also removes the 14 carousel images (`CAROUSEL`) and does not add the Coverflow card icons. The build adds `usr/bin/q2video` ([Videos](#videos)), the new Local Music icons and Streaming's Spotify icon. The kernel is byte-identical, and the builder checks every other inode's name, type, mtime, mode, uid and gid against stock.

## Hooks

Checked MIPS prologues redirect into a payload at `0xb00000`, using the final unused `PT_NULL` program header. demo's `.pdr` section, outside every segment, is zeroed for rootfs space ([building.md](building.md)). Trampolines restore the stock GOT base and resume each original function after its PIC setup. Writable input and position state is mapped at `0xb80000`, a `NOLOAD` section (with any stray `.bss`): the segment's memory size covers it, the file does not.

`HOOKS` and the `WM_PAINT_LEAF` in `tools/build.py` are hooked in both variants; `IPOD_HOOKS` and the `IPOD_LEAF` only in iPod, so the Legacy build keeps those entry points stock.

| Stock callback                        | Address    | Purpose                                                                                                      |
| ------------------------------------- | ---------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `on_wm_keyup_before_fun`              | `0x4e85c8` | Stock lock filter first, then wheel/centre navigation (and iPod scrub)                                       |
| `on_wm_tsdown_before_fun`             | `0x4e8bd0` | Preserve stock touch processing, interrupt wheel glide (and iPod pull-to-search)                             |
| `widget_on_paint_border`              | `0x6596a0` | Outline (Stock); chevrons and letter (iPod) after children                                                   |
| `widget_dispatch`                     | `0x65e0ec` | Observe a native click before its app callback changes the UI                                                |
| `on_wm_keylong_fun`                   | `0x4e873c` | Play/Pause hold opens the queue menu, or toggles a radio favourite                                           |

| `home_page_init`                      | `0x523c84` | Binds the Coverflow card (iPod: row), then iPod Home art and layout                                          |

Single checked instruction words are patched as well. Both variants: `mclNextSong`'s shuffle pick (`0x5addf0`, [Queue menu](#queue-menu)), the long-Return Home call (`0x4e8924`, [ipod.md](ipod.md#hold-return)), `check_mem_thd`'s entry (`0x512050`, returns at once, [Battery](#battery)) and `device_standby`'s screen wake (`0x4f7d24`, [Wake](#wake)). iPod, from `tools/ipod.py`: the row pitch, artwork and Now Playing bar immediates and the row-layouter calls listed in `patch/ipod.json`, the folder rebind's resize call (`0x522410`, removed), the boot resume call (`0x523de0`, [Boot resume](#boot-resume-ipod)) and one data word, the `list_view` children layouter's layout slot (`0x926930`, stock `0x5e9eb4`), which becomes `ipod_list_layout` for the settings rows ([ipod.md](ipod.md#settings)). The manifest's `compact_code` lists every changed word.

Stock V1.32 turns the touch wheel into key events 172/173: the encoder thread (`0x6258e0`, unnamed in the symbol table, after `encoderknob_thread_run` at `0x6256a0`) reads tslib samples (x, 1-200 a turn, and pressure) and calls `get_direction` (`0x62587c`), but only once per 80 ms gate (200 ms after touching), at most one tick per look, dropping any travel past its threshold. On the device that cost more than half the travel (27 units per tick against a threshold of 12), a single tick for a fast half turn and nothing in a touch's first 200 ms. So the build replaces the tick logic: the thread's `ts_read` call (`WHEEL_TS_READ` in `tools/build.py`) goes through `ringnav_ts_read`, and `wheel_sample` (`patch/playback.c`) makes a tick of every `WHEEL_TICK_UNITS` (28, a seventh of a turn) of travel at the default Wheel Sensitivity (below), carrying the rest; a reversal starts the count again and a lift drops it. It posts each tick with stock's poster (`WHEEL_POST`, `0x6257f0`) as a key-down (`0x110`) then a key-up (`0x114`), and hands stock the touch-down x, so stock's own logic sees a still finger and posts nothing. The payload moves on the release, at `on_wm_keyup_before_fun`; iPod also sees the press ([iPod row clicks](#ipod-row-clicks)). These events are not fixed physical detents. Below, a tick is one such event and a step is a row move the payload makes.

**Input queue.** The wheel, the touchscreen and the buttons all post to AWTK's main-loop queue (`main_loop_simple_init`, `0x68b70c`: `event_queue_create(20)` at `loop+0x38`), which drops its oldest event when full. A tick is two events, so a fast turn while the UI was busy could evict a touch's release: the window manager then kept the pointer pressed (a row stuck in its pressed shade), and `ringnav` dropped every tick until the next tap. `wheel_post` reads the queue's `r`, `w`, `full` and `capacity` (unlocked; off by one at worst) through the poster's main loop (`WHEEL_INFO_LOOP`, `LOOP_QUEUE`) and drops a tick that would leave fewer than `WHEEL_QUEUE_FREE` (8) slots, so a busy UI loses ticks it could not have shown yet instead of a touch or a key, and nothing replays later. Checks in `test/playback.py`.

Stock drops wheel ticks for 800 ms after Centre or Return (`KEY_LOCKOUT`, 8, counted down per 100 ms) so a press that brushes the wheel does not scroll; `ringnav()` keeps only its first `LOCKOUT_KEEP` (2) x 100 ms. A tick that lands while a page slides, or before the new page's list loads, is held (`hold_wheel`) instead of dropped: up to `HOLD_MAX` (8) of them, a reversal cancelling, play into the page one per `HOLD_POLL_MS` (16 ms) once it is ready, unless Centre or Return comes first or `HOLD_MS` (1.2 s) passes. So a turn begun on Back or Select carries on into the page.

**Wheel Sensitivity.** **System settings → Display → Controls → Wheel Sensitivity**, the last row in both builds (`ringnav_display`, the `system_display` icon), reads `Wheel Sensitivity: 50%` and sets the travel per tick: 50-200% in tens, `WHEEL_TICK_UNITS` x 50 / sensitivity, so 50% is 28 units, 100% 14, 130% 10.75, 150% 9.3 and 200% 7 (3.5 to 14 ticks a turn). `wheel_sample` counts in sixteenths of a unit (`WHEEL_FINE`), so the carry keeps the fraction and every step differs. It is `Q2POD WHEELSENSITIVITY` in `config.ini`, read on the first UI pass (`playback_poll` calls `wheel_pct`); a missing key or anything but a canonical ten in 50-200 is 50%. That is 1.0.6's key and scale (1.0.6 rounded the travel to whole units), so a value saved in 1.0.6 comes back as it was. A value saved before 1.0.6 comes back with the same label but about twice its old speed: the old engine's threshold scaled the same way but lost about half the travel to stock's 80 ms gate (27 units a tick at 100%), so its 100% felt like today's 50%. 1.0.7 removed the setting (fixed at 28) after 1.0.6 had kept those pre-1.0.6 values under the new meaning. `wheel_units` is one word the UI thread writes and the encoder thread reads, 0 until read, which `wheel_sample` takes as 50%. A change applies on the next sample: when the travel changes mid-turn, the carry is scaled to keep its share of a tick (`wh_acc * new / old`), so it stays under the new travel and fires no burst. Only the travel changes: `LOCKOUT_KEEP`, `hold_wheel`, the list and scrub ramps (`WHEEL_RUN_MS`, `WHEEL_RAMP_MS`, `LIST_FIRST_MS`) and every consumer (rows, volume including screen-off, scrub, Coverflow, Books, Videos) count ticks and time, so they just get more or fewer ticks a turn. iPod's lyrics page takes the raw travel instead and moves a row per tick's worth of it ([Lyrics wheel](#scrub-ipod)). The row opens [Crossfade](#crossfade)'s re-dressed Boot volume page (`xfade_open` with a nonzero context, `xf.wheel` in `patch/crossfade.c`): titled Wheel Sensitivity, the switch hidden, its slider 50-200 in steps of 10 (a drag snaps to the nearest ten), +/- and the wheel a ten a tick (`xfade_key`), and Centre going back as on Brightness (`edit_key`). Each change is saved with `write_int_config` (a drag at its release, as Crossfade below) and the Display row's label (named `label_wheel`) follows.

## Supported screens and panes

Navigation requires a supported top-window name from `patch/contexts.inc`, screen-on and no lock/test/guide/power-off/USB-link/Bluetooth-receive screen, and a navigable pane: a vertical `scroll_view`, a `table_client`, a `slide_menu` or, in iPod, a dialog or page flagged `BUTTONS`, whose buttons are its rows and which never scrolls ([ipod.md](ipod.md#pop-ups)). Horizontal and page-snapping scroll views are not candidates, so they cannot make a page look like it has two panes. When two panes are visible and neither owns the selection, the first wheel turn chooses the first pane in UI order. Later turns follow the selected pane, and tapping a row switches ownership. If both panes already hold a selection, the wheel event is left alone. Only active `pages` children are searched. The bounded walk collects at most 512 targets in a non-virtual list; virtual music tables navigate by total logical row count instead.

A native click chooses its pane from the actual target before walking up to the nearest collected ancestor. A successful selection clears the other pane’s selection and invalidates both panes, so the drawn selection, wheel and centre follow the row that owns the tap. Hidden, disabled and inactive-page panes remain excluded.

## Selection

Selection is stored in widget-owned integer properties on the navigation surface, independently of AWTK's focused flag. The drawn selection (outline or bar) and centre action resolve that same logical selection against the current entries. Centre dispatches a synchronous native `EVT_CLICK`, so a queued click cannot hit a row rebound between selection and delivery. It never dereferences the target after delivery.

## Wheel movement

Lists with at most `SHORT_LIST_MAX` (16) logical entries always move one row per step. Virtual tables use their total logical count, not the recycled row pool size. Longer lists use a continuous ramp: a same-direction tick spaced at most `WHEEL_RUN_MS` (140 ms) from the previous one extends a spin run, and the step grows with the run's duration, capped at `WHEEL_MAX_STEP` (8) rows. It is a duration ramp, not a measure of the wheel's angular speed. `ramp()` takes the curve from its caller:

| Caller                                                              | One row while the run is under | Then                                      | Eight rows from |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------ | ----------------------------------------- | --------------- |
| Legacy build; iPod's pixel-scroll fallback and [scrub](#scrub-ipod) | `WHEEL_RAMP_MS` (100 ms)       | `1 + run / WHEEL_RAMP_MS`                 | 700 ms          |
| iPod row lists and button dialogs                                   | `LIST_FIRST_MS` (300 ms)       | `2 + (run - 300) / LIST_RAMP_MS` (200 ms) | 1500 ms         |

The pixel-scroll fallback is a pane without clickable rows, which scrolls `RING_STEP` pixels per step. A pause, reversal, touch, native click, centre press, list boundary or rejected navigation resets the run. Acceleration belongs to the current window, pane and browsing scope; a list resize also resets it.

Wheel movement cancels existing momentum and uses the stock `scroll_view_set_offset` / `table_client_set_yoffset` setters immediately, reloading table row references after synchronous rebinding. Each wheel step then dispatches `EVT_SCROLL_END` (`0x14f`), which touch momentum sends when it settles: the setters send only `0x14e`, which the album page takes as pause, and stock lists queue their visible covers on scroll end (album page `0x4a440c`). Remembered-position restoration retains its separate glide. This follows the row-based approach of Rockbox's [H2/Eros Q wheel mapping](https://github.com/Rockbox/rockbox/blob/master/apps/keymaps/keymap-erosq.c) and [list implementation](https://github.com/Rockbox/rockbox/blob/master/apps/gui/list.c).

Selected and restored rows use a `SCROLL_MARGIN` of 12 pixels above and below, reduced to half the spare viewport height in tight lists and clamped at content boundaries. Rows taller than the viewport align their top edge when selected or restored, keeping their title visible.

## List ends

The local file and music lists, the hold menu (`sortselect_dialog`, also the sort picker), Now Playing's More page, Playback settings and System settings carry over at their ends, and so does the Library's Albums list (`album_page` with class `0xf003`, which `ring_list` checks by class, since the same page shows an album's songs at `0xff10`, and those keep hard ends). `patch/contexts.inc` marks those audited row lists with `ring`, and iPod Home does too, while other grids, other settings menus, dynamic pages, and the Stock build's Home carousel keep hard ends. The first tick past an end nudges only the drawn selection by `BUMP_PX` for `BUMP_MS`, without moving the viewport. Further ticks hard-stop there, bumping again, while the wheel keeps turning: each one re-arms. Only the first same-direction tick at least `EDGE_PAUSE_MS` (300 ms) after the last stopped one selects the opposite end of the same list and reveals it immediately. The existing boundary rule already reset the spin run, so the wrap lands at one row per tick. The arm is cleared by a move off the boundary row, a reversal, touch, a native click or a centre press.

Every tick against an end bumps and re-arms, so the pause is measured between ticks. Reaching an end, a bump and the wrap end the run and leave no speed; the next tick is a first one.

## Key Tone

The click is the MCU's buzzer, the device's own speaker: `buzzeer_switch` (`0x4f3cc8`) runs `system("cmd_mcu write_str buzzer")` when the byte `g_keytone_flag` (`0xa38c31`, the Key Tone setting) is set, and nothing else; stock sends it whatever plays and whatever is plugged in. Both builds hook it (`ringnav_buzzer`, stock resumed at `0x4f3cd4`), so every click, stock's three call sites and the iPod row click below, passes one gate: none while headphones or another output listen: either jack (`g_po_status` `0xa38af3`, 3.5 mm, GPIO `PA07`; `g_bal_status` `0xa38af2`, 4.4 mm, `PA08`; 1 plugged, kept by `check_headset_status` at `0x4e8e14`) or an output way other than the DAC (`mclGetOutputWay`, `0x5ac484`: 1 Bluetooth, 2 USB DAC, as `config_outputchannel` sets it). Otherwise stock runs, so with nothing plugged in the buzzer clicks as before, music playing or not, and Key Tone off is still silent. Stock runs on a detached worker (`tone_worker`), not the UI thread: with PWM clock gating, `cmd_mcu`'s write wakes the MCU, waits out the buzzer and parks it again (`tools/mcu_pwm_clock.c`), which stalled the UI loop on every click of a fast scroll. A click while the last one still sounds (`tone_busy`) is dropped, as the buzzer could not play it yet; Key Tone off and the iPod row latch are still checked on the UI thread, before the worker starts. There is no headphone click: a paused hciplayer keeps its PCM (`hw:1,0`, or `bluealsa`, one client per PCM, no `dmix`) open and stock's pause mutes the DAC (`mclSetMute`, `player_play_pause` `0x5157d8`), so a click has no free output; silence is the fallback, never the speaker.

### Wake

**System settings → Power → Wake** (`Q2POD WAKEDOUBLE`, read with Charge Limit and Low Power). With it on and the screen off (`g_backlight_status` clear), `wake_gate` in `ringnav_input` (`window_manager_dispatch_input_event`, before the window manager and every key hook) drops a centre (218, the power key) `EVT_KEY_DOWN` (`0x110`, as `widget_on_keydown` tests at `0x6627c4`; `0x111` is its long press) and its release, and records the press. A second press within `WAKE_MS` (500 ms, looser than `DOUBLE_CLICK_MS` since it is done blind) passes, press and release, so stock wakes the screen on it, and holding that accepted press still reaches stock's long-press power-off. Return (170) is dropped while dark in either mode, press, long press (`0x111`) and release, so a bump of Back leaves the page alone. Every other key passes, so the wheel's volume, the media buttons and Spotify's keys work dark; with the screen on nothing is touched and a pending pass is cleared, and with the setting off a centre press passes at once. Another centre press within `WAKE_MS` of the one that woke the screen (`woke_at`, either mode), or of the first UI loop pass with the screen lit (`ringnav_sleep` restarts `woke_at` then), is dropped with its release (`wake_eat`), the blind double press's window: turning the screen on (stock's `on_wm_keyup_fun`) and that pass's paint stall the loop about that long, and `wake_gate` times presses as they are dispatched, so a second press made during the stall arrives after it. So a quick double press from dark only wakes: it neither clicks the selected row nor, as a screen-on double press, turns the screen straight off. Centre's repeated downs (first at about 258 ms, then every 50 ms) share their original press's fate, so holding a waking press keeps the release that lights the screen. A dropped press stays dropped with its repeats and release; holding it does not add a wake gesture. A passing hold keeps stock's long-press power-off. AWTK generates the long event downstream of this input hook. Stock's Standby (`check_to_poweroption` calls `device_standby`, `0x4f7a2c`, which suspends in `toolsIntoStandby`) wakes the screen once any key resumes it, a `bal awake_screen` (`0x4f7d24`) whatever the key; that call becomes `li $v0, 1`, skipping it in both modes, so the resuming key acts as it does dark: Play/Pause plays, a centre press wakes (Single Press) or counts as the first of two (Double Press). Stock then sets `BOOT_KEY_GUARD` (`0xa37c8a`), which drops the next centre release so the resuming press cannot turn the screen straight off again; the first centre down while dark consumes it and resets held-key tracking, so repeats keep that press's fate even if Standby lost the preceding release.

Before `wake_gate`, `chord_gate` drops a whole centre press (down, repeats, long press and release) whose first down arrives while an edge button (Return, Play/Pause, Previous or Next, `edge_held`) is held, within `CHORD_MS` (500 ms) of that button's press: a loose wheel, or a thumb on the rim between a button and the centre, can close both switches on one press, which then only does the edge button's job. The edge button itself always passes. A centre press already held when an edge button goes down passes too, but an edge press within `CHORD_MS` of it removes its long-press timer (`centre_hold`, kept by `ringnav_hold_timer`), so a rim press held near a corner never reaches stock's centre hold, the Shut Down dialog; its release still clicks. The time limit covers an edge release that never arrives (Standby while held); once the button is up, Centre works at once. A Return press that starts within `RETURN_GHOST_MS` (135 ms) of a Return hold's release (at least `KEY_HOLD_MS`) is dropped whole: letting go of Hold Return could send a second press. A centre down more than 300 ms after the last centre event is a new press, not a repeat, so a dropped press whose release was lost does not drop the next one, and a passing one takes a fresh time for the edge press after it. An edge button's down more than 300 ms after its last press or repeat (`edge_seen`) is likewise a new press with a fresh `edge_at`, so a lost edge release does not cost the next press its guard.

### Volume guard

**System settings → Power → Volume Guard** (`Q2POD VOLGUARD`, off by default, read with Wake into `wheel_guard` in `patch/playback.c`), against pocket brushes moving the volume (#46). With it on and the screen off, `wheel_sample` (the encoder thread, before any tick is posted) counts a touch's travel one way in `wh_guard`, in sixteenths of a unit, and posts no tick until `WHEEL_DARK_GUARD` units (100, half a turn of the 200-unit wheel, whatever Wheel Sensitivity is). That travel is dropped, the dead zone, and the tick carry starts from zero there. The wheel is then open: it ticks as lit, reversals and new touches included, until it has gone `WHEEL_DARK_IDLE_MS` (1.5 s, `time_now_ms`) without travel. A reversal, a lift or that pause before the guard is passed starts the count again, and so does the screen coming on, where the wheel is never guarded. Nothing is held for later, so `hold_wheel`, the volume overlay and Bluetooth volume see only the ticks that pass; with the setting off the dark wheel is stock's. Both constants are in `patch/offsets.inc`.

### Standby limit

**System settings → Power → Standby Limit** (`Q2POD STANDBYLIMIT`, Off by default, read with Wake): **1 h**, **8 h** or **24 h** (`STANDBY_LIMIT_1` to `_3` in `patch/offsets.inc`) powers the Q2 off once one Standby has lasted that long, so a Q2 left asleep for days does not run flat. Stock's Standby (`check_to_poweroption` calls `device_standby` once its timer runs out with nothing playing and no cable in) stops the player, powers the DAC down, stops Wi-Fi and Bluetooth, unmounts the card and unloads the Wi-Fi and card drivers; then `toolsIntoStandby` (`0x5be57c`) writes `mem` to `/sys/power/state`, which returns when a key resumes it. Nothing else ends it: stock never sets the RTC's wake alarm. `ringnav_standby` takes `toolsIntoStandby`. With a limit it reads the RTC (`/sys/class/rtc/rtc0/since_epoch`, UTC as [the clock](#clock) keeps it), sets `wakealarm` that far ahead (after writing `0`, since rtc-sysfs refuses a new alarm while one is set), suspends through stock and clears the alarm on the resume. A resume at or past the alarm is the alarm's: the next UI loop pass (`standby_poll`, in `power_poll`) opens `poweroff_page`, as `check_to_poweroption`'s Auto power-off does, unless a USB cable is in (`g_usbdet_value`) or local music plays. Anything that resumes it sooner, a key or the charger, leaves it on, and the next Standby counts from zero. With the RTC unreadable or the alarm refused, Standby is stock's. The alarm's resume runs all of stock's resume first (the drivers, and Wi-Fi if it was on) and stays dark (`STANDBY_WAKE`, [Wake](#wake)), so the Q2 powers off a few seconds after the alarm. On a Q2, a wake alarm set before Standby resumed it 5 s after the alarm time, dark and paused, with no key pressed.

### iPod row clicks

Stock clicks on the press. `on_wm_keydown_before_fun` (`0x4e8424`) calls `buzzeer_switch` (the exported spelling; its only three call sites). A latch (`0xa37c90`), set after the call and cleared by `on_wm_keyup_before_fun`, keeps it to one click a press. Every wheel tick is a press and a release, so stock clicks on every tick, whether or not a row moves.

iPod hooks that callback (`ringnav_keydown`, stock resumed at `0x4e8430`). For a wheel press that the release will take as row navigation it saves `g_keytone_flag`, clears it, runs the whole stock body and restores the byte before returning stock's result: the power timer, the lock gates and both latches run as stock, and only the click is missing. Besides `buzzeer_switch`, the byte is read by `config_init` and by the System settings page's Key Tone row and its click handler (`0x4cc0a0`, `0x4cc2dc`), all on the UI thread, and the stock body calls nothing back into the UI, so nothing else sees it cleared. Whether a press is such a press is decided from the widget tree alone (screen usable, a `contexts.inc` window, a pane that is not a `slide_menu` and holds a clickable row); nothing is loaded, selected or restored. Every other press runs stock unchanged: the buttons, the volume, scrub, carousels and other slide menus, the pixel-scroll fallback, and the wheel while the screen is off or locked.

The press leaves its key in `tone_key`. Every key-down rewrites it and every key-up clears it, so a dropped release cannot carry it into a later input. On the release, `ringnav()` calls `buzzeer_switch(1)` once when that tick's step changes the selected row, with the setting as it is then: one click for a step of any size and for a wrap (a held tick's replay included), and none for a hard end, a bump, a rejected tick (a pointer down, stock's wheel lockout after a button) or a release whose press stock already clicked. If the page stops being a list between the press and the release, that tick is silent and the next press is stock's again. The call is in the wheel path, not in `select()` or the paint hooks, which also run for restoration, touch and repaints. The Stock build has no key-down hook and clicks as stock.

## Scrollbar

Wheel motion also wakes the native scrollbar of the controlled surface. `native_scrollbar()` finds the surface's `scroll_bar_m` sibling under its `list_view` or `table_view` parent and calls the stock `scroll_bar_scroll_to` with the bar's current value; the stock bar keeps ownership of the thumb value and runs its own show, wait and fade lifecycle. An initially transparent bar is still woken, and a surface without a native bar is unchanged. The payload retains no bar pointer and no fade timer, so page destruction and widget recycling need no extra handling. The end bump's `fx_token` widget property and its `fx_expire` timer remain separate from scrollbar visibility: the token rejects a recycled surface, and the timer repaints the selection at the `BUMP_MS` (120 ms) deadline.

## Centre button

A centre release arms a stock UI timer for `DOUBLE_CLICK_MS` (250 ms). A second release before expiry on the same live selection cancels confirmation and passes through the stock downstream key-up handler to turn the screen off. On a listed page where no row confirmation is pending (an empty list, nothing selected, a page still sliding in, the Books reader whose Centre is its caption), `ringnav` times the presses itself (`dc_top`, `dc_at`): a second within `DOUBLE_CLICK_MS` on the same window replays the release to `on_wm_keyup_fun`, so a double press turns the screen off on every page. Videos keep Centre for seeking. A single release dispatches exactly one synchronous click at expiry. Stock's key-up filter rejects wheel releases while a centre-key lockout (`KEY_LOCKOUT`, `0xa37c89`) is non-zero: the centre key sets it to 8 and `on_wm_timer_setup_state` counts it down every 100 ms, so a page opened by the centre button would ignore the wheel for up to 800 ms. The click clears it just before dispatch, so the opened page scrolls at once; a double press keeps stock's lockout. Touch, wheel input and invalid navigation state cancel pending confirmation. The timer resolves the target from the live menu and requires the original window, surface, content scope, logical selection, row count and row-text identity to match. Widget-owned tokens also reject reused window/surface addresses, and an ordinary list requires the armed row widget's own token; virtual tables resolve recycled pool widgets by logical index. An overdue confirmation runs before the power and lock gates that follow it. No delayed row pointer is retained; pending state clears before dispatch. Timer allocation failure consumes the press without activating anything.

## Setting editors

Stock's setting pages without a list take the wheel and Centre themselves (`edit_key`), ahead of list navigation. **Brightness**, **Maximum volume**, **Startup volume** and **Balance** (`backlight_page`, `maxvol_page`, `bootvol_page`, `balance_page`): the wheel dispatches a click to the page's own `img_add` or `img_dec`, so each tick is one stock step with stock's limits and saving; Centre goes back. The pull-down's brightness slider (`statusbar_dialog`) has no +/-, so the wheel sets its `value` by one within its `min`/`max`, which fires stock's change callback; Centre there stays stock's. Its swipe up to close may start on that slider, which then moved the brightness before stock took the swipe. `ringnav_dispatch` keeps the slider's pointer-down back (Quick Settings' only, under `statusbar_dialog`; Settings' Brightness page is stock's): the slider gets it, at the touch's start, only once a move goes `SLIDE_PX` (10 px) more sideways than up or down, or with a tap's release. A touch that first moves `SLIDE_PX` up or down, or any while stock slides the pull-down or it follows a swipe (`g_dropdown_menu_state` 1 or 2), never reaches the slider, and a press it already has is aborted (`EVT_POINTER_ABORT`). A withheld event returns 0 rather than `STOP`, which would also end its parents' handling, so the window manager's pointer-down still records the touch's start (`g_pressdown_y`) that stock's swipe measures from. A drag that set the brightness before the pull-down moved keeps it. A held Play/Pause in the pull-down (`ringnav_keylong`, `quick_wheel_toggle`) switches its wheel to stock's volume and back (no toast: its `msginfo_dialog` would cover the pull-down and take the wheel for 2 s) and saves the choice as `Q2POD` `QSVOLUME` (0 brightness, 1 volume); in volume mode `edit_key` leaves the wheel to stock, and the release is swallowed as the queue menu's (`qm_press`). Stock's swipe down (`on_wm_tsmove_fun`) sets `g_dropdown_menu_state` 1 and opens the dialog off screen; only the touch's release reaching the dialog starts its slide (its pointer-up handler adds the `timer_idex` timer, which ends at 3, open). A release the dialog never gets, as with a swipe while the player is still starting, left it on top and invisible at 1, so the wheel went to its brightness or volume until a touch showed it. `quick_stuck` treats it at 1 or 2 with no touch down and no `timer_idex` timer as stuck: `ringnav_keydown` swallows the press and `ringnav` closes it on the release (`dialog_quit`, whose close handler sets the state back to 0); that key does nothing else. Only keys check it: a touch on the hidden dialog shows it, as stock already did. **Set date and time** (`manualtime_page`: year, month, day, hour, minute) and the **sleep timer** (`sleepshutdown_page`: hour, minute) edit one `text_selector` at a time, framed in two white pixels by the border hook: the wheel steps its selected index (`SELECTOR_INDEX`, set through stock `text_selector_set_selected_index`) within `text_selector_count_options`, Centre moves to the next field, switching the date page's `slide_view` to the time page after the day, then to OK (`btn_enter`), drawn while it is next as a selected row (Minimal's off-white `MINIMAL_FILL` card, `SEL_RADIUS` corners, its label dark at `EDIT_OK_PX`) rather than framed, which the next Centre clicks; a turn on OK goes back to the last field (Previous) or the first (Next). A swipe between the date and time pages moves the field to that page's first. The side keys (Next, Previous) move between the fields and OK the same way, stopping at the ends, ahead of stock's track skip (`field_key`). On the date and time page OK sets the clock (stock's `set_systime`, which stays on the page) and then goes back to the Time page (`time_ok`, `navigator_back`), after a tap (`ringnav_dispatch`) or Centre's click (`edit_single`); the sleep timer's OK stays stock's. Centre waits `DOUBLE_CLICK_MS` (`center_press`) and clears `KEY_LOCKOUT` as a list's click does, and a double press is stock's screen toggle. The field lives in the page's `_edit_field` property, so it dies with the page.

**Wheel QS** (`playback.c` `qs_begin`, Q2POD `WHEELQS`, default off, Controls row). The ring has no middle, so a swipe across it is a touch at one edge, then within `QS_GAP_MS` (400 ms) a touch at the opposite edge: either after a lift, or as a position jump during one touch. Each touch must move under `QS_SLACK` (20 units), so turning never triggers it. It writes a finger on the screen (q2remote's swipe) to `hyn_ts`, streamed with the gesture, and stock slides the pull-down with it as for a finger. The second touch puts the finger down where the swipe starts and, for the gap over Centre the ring cannot sense, straight on to where the gesture's pace puts it; the pace is the first touch's down to the second's, doubled and clamped to `QS_MIN_MS`-`QS_MAX_MS` (150-500 ms) for the whole swipe. Each of the second touch's samples (`qs_follow`) moves it on at that pace, and the lift (`qs_finish`) ends it at the far end. A second touch that turns past `QS_SLACK` lifts the finger where it is and leaves `qs_back`. Stock slides the pull-down on a release the way its swipe started it (`g_slide_direction`, 1 open or 0 closed, set once as the swipe starts: `on_wm_tsmove_fun` for a pull down, the dialog's move handler for a push up), wherever the finger lifts, so moving the finger back would not undo it, and a lift where it went down is a tap. `ringnav_dispatch` sets `g_slide_direction` from `qs_back` on that release, ahead of the dialog's pointer-up handler, so the pull-down slides back to how it was; any touch down but the gesture's own finger (`qs_down`, whose down the UI may handle after the turn) clears a stale `qs_back`. The writes happen on the encoder thread, a frame per sample and only during a gesture; nothing sleeps. Jump handling only runs while the screen is lit. Limits: either direction toggles, since the wheel's top x is not known; the pace is a straight line with no easing. ponytail: gate on the first touch's x once a device trace finds the top.

Pickers flagged `CHOICE` in `patch/contexts.inc` (Play mode, Filter, Language, Sort and the like) open on the row holding stock's `select` check image instead of the remembered position, so the bar starts on the current setting. In Minimal, stock's white radio images (`select`, `unselect`) would vanish on the selected row's off-white card, so the border hook draws them again over it in the card's dark ink (`paint_radio`): the 26-pixel ring and, when checked, the 12-pixel dot. The Wi-Fi, Bluetooth and Tidal rows' white delete mark (`navbar_delete`) is drawn again there as `list_delete`, a build-added copy that loads in `list_into`'s grey; `navbar_delete` itself stays white, as the playlist page's Delete wants it.

USB mode's exit asks whether to scan for music in a `confirminfo_dialog` before it clears `g_usblink_status`; with the cable already out (`g_usbdet_value` 0), that prompt alone counts as usable, so its buttons take the wheel and Centre.

## Queue menu

Holding Play/Pause (key 171) fires the stock long press once per press, after 700 ms (`KEY_HOLD_MS`, as Return's hold does; every other key keeps stock's 1000 ms, 500 for key 232, unused on the Q2): `HOLD_KEYS` turns the key-down's `timer_add` into `ringnav_hold_timer`, with the key in `$a3`, and the timer's `input_device_status_find_key_long_press_info` into `ringnav_hold_info`, so both the timer and its threshold give those two keys `KEY_HOLD_MS`: `input_device_status` (`wm+0x98`, via `window_manager_get_input_device_status` at `0x66e734`) dispatches `0x111` to the window manager, where stock `on_wm_keylong_fun` has no action for 171. `ringnav_keylong` takes it only with the navigation gates, AirPlay off (`airplayGetFlag() != 2`), no batch-select mode (`g_navbar_status`) and the row a centre press would open, on a `CTX_LOCAL`/`CTX_FOLDER` page, an artist's page (`artistinfo_page`, whose tabs fill the showlist) included. There the row's class is `g_local_classinfo_save+0`, which its tabs' `load_localartist_list` sets (`0xff07` songs, `0xff01` albums); `g_class_type` keeps the class the page opened or was last returned to with. The page's row count must equal `deque_size(*p_deque_showlist)`: these lists bind row _i_ to showlist record _i_ (Local Songs `table_row_of` → `deque_at` at `0x4a8838`), and a grid or extra clickable row fails the check. Everything else tail-calls the stock body.

The release still reaches `playpause_quick_click` (`on_wm_keyup_fun` `0x4e8b7c`), which has no long-press check. The hold stores the key record's u64 press time (record `+8`; 16 records of `0x18` from `+0x30`); the key-up hook swallows a Play/Pause release carrying that time and clears the latch on every Play/Pause release. AWTK drops the release when it aborts pressed keys on a window change; a later press has a new time, so a stale latch cannot eat it.

The target is the showlist index plus hashes of the record strings (`+8` name, `+0xc` path, `+0x10`, `+0x14` album, `+0x18` artist) and of the browsing state (`g_folder_path`, `g_class_type`, `g_local_classinfo_save`, `g_artist_type`); the dialog open and the action both re-check it. A type-8 record is one song. In `g_class_type` `0xf003` a row is an album: `getMusicByAlbum(id == -1 ? NULL : album)`, as `load_album_detaillist` → `load_localclass_list(0xff10)` does. In `0xff01` (an artist's albums) it is `getMusicByAlbumAndAlbumSonger` when `g_artist_type == 1`, otherwise `getMusicByAlbumAndSonger`, with `(album, classinfo[0xa] ? NULL : artist, id == -2)` (`0xff11`). A composer's (`0xff02`) or genre's (`0xff03`) albums use `getMusicByAlbumAndComposer`/`getMusicByAlbumAndGenre` with `+0x20` composer or `+0x1c` genre and `classinfo[0xb]`/`[0xc]`. An artist row (`0xf004`) is `getMusicBySonger(+0x18)`, or `getMusicByAlbumArtist` when `g_artist_type == 1`; a composer (`0xf005`) `getMusicByComposer(+0x20)`, a genre (`0xf006`) `getMusicByGenre(+0x1c)`; an id of -1 (Unknown) passes NULL. These are the expansions `batch_add_file` (`0x4f4be8`) runs for each class. A folder row (type 4) calls `toolsLoadDirectory(g_folder_path + "/" + name)` like `folder_enter`, then the same for each type-4 subfolder in that listing's order (`folder_tracks`: names starting with a dot skipped, 8 levels deep at most). ponytail: one directory load per folder on the UI thread, so a large tree blocks for a while; a database path-prefix query would be quicker but misses unscanned files. Queries fill `tools_pdeq_directory`; its type-8 records join in order and the staging deque is restored.

Coverflow album holds resolve the live `SLIDE_INDEX` against the current sorted album deque, without opening its tracks. Sort, Refresh, preparation, empty screens and pending screen changes are excluded. Both the deferred open and action revalidate the selected record. Play Next, Add to Queue and Shuffle use `coverflow_album_tracks`, shared with opening an album, so disc/track/path/CUE ordering and staging restoration are identical. Go to Artist is available when the album has an artist; playlist actions remain omitted.

The menu is the stock `dialog/sortselect_dialog`: its init is synchronous and clears `to_modal`, so `navigator_to` returns with it on top, and it is already a navigation context. The payload replaces its five sort rows with the row's own, titles it with the item (a song by its title tag, the player's parsed title while it plays an untagged folder record, else its file name less the extension), and swaps its key-up (`0x49f3e0`) and back arrow (`0x49f3c0`) handlers, both of which set `g_sort_changeflag`, which makes Local Songs reload and scroll to the top on return. The open is deferred out of the long-press dispatch and the action out of the row click; successful queue, favourite and rating actions are silent; failures use a stock `dialog/msginfo_dialog` toast (`{1, 2000, text}`).

The rows are stock actions. Every row has Play Next and Add to Queue. A song (and a Coverflow track) adds or removes a favourite, labelled from `checkFavExist`, as Now Playing's heart (`manage_myfav`): Add selects the row alone in the batch record (`batch_init_selectrecord(rows)`, `batch_set_selectitem(index)`, as `player_addplayList` does) and runs batch-select's Add to My Fav, `batch_add_file(class, 0xf00a, list, *p_vector_select_record, 0)`, which reads a folder file's tags first; Coverflow passes its own deque as class `0xf001`. Remove is `deleteMusicFromFav`, and on My Fav (`0xf00a`) it sets `g_delete_flag` so the list reloads, as the heart does. Albums, artists, composers, genres and folders get Shuffle instead: their songs, the play mode saved as shuffle, from a random song, as Shuffle Songs. Everything but Coverflow gets Add to Playlist: the row selected the same way, then `localmusic/playlist_page` with context `1 << 16 | class`, the page's add mode, whose pick runs `batch_add_file(class, 0xf00b, showlist, ...)` for the playlist and shows its own toast. A song gets Go to Album and a song or a row of all albums Go to Artist, when the record has one: Now Playing More's Album info (`0x5283b0`: `classinfo` `+0` = `0xff10`, `+9` = 0, `+0xd` album, `+0x10d` artist, then `playerjumpinfo_page`) and Artist info (`localmusic/artistinfo_page` with `{class, record}`). Both pages reload from `g_local_classinfo_save`, and so do the pages underneath when they come back, so the payload copies it before and writes it back on the opened page's `EVT_WINDOW_CLOSE`. Go to Album is left out on an album's own list (`0xff1x`) and either is left out while its page is already open (`navigator_window_is_exist`): both keep single-instance state, which stock's More closes first. With a `coverflow_page` open (the song was played from Coverflow) or Display → Coverflow → Artist Albums set to Coverflow, Go to Album opens the album's tracks in Coverflow instead (`coverflow_goto_album`, see [Artist Albums in Coverflow](#artist-albums-in-coverflow)), falling back to `playerjumpinfo_page` when the album has no tracks; it is left out while that Coverflow already shows this album (`coverflow_shows_album`). Now Playing keeps its stock heart and More page; Photos, Books and Videos have no menu.

A song whose path is on the card (`/mnt/mmc/`) gets **Go to Folder** after Go to Artist, also on Now Playing, when `song_folder` accepts its parent: it fits `g_folder_path` (1024 bytes with its terminator), has no empty, `.` or `..` name (stock's `folder_enter` never builds one) and is still a directory (`__xstat`, checked again at the pick: "Folder not found"). The pick stores the path and its layer (the card 2, each folder below one more, as `folder_enter` counts) and opens `folder_page`, whose synchronous init, `ringnav_folder`, opens it through the [Folder Root](#folder-root) block, the card present; the pending folder is cleared either way. Stock `folder_back` then climbs to the card and out, as from Folder Root. The path is copied, never formatted. The page opens at the folder's top, not on the song. ponytail: the row is left out while a `folder_page` is open (stock keeps one), so Now Playing after a folder play has none; Return reaches that page, which shows the previous folder once the play has moved on.

The queue is `*mcl_pdeqplaylist` (`0xa269a4`) with static state at `0xa3bdb0` (shuffle pool, a deque of unplayed indices), `0xa3be90` (shuffle previous index), `0xa3be94`/`0xa3be98` (gapless preloaded index/flag), `0xa3bea0` (queue class), `0xa3bea4` (position), `0xa3bea8` (mode) and `0xa3beb4` (player socket). Only a local queue (class 1 or `0xfxxx`) grows. An empty queue is loaded by `mclLoadPlayList(tracks, 0, class)`, which does not start playback. Otherwise Add appends with `_deque_push_back` and Play Next rebuilds the deque with the tracks at position+1 (`deque_clear` + `deque_assign`, as stock loads it). `playback_queue_insert` (`patch/playback.c`) owns every queue index fix-up for both: the pool entries, the last pick and the previous index (`back_to`) at or after the insert shift, the new indices join the pool, and then advanced play's seen, history and pending list follow (`playback_insert`, a no-op when it is off). Gapless preloads only pos+1 (modes 0 and 3), so a set preload flag is closed exactly when the tracks land there, with the `mclSetPlayMode` sequence (`{mcl-closegapless\null}` to the socket, flag -1); advanced play then closes any preload as its own (flag 0, index -1). In shuffle the checked call at `0x5addf0` in `mclNextSong` (`bal 0x5a8120`, delay slot `a0 = 1`) becomes `jal ringnav_shuffle`: it runs the stock pick, then makes the first Play Next track the position once, if the queue still holds it there. Repeat-one repeats the current track by design.

Now Playing holds capture the queue pointer, length, index, song identity (including CUE offsets) and an ordered queue hash. Deferred opening and actions revalidate this target. View Queue opens stock's `playerqueue_page` after closing the hold dialog, so its rows track the live playing queue. That is list order, not play order, so while advanced shuffle is on (`playback_option(0)`) View Queue opens the payload's Up Next list instead (`upnext_page` in `patch/upnext.c`, titled View Queue, `playback_upcoming`): the successor walk of the shuffle's order, as a snapshot. A View Queue row plays through stock's `player_start_queue` (`0x5160dc`, which `mclSetPlayPos`es and starts the player); a row of the shuffle list calls it with its snapshot index and closes the page. A Play/Pause hold on one of its rows (`ringnav_keylong`'s `un_hold` → `un_move`) plays that occurrence next (`playback_move`), ahead of any pending Play Next. Only the pending list changes: the occurrence goes to its front, out of its old place there, so the queue, its indices, the order and every album or folder group stay as they were. Play Next's list is queue indices in play order, at most 64 (`PEND_LIMIT`); a hold or Play Next past that drops the list's last entry. Playing one takes its order entry to the cursor, as a Shuffle Songs pick does (`commit`'s `play_now`). The gapless preload is closed and the rows are retaken with the pick at row 0, the selection there. A successful hold is silent, including a row already next; one no longer up next (played since) or a queue whose `queue_hash` changed since the rows were taken retakes the rows from the live queue and toasts "Queue changed"; a page gone or a row out of range does nothing. ponytail: a row from the next pass (Repeat All) still plays in that pass too. Its hook (`ringnav_start_queue`, `patch/playback.c`) commits the pick in advanced play first, so Previous returns to it. Under Shuffle Songs it goes through Play Next's path, moving it right after the cursor, so the order carries on after it: the pick does not play again later in the pass, and the tracks it jumped stay up next. An ordered queue (or Shuffle Albums, Folders, a category) moves the cursor to it, so the tracks after it follow. A pick inside a pending Play Next takes only its entry off the list; the rest stay pending. ponytail: the shuffle list's rows are indices from when it opened; a queue replaced meanwhile (a folder play's next folder) makes them name other tracks. Stock Shuffle draws each next track at random, so it has no order to list: Shuffle Songs and a collection's Shuffle save stock's Shuffle mode, then hand the loaded queue to advanced Shuffle Songs with Repeat All (`playback_shuffle`). View Queue does the same on a stock Shuffle chosen elsewhere (Now Playing's mode button; `playback_listable`, only while advanced play is off), then opens Up Next, a Play Next stock's Shuffle was holding (`qm_forced`) moved to the front of the pending list; ponytail: the order is fresh, so songs stock's shuffle already played can come round again. On Now Playing's hold menu a last row, More…, sends Now Playing's own `img_more` a click (stock's handler opens `playermore_page`), only while that page is open (`np_more`). Playlist add mode 2 uses the playing queue; its existing row click handlers are guarded again before the stock callback, with the captured row selected. Browsing state is independent.

## Home carousel

iPod has no carousel: Home is an ordinary list ([ipod.md](ipod.md#home)) that bumps and wraps like a ring list. The Legacy build's carousel uses `HOME_SLIDE_MS` (200 ms) for isolated ticks and `HOME_FAST_SLIDE_MS` (120 ms) when consecutive same-direction ticks arrive at most `HOME_FAST_WINDOW_MS` (200 ms) apart. There is no extra home input gate; stock filtering and audible ticks stay stock-controlled. Each accepted tick retargets one animator from its live offset, extending the intended destination by one icon. Reversal discards that destination and heads toward the adjacent card in the new direction at normal speed. Stock completion handles wrapping and card selection; centre confirmation follows the intended final icon. Touch, native clicks, centre presses, leaving home and unavailable navigation reset speed tracking; wheel input cancels pending centre confirmation. Allocation failure settles on the requested icon without retaining an animator.

## Coverflow

Coverflow (`patch/coverflow.c`) follows Rockbox PictureFlow: albums and tracks come from the stock library queries, never from tags, and the payload owns only a thumbnail cache. Audit (V1.32, 2026-09-28); every entry point is exported, and the builder pins the executable through the stock ZIP's SHA-256:

| Stock entry                                       | Address                 | Size      | Prologue                                                |
| ------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------- | --------- | ------------------------------------------------------- |
| `home_page_init` (hook)                           | `0x523c84`              | 1028      | `50001c3c3c309c2721e09903`                              |
| `getAllAlbum`                                     | `0x4fceb0`              | 800       | `53001c3c109e9c2721e09903`                              |
| `getMusicByAlbum`                                 | `0x4ff7d8`              | 480       | `52001c3ce8749c2721e09903`                              |
| `toolsThumbSpecCover`                             | `0x5c4550`              | 264       | `46001c3c70279c2721e09903`                              |
| `toolsGetAlbumCover`                              | `0x5c4444`              | 268       | `46001c3c7c289c2721e09903`                              |
| `window_create`                                   | `0x66a274`              | 72        | `3c001c3c4cca9c2721e09903`                              |
| `widget_factory` / `widget_factory_create_widget` | `0x666c68` / `0x666f04` | 24 / 188  | `3c001c3c58009c2721e09903` / `3c001c3cbcfd9c2721e09903` |
| `image_create` / `image_set_draw_type`            | `0x614cfc` / `0x61527c` | 112 / 116 | `41001c3cc41f9c2721e09903` / `41001c3c441a9c2721e09903` |

| `bitmap_lock_buffer_for_read` / `_for_write`      | `0x630000` / `0x630078` | 120 / 120 | `3f001c3cc06c9c2721e09903` / `3f001c3c486c9c2721e09903` |
| `bitmap_unlock_buffer` / `bitmap_get_line_length` | `0x6300f0` / `0x62fabc` | 60 / 88   | `3f001c3cd06b9c2721e09903` / `3f001c3c04729c2721e09903` |
| `canvas_draw_image`                               | `0x635254`              | 260       | `3f001c3c6c1a9c2721e09903`                              |
| `slide_menu_set_spacer` / `widget_to_local`       | `0x5f53e0` / `0x65a9b4` | 116 / 220 | `43001c3ce0189c2721e09903` / `3d001c3c0cc39c2721e09903` |

Data: `tools_pdeq_directory` (`0xa269bc`, 4), `parse_cover_mutex` (`0xa37cb8`, 24) and `g_playcover_mutex` (`0xa39c18`, 24) are size-checked `CONTEXT_DATA`; the scan thread handle `0xa271d8` and done flag `0xa271d4` have no symbol (`offsets.inc`).

**Albums.** `load_localclass_list(0xf003)` (`0x5088cc`) is `getAllAlbum()` followed by `deque_assign(*p_deque_showlist, *tools_pdeq_directory)`, so the payload calls `getAllAlbum` itself and copies the staging deque instead, restoring it afterwards: no private state, and a Local Music page underneath keeps its showlist. `getAllAlbum` runs `select id,album,songer,fileurl from songtable group by album COLLATE NOCASE` on `/mnt/data/database.db` through `toolsQueryDbTable` (`0x5c8044`: the lazily opened shared handle at `0xa3bf44`, `deque_clear`, `sqlite3_exec`, then `algo_sort_if` with the stock comparator). Its row callback (`0x4fc324`) skips empty albums and fills `+0` id, `+0x10/+0x14` album, `+0x18/+0x20` artist and `+0xc` the group's `fileurl`, which is the first-track path the art build uses. When any album exists it then appends an "Unknown Album" row (id -1, no path). It returns the query count: -1 without a database, 0 when empty. That grouping is by name alone (stock's Albums list shares it), so Coverflow then runs `ALBUM_SQL`, the same columns plus `albumsonger`, grouped by album, then album artist, or for songs without one the folder (`rtrim(fileurl, replace(fileurl, '/', ''))`), through `split_row`: `album_row` on the first four columns, then the album artist into `+0x24` (`REC_ALBUM_ARTIST`), which the `stSongInfo` copy (`0x5b3b1c`) duplicates. Each stock row is replaced in place by its name's rows (`split`), one per `album_cmp` album, so same-named albums by different artists are separate covers with separate art keys; `album_cmp` tells albums apart the same way everywhere else (tracks, Sort ranks, play counts, playback's album groups). Its folder fallback takes a trailing disc folder (`CD1`, `Disc 2`, `Disk_03`: cd/disc/disk, an optional space, `_` or `-`, digits) as its parent, so an untagged multi-disc album is one; the SQL keeps disc folders apart and `split` merges them. Shuffle Folders (`folder_cmp`) keeps each folder apart. The stock Albums list gets the same split (`ringnav_localclass`), and a row's album page (`load_album_detaillist`, hooked to keep the row) and resume (`memeory_startplayer`'s 0xff10 rebuild) keep only that album's songs from `getMusicByAlbum`. Its art cache, `/mnt/mmc/.sldp/<md5 of the name>0.jpg`, adds the album artist, else the album folder, to a name that adjacent rows share (`art_key`): the row renderer (`0x4a388c`), the page's `albumcoverinfo_init` cover tasks the art thread hashes, and the album page's big cover (`0x4a72a4`). An artist's albums tab (`load_localartist_list(1)`, hooked) groups by name too, and has one row of a name another artist shares, so adjacency can't tell: `ringnav_artist_list` splits a copy of the tab as Albums is and gives each row its album's `art_key`, the same-named album in its folder, else with the row's artist as album artist. Its renderer (`0x4ab804`, the row in `$s2`) and four `albumcoverinfo_init` calls (Album tab entry `0x4ac840`, page return `0x4ae860`/`0x4ae968`, init `0x4aebc0`) use those keys, and the album page's big cover follows its cover task as before. Local art completion (`0x4edda0`) passes the worker's copied key (`sp + 0x1688`) to `ringnav_cover_done`; the folder-cover path, which branched to the call itself (`0x4eeddc`), now enters one word earlier so it passes the key too: under `aclist_mutex`, it sets the small/big ready flag only if that row still has the key. A job finishing after navigation rebuilt the list therefore cannot mark a different album ready and make later requests skip its missing cover. When `rename_covers` changes a row's key, it clears both ready flags under the same lock, including a completion arriving between stock initialization and renaming. The grids' scroll start (`0x14e`: `0x4aab40` an artist's albums, `0x4a22b0` Library Albums) set the worker's shared `albumcover_taskstate` to 2, which it sleeps through, and their scroll end resumed it (`local_albumcover_task`) only when the first row in view changed; a wheel tick within the same rows, or the return to a scrolled grid (its `scroll_view_set_offset` sends `0x14e` alone), left every grid without art until reboot. Library Songs (`music_table_client`, scroll start `0x4a7b90`) did the same, so returning to a scrolled Songs list showed every row's placeholder until the wheel moved. All three stores are `nop` (`COVER_PAUSE`): the worker keeps going, and each scroll end replaces its queue as before. An artist's albums scroll end (`0x4ad878`) took its first row as `yoffset / 256 * 3`, though its rows are 160 px as Library Albums divides; it divides by 160 (`ARTIST_COVER_ROW`), so the nine covers it requests, and the renderer (`0x4ab798`) draws, are the ones in view. Coverflow keeps this order and shows the Unknown row with the placeholder, only when its card's query, `getMusicByAlbum(NULL)`, finds a song (`drop_unknown`, as [Unknown rows](#unknown-rows)): before V8.6 it showed even with every song tagged.

The copy outlives the page, because the query is the slow part of an open: `toolsQueryDbTable` sorts with a stock comparator (`0x5ba658`) that runs `utf8_to_pinyin` on both names, four `snprintf`s and several `memset`s per comparison. Only `scanAllMusicFile`, `scanSpecFolder` and `deleteMusicFromMusicDb` write `songtable` (every other `toolsInsert/Delete/UpdateDbTable` caller writes the favourites, playlist and play-record tables); their hooks step a generation before and after stock runs, and an open queries again only when the generation moved since the list was taken, or on Refresh. A list taken while a writer runs is never kept, and an empty or refused result is not kept at all.

**Folder tag refresh.** Scan specified folders keeps stock's recursive scan and cancellation, but its `toolsCheckItemExist` call (`0x5c981c`) goes through `ringnav_scan_refresh`: existing `songtable` rows re-read tags and update in place, matching filename, full path and CUE start/end. IDs, creation time and song count stay intact; a full library still refreshes existing files, while new files wait for space. CUE entries use the freshly parsed CUE metadata; ordinary files use `toolsGetMusicInfo`, including the FLAC album-artist fallback, after checking TagLib can read them. Tag validation and refresh disable TagLib audio-property reads, so rescanning existing tags does not probe the audio duration. The title’s stock pinyin initial is refreshed too. SQL text is bounded and quotes apostrophes; an unreadable tag leaves the existing row intact. The existing scan writer hooks invalidate cached album groups. New songs and Update Local Music keep stock's insert path.

**Empty or scanning.** Only the modal Update Local Music dialog (`dialog_updatemusic_dialog_init`) starts the scan: `tk_thread` `wait_scan` → `scanAllMusicFile` (`toolsInitDatabase`, `toolsLoadAllFile`, commit), storing the handle at `0xa271d8` and clearing `0xa271d4`, which `wait_scan` sets when it ends. Its Back button stops the scan and quits the dialog before the thread ends. Coverflow shows "Update Local Music first" while a scan runs (handle set, flag clear) or when `getAllAlbum` returns ≤ 0, as PictureFlow's `check_database()` does. Before each folder entry `toolsLoadAllFile` (`0x5c9660`) stops the scan, as Back does, once the song count (`toolsGetMusicNum`) reaches 20,000 (`slti` at `0x5c9780`). 65,000 does not fit `slti`'s signed immediate, so its count load becomes `jal ringnav_scan_room` with the count loaded into `a0` in the delay slot (`SCAN_LIMIT`): a leaf, as the scanner keeps using `gp` after it, true below 65,000. The queue's `QUEUE_LIMIT` (65,536) already holds that many.

Coverflow's main, artist and rated views keep only albums with a track outside `/mnt/mmc/Audiobooks/` and without an `.m4b` extension, case aside (`music_albums`, `MUSIC_ALBUM_SQL`). Filtering precedes the SQL grouping so a mixed album uses a music track for its artwork. The stock library lists keep their audiobook entries.

**Tracks.** A cover opens `getMusicByAlbum(id == -1 ? NULL : album)`, which is what a stock Albums row opens (`load_localclass_list(0xff10)` with `g_local_classinfo_save+0xd`, `+9` flagging Unknown); it returns stock-ordered songs (`algo_sort_if`). The Albums list has no -2 row; that one only occurs in an artist's albums (queue menu). The result is copied out of the staging deque the same way, keeping only the cover's own album (`album_cmp`: the query matches the name alone). Coverflow then re-orders the copy into album order, disc then track then path then CUE start, so an untagged album keeps its file-name order. Its row callback (`0x4fb8b8`, `select *` from `songtable`) fills `+0x08` the file name, `+0x10` the tag's title, `+0x3c` `track` and `+0x40` `cd_num` (`REC_NAME`, `REC_TITLE`, `REC_TRACK`, `REC_DISC`). Each row shows the title tag when present, otherwise the file name less its file's extension; a CUE track's name, which does not end in it, stays whole. Folder play gets the re-ordered deque, so playback follows the list.

**Play class.** `0xff10` would resume the wrong album: memory-play re-runs `getMusicByAlbum` from `g_memory_info+0x611` (`memeory_startplayer` `0x5164e0`), which only `player_load_songlist` (`0x513634`) fills, from the live `g_local_classinfo_save`. Coverflow therefore uses folder play, `navigator_to_with_context("playing_page", {tracks, index, 1, 2})` as `startPlayFolderSong` does. `mclLoadPlayList` copies the deque synchronously, and memory-play reloads the last track's folder.

**Art.** Every stock caller holds a lock around the art calls, and none nests the two: `parse_albumcovertask_thd` (`toolsGetAlbumCover` `0x4edf18`/`0x4ef008`/`0x4ef0d0`, `toolsThumbSpecCover` `0x4ee960`/`0x4ef2d0`) and the UI-thread online thumbnails (`0x525244`/`0x52525c`) hold `parse_cover_mutex`; `player_parsecover_thd` holds `g_playcover_mutex` around `toolsGetAlbumCover` (`0x512fe0`) and `parse_cover_mutex` alone around its Tidal/Baidu downloads. `toolsGetAlbumCover` writes and removes the shared `/tmp/.tmp_picture`, so both stock threads can already race on it; Coverflow takes both locks, parse then play. `taglib_album_picture` (`libtag_c` `0x12508`) dispatches by extension to the C++ `get*_file_albumpic` helpers, none of which touches libtag*c's unlocked string list (`taglib_tag_free_strings`), which the UI thread's `toolsGetMusicInfo` uses. That list is what failed the first design; embedded art is only as concurrent as stock's own player thread already is. `toolsThumbSpecCover(src, dst, w, h)` decodes JPEG (≤ 6 MB, with libjpeg's DCT scaling for large ones) or PNG (≤ 1 MB), by their magic bytes (`toolsGetPictureInfo`), and encodes, or `cp`s a source already small enough, straight to `dst` with no extension check, so `<key>.jpg.tmp` is safe. Both return 1 on success. Stock's own cover cache (`/mnt/mmc/.sldp/<md5>{0,1}.jpg`) and folder-image finder (`toolsGetExternCover`, `0x5c49e8`) are not used: the first fills lazily as the album grid scrolls, and the spec fixes cover.jpg → folder.jpg → embedded. Coverflow tries embedded art, then `cover`/`folder` `.jpg`, `.png`, `.webp`, then ffmpeg on the embedded art (last: a process for every album without art). Whatever stock refuses (WebP, a PNG over 1 MB, a JPEG over 6 MB) goes to stock `/usr/bin/ffmpeg` (`ff_thumb`: fork and exec, its first picture or embedded `attached_pic` fitted into the box, written as a baseline 4:2:0 JPEG). `ff_thumb` has ffmpeg write `<dst>.ff` and renames it onto `dst` only on exit 0 with a non-empty file; otherwise it removes `<dst>.ff` and leaves `dst` alone, as stock's grid cache takes any file at its path for art. ffmpeg creates its output before decoding, so a picture it can't decode leaves an empty file even on exit 0, and a killed run a partial one. `-xerror` ends the run at a picture's first decode error instead of demuxing the rest of the audio file, and `waitpid` is retried on `EINTR` so the child is always reaped. That covers the embedded picture in `build_art` and every `thumb` caller with a readable source (photos, Spotify). It writes no shared file and takes no lock. Stock's decoder also refuses palette and greyscale PNGs (its PNG reader, `0x5b66f8`, takes only colour types 2 and 6). Now Playing gets the same fallback: `player_parsecover_thd` tries `toolsGetAlbumCover` (embedded) and then `toolsGetExternCover` (folder images) under `g_playcover_mutex`, and that second call (`PLAYER_COVER`, `0x51301c`) becomes `jal coverflow_player_cover`. That runs stock's call first, then `ff_thumb` on the same `cover`/`folder` names and then on the embedded picture. It never calls `thumb`, whose `parse_cover_mutex` would nest under the player's lock in the opposite order from `build_art`'s. Folder images cost a process only when one exists. The embedded probe costs one for every track without art, so it is skipped when `g_play_id3_info` holds the track's path (`player_get_id3info` parses it before the cover thread runs) and Coverflow's cache file for those parsed tags, keyed as Home's art is, is an empty marker. ponytail: the marker records one track's result, so another disc's tracks, or tracks of a same-named album, whose only art is embedded WebP or a large PNG show none on Now Playing; tracks on albums Coverflow hasn't built, or by another artist than the album's key (compilations), still pay a probe per play. Albums already marked empty need **Refresh Coverflow** (Display → Coverflow). ponytail: one process per refused cover; a resident decoder if first builds drag. Library Albums grids and Folder view get the Ogg case: `parse_albumcovertask_thd`'s three `toolsGetAlbumCover` calls (`GRID_COVER`, `0x4edf1c`/`0x4ef018`/`0x4ef0d4`: an album's cover, a folder's first track with art, a track row) become `jal coverflow_album_cover` (the first) and `jal coverflow_grid_cover`, stock's call and then, for an `.opus` or `.ogg` track only (stock's libtag reads no Ogg picture), `ff_thumb`. All three run on stock's one cover thread (`platform_init` starts it) under `parse_cover_mutex`, which the UI thread also takes for Library's online thumbnails (`localmusic_page_init`, `0x525244`/`0x52525c`) and Spotify's art (`thumb`), so the lock is dropped for the ffmpeg run and taken again before returning. That is safe: each call's source and destination are the thread's stack paths (`.sldp/<md5>{0,1}.jpg`, `.downpic/fd*<md5>.jpg`), which no other lock holder writes (the UI thread's are `.downpic/s\_<md5>.jpg`), and what stock does after it (`toolsGetExternCover`on the album call) runs with the lock held again. The album call's stock fallback is`toolsGetExternCover`, which scans the folder for any `.jpg`, `.png`or`.jpeg`, `folder`/`cover`names first;`coverflow_album_cover` calls it before ffmpeg, so an Ogg album whose art is a folder image costs no process, and its Ogg art is ordered as Coverflow's. The folder-row call already runs it first, and the track-row call has no folder fallback, so neither does. Stock caches only a hit (`.sldp`, `.downpic`), so a track ffmpeg found no picture in is remembered in memory (an FNV hash of its path; not when the track itself is gone, the fork was refused or ffmpeg was killed) and its rows ask no further process. ponytail: 1024 misses remembered until reboot, the oldest overwritten; a picture added to such a track since shows after a reboot.

**Build.** On open, the UI thread copies each uncached album's track path (`strdup`) and FNV key (artist, album) into a job array. One pthread then renders 160×160 art to `/mnt/mmc/.coverflow/<key>.jpg.tmp` and renames it into place. Missing art leaves an empty `<key>.jpg` marker; a track that is gone leaves nothing, so the next open retries it. There is no build below `MIN_FREE_MB` (16) free on the card (`statfs`, o32 `f_bsize` word 1, `f_bavail` word 7). Progress and the cancel flag are volatile `.scratch` ints; a 250 ms timer polls until every job is done. Cancel and window destroy join the thread, a wait bounded by one album. **System settings → Display → Coverflow → Refresh Coverflow** (`coverflow_refresh_art`) unlinks the cache directory and steps `library_gen`, so the next open queries the albums again and rebuilds their art.

**Page.** The Home card clones `btn_localmusic` at index 2 in Legacy, reusing its images, and is the third row of iPod's Home list; `home_page_init`'s `widget_foreach` visitor (`0x5239b4`) binds `img_*` clicks and translates `label_*` by name and ignores unknown names. The Local Music card's label is renamed `label_library`, so it skips it too and its literal "Library" shows in every language, in both variants. The hook runs the stock init through `stock_home_trampoline` (`0x523c90`), then binds `widget_lookup(win, "img_coverflow", 1)`, whatever stock returned. In iPod it also sets up Home's art ([ipod.md](ipod.md#home)). The card opens a runtime `window_create(NULL, 0, 0, 0, 0)` named `coverflow_page`; `window_base_create` opens it through `window_manager_open_window`, and the window-manager key handlers that `home_page_init` registers route it like any page (`CTX_DYNAMIC`, exempt from the rootfs window-name check). Covers are a factory `slide_menu` of `image_create` children, each item `h` square (`0x5f3040`); with depth (below) the children stay empty and the payload draws the covers. In the flat fallback they are stock images (`image_set_draw_type` 4, as stock cover rows). `slide_menu` dispatches `EVT_VALUE_CHANGED` (`0xe`, after `0xd` will-change, `0x5f320c`) with the new index already at `+0x78`, which updates the captions (the album over its artist; with Artists set to Album Artist, `artist_type` 1, the album artist tag when it has one, `cover_artist`, so a compilation reads "Various Artists" or its compiler, and the Artist sort and its letter jump use the same name, sorting again when Artists changes); in the flat fallback only covers within ±3 then load their file, the stock way: `widget_load_image` → `image_base_set_image` → `widget_unload_image`. A failed load (the empty marker) shows `default_album_big` in Legacy or `default_album_flow` in iPod. Clicks only schedule screen changes on a 0 ms timer, so no screen change destroys the widget whose click is running. Tracks hide the covers rather than destroy them, so Return restores the album.

Coverflow’s Album and Artist sorts, once a spin passes `CF_FIRST_MS` (700 ms, longer than the lists' `LIST_FIRST_MS`), jump to the first album of the next or previous populated initial group. The active sort key skips leading articles; numeric, punctuation, separator and missing-name initials share `#`, while other Unicode initials are retained. The same carousel animator is retargeted; utility cards and wrap edges remain individual stops. The existing letter style expires after 400 ms. Recently Added, Most Played and Random keep single-album stepping.

## Tag text

Stock reads a song's tags with its own `libtag_c` (`toolsGetMusicInfo`, `0x5c5f40`, for the library scan and the player): for each of six fields (title, artist, album, genre, composer, album artist) it asks for the field twice, `taglib_tag_<field>(tag, 0)` (TagLib's `String::to8Bit(false)`: one byte per character, Latin-1) and `(tag, 1)` (UTF-8), and whether TagLib found every character below U+0100 (`taglib_tag_islatin1`, `String::isLatin1`). `toolsFixId3ErrorCode` (`0x5c5cf8`) then took a field for GBK whenever it was "Latin-1" that way: it converted the Latin-1 bytes from GBK to UTF-8 (`toolsIconvConvert(s, "utf-8", "gbk")`, with an `iconv_open("gbk", "utf-8")` round trip as its check) and kept the result, so any Western title with an accented letter came out Chinese: "Lo que la música me enseñó" pairs `ú` and `s` (0xFA 0x73), `ñ` and `ó` (0xF1 0xF3) into GBK characters. That covers FLAC and Vorbis comments (always UTF-8) and UTF-8 ID3v2 frames as much as Latin-1 ID3 frames: the test is on the decoded text, not the tag's encoding. `TAG_TEXT` in `tools/build.py` turns that round trip's `strcmp` (`0x5c5e7c`) into `jal ringnav_gbk_tag`, which also asks that the Latin-1 bytes read as GBK (`gbk_text`): not UTF-8, and every byte from 0x80 one of a pair, lead 0x81–0xFE then trail 0x80–0xFE, with no pair between two ASCII letters. Western text puts an accented letter next to an ASCII one ("mú-s" is 0xFA 0x73) or a pair inside a word ("Can-çã-o", "Beyonc-é’-s"), so it fails and keeps TagLib's UTF-8 copy; a GBK-tagged MP3 (Chinese rips' Latin-1 frames holding GBK bytes) is all pairs and still converts. Limits: a GBK character with an ASCII-range trail byte (0x40–0x7E, the rarer GBK/3 and GBK/4 ones) or a single one inside a Latin word ("a周b"), makes its field read as Latin-1, and Western text whose accented letters only come in pairs at a word's edge ("ñó" alone) reads as GBK. Stock's conversion writes back over a CUE field unbounded, and GBK grows by half as UTF-8, so a CUE field over 340 bytes is read as Windows-1252 instead. The library keeps what the scan stored: **Library → Update Local Music** reads the tags again.

CUE sheets went through the same `toolsIconvConvert(field, "utf-8", "gbk")` in their parser (the unnamed function after `toolsFreeStSongInfo`, seven fields, `CUE_TEXT`): each call is `jal ringnav_cue_text`, which keeps UTF-8 (lead and continuation bytes, nothing overlong, no surrogate), passes GBK (`gbk_text`, as for tags) to stock's conversion, and reads anything else as Windows-1252, the code page Western rippers write, re-encoded in place and cut at a whole character to 511 bytes (the fields' buffers hold at least 512, and their lines 500).

`test/patch.py` runs the patched `toolsFixId3ErrorCode` on accented Latin-1 fields and on a Chinese one, and `ringnav_cue_text` on UTF-8, Windows-1252, overlong and surrogate input.

## English

Q2 Pod runs in English with nothing Chinese left in stock's interface:

- **Strings:** `en_US.bin`'s About row `system_prove` reads Certification (stock: 技术证明), with the other `LABELS`.
- **UI assets:** stock's `.bin` layouts carry Chinese `text` in about 40 of them, designers' placeholders that stock's code mostly replaces, but some shows in every language: Search's tabs (单曲, 歌手, 专辑, 风格), Now Playing's info labels, Folder's and Streaming's titles, Quick Settings' rows, the factory test pages. The build finds every asset with Chinese text (`CJK`) and `english_ui` gives each such `text` in English (`ENGLISH`, with per-asset choices in `ENGLISH_IN`), checking the round trip and that nothing Chinese is left. Layouts the build already rewrote (`patch_asset`) are given English after that.
- **Keyboard:** the T9 keyboard's EN key opened its Chinese pinyin page (`page:chinese`); it is a case key now (ABC on the lowercase page, abc on the capitals), so that page is never reached, and its 《》 key types «».
- **Streaming:** stock's Baidu Netdisk row (a Chinese cloud drive, signed in to from Baidu's app) is removed (`ringnav_stream` destroys the row whose image is `list_baidu`).
- **Tags:** [Tag text](#tag-text).

The language picker still lists stock's languages; Q2 Pod's own pages are English in all of them.

## Sorting

Every name-sorted library list (artists, album artists, composers, genres, albums and songs: the `group by … COLLATE NOCASE` queries and `sort_first_alphabet`) is sorted in C, not SQL: `toolsQueryDbTable(…, 1)` and `toolsQueryDbTableAndSort` mode 0 pass `algo_sort_if` one of two comparators, `0x5ba658` or, with the flag at `0xa3bf4d` clear, the pinyin one `0x5b9d40`. The `ORDER BY` queries sort by time, sample rate or id, and folders sort by file name in `toolsLoadDirectory`'s own comparators. Both name comparators `snprintf` the row's `+0x10` name into a local buffer and `bal toolsTrimLeft` on it, falling back to the file name when it is empty. Those four calls (`SORT_TRIMS`) become `jal ringnav_sort_key`, which trims and then drops a leading "The ", "A " or "An " (any case) when a name follows, as Apple does; the record is untouched. "Them", "Anthrax" and a bare "The" keep their place, and equal keys ("The Cure", "Cure") stay as stock's `algo_sort_if` leaves them. Coverflow follows this order. Stock has no A–Z jump index; on a `CTX_LOCAL` page iPod's fast-scroll letter skips the same article.

## Unknown rows

`load_localclass_list` (`0x5088cc`) runs the class's query into `tools_pdeq_directory`, `deque_assign`s it to `*p_deque_showlist`, clears the staging deque and returns `deque_size(*p_deque_showlist)`, which its callers take as the row count. For `0xf003`–`0xf006` the query is `getAllAlbum`, `getAllArtist` (`GetAllAlbumArtist` when `g_artist_type == 1`), `getAllComposer` or `getAllGenre`, and each appends an Unknown row (id -1) whenever the list has any rows (`getAllArtist` `0x4fd4c4`: type 4, the "Unknown Artist" string), even when every song carries the tag. `ringnav_localclass` runs stock, and when that class's last row has id -1 it runs the query the row's press would run (`class_query`, shared with the [Queue menu](#queue-menu): `getMusicBySonger(NULL)` and its kin, `where songer=""`). With no song it `deque_pop_back`s the row and returns one less. The staging deque is cleared again, as stock leaves it. Coverflow builds its own album list and drops an empty Unknown card the same way (`drop_unknown`).

## Shuffle

`localmusic_page_init` (`0x524424`) destroys `scroll_view_localmusic`'s children and builds 11 rows (`0x5247ec`), each button named by its index; the shared row click (`0x5241fc`) `atoi`s that name into a switch. Stock's rows, by index (`get_localmusic_showinfo`, `0x5012d0`), are Update Local Music, All Songs, Album, Artist, Genre, Hi-Res, My Fav, Frequent, Recent, Recently Added and Playlist. The hook runs stock through `stock_localmusic_trampoline`, appends its own rows in the same widgets and styles (`list_row`, also Display settings'; `library_into` adds stock's `list_into` chevron), then `widget_restack`s every row into one order: Shuffle; Artist, Album, All Songs, Genre, Era (see [Era](#era)), Playlist, Ratings (My Fav hidden after it, see [Ratings](#ratings)); Recently Added, Recent, Most Played, Frequent, Hi-Res, Long Songs; the media rows; Upload Scrobbles; Update Local Music last. A page that doesn't hold exactly 11 rows is left as stock built it. The added buttons have no name, so stock's click never sees them. The Shuffle row carries the `local_shuffle` icon, a build-added copy of stock's 52 px `playset_playmode`, which iPod pre-sizes to 40 px for Settings, and opens `shuffle_page`, a runtime page (`page_list` and `page_row_detail`, as Most Played's). Its Shuffle Songs row runs `getAllMusic(3)` (`0x4fce5c`, no title sort) through `staged`, scoped to music as the Library's Artists and Albums are (`MUSIC_WHERE`, see [Podcasts and Audiobooks](#podcasts-and-audiobooks)), saves shuffle with `config_playmode(2, 1)` (`0x4f3fe8`, as the play-mode setting does), clears advanced play (`playback_clear`, so stock Shuffle's keep-the-groups rule below doesn't rebuild group shuffle), and folder-plays the copy from `rand_below(n)` as Coverflow does with `toolsRandnum` (`0x5b9bec`). Shuffle Songs is one-shot: `playback_unshuffle` takes the stock mode it replaces (or the one an earlier Shuffle Songs still holds), and when the next other queue loads, `ringnav_load` applies it with `config_playmode` before stock does, ending the advanced shuffle; a mode choice in between (`ringnav_mode`) is kept instead, and a reboot mid-shuffle keeps Shuffle, as the mode is held in RAM only.
