Its Shuffle Albums and Shuffle Folders rows run `playback_groups(all, 0|1)`. Picks leave the page open for another, and a note under the rows says albums and folders take a moment to shuffle. A resume after reboot therefore reloads the last track's folder, not the library. An empty library shows "Update Local Music first". **Most Played** uses `local_frequentplay`, the icon of stock's Frequent; see [Play counts](#play-counts). **Upload Scrobbles** shows with an account set up; see [Scrobbling](#scrobbling). **Podcasts**, **Audiobooks**, **Photos**, **Books** and **Videos** show with their folders; see [Podcasts and Audiobooks](#podcasts-and-audiobooks), [Photos](#photos), [Books](#books) and [Videos](#videos).

## Coverflow depth

Coverflow draws its covers in software, after Rockbox PictureFlow's renderer: the selected cover faces the viewer at its native 160 pixels, two neighbours a side are turned 60 degrees inward, pushed back and dimmed, and each has a reflection. `patch/coverflow.c` owns the drawing; the stock `slide_menu` stays the navigation and animation controller, so the wheel (one retargeted animator, 200 ms or 120 ms fast, reversal), wrapping, centre, the saved album and track positions all work as before. Touch is the payload's own (below): the `slide_menu` stays insensitive, so stock's drag and throw never run. Tunables are `CF_*` in `patch/offsets.inc`; adjust them together.

**Layout.** With depth the `slide_menu` spans the frame, 375 × 210 (`CF_VIEW_W` × `CF_VIEW_H`) at the top of the page, so every animator step invalidates all of it. Its items stay `h` square (210), and `slide_menu_set_spacer` (a store at `+0x84` and an invalidate) sets the spacer to `CF_STRIDE - 210`, so the stride that stock scroll, completion and ringnav's wheel steps use (item width plus spacer) is `CF_STRIDE` (110 px). The children are `image` widgets with no image, so stock paints nothing under the frame. The album and artist captions sit under the frame at `CF_TEXT_Y` in both builds and follow the centre cover: when the album `coverflow_paint` drew in the middle changes, a zero-delay timer (`retitle`, never text set inside the paint) names it, once per change; `EVT_VALUE_CHANGED`, when a move commits, writes them again.

**Position.** `ringnav_paint`, the border hook both builds install, calls `coverflow_paint` for every widget after stock has painted it; only Coverflow's `slide_menu` on the covers screen draws. The visual position is the index less the live offset over the stride (stock completion commits `index - offset / stride`): centre album `c` and `frac`, 16.16, in `[-1/2, 1/2)`. Every cover's rotation, place, depth, brightness and reflection come from that one continuous value, so an incoming side cover unfolds into the centre as the outgoing one turns away.

**Projection.** All integer (the stock build is `-mfp64`, and integer arithmetic is also cheaper here). A cover `t` albums from the position gets a rotation that grows linearly to `CF_ANGLE` (60 degrees, a 17-entry sine table) at one album, a centre `x` that reaches `CF_X1` then steps `CF_XS` per album (quadratic up to one album, so the two covers turning mid-step keep clear of each other), a depth that reaches `CF_Z1` then `CF_ZS` per album, and a brightness of 256 at the centre, `CF_BRIGHT1` (75%) one out, `CF_BRIGHT2` (50%) two out and 0 three out, so covers fade in and out at the edge of reach. Left covers mirror right ones about the centre line (between columns 186 and 187). For each screen column the renderer inverts the perspective (eye `CF_EYE` px away) to the cover column under it and the cover's projected height there, then steps down the texture column, top and bottom rows weighted by coverage. At rest the selected cover is its texture pixel for pixel. Below each cover its reflection is the column mirrored, `CF_REFLECT` rows at native size scaled by perspective, from `CF_REFLECT_TOP` (18.75% in iPod, 25% in Stock) of its brightness down to nothing: no blur or glow.

**Compositing.** The frame is drawn nearest cover first: each pixel's alpha byte holds the coverage so far and every layer fills only what is left, which composites as drawing back to front would, and per column a span of rows known to be opaque is skipped outright, so hidden parts of side covers cost nothing. A last pass makes every pixel opaque over black. The hit test walks the same order, nearest first, over cover bodies only.

**Frame and textures.** One `bitmap_create_ex(375, 210, 1500, 1)` (RGBA8888; `bitmap_t` is 0x48 bytes, flags `u16 @0xc`, format `u16 @0xe`, buffer `@0x14`) is created with the covers and marked `BITMAP_FLAG_OPAQUE` (bit 0, as `bitmap_init_from_rgba` sets it for opaque images); `bitmap_lock_buffer_for_write`/`bitmap_unlock_buffer` bracket each render and `canvas_draw_image(canvas, bitmap, src, dst)` (`0x635254`, `dst` relative to the canvas origin, which is the `slide_menu`'s) draws it 1:1. It is re-rendered only when the position or a texture changes; a repaint in place only draws. Textures come from the existing JPEG cache: `widget_load_image`, `bitmap_lock_buffer_for_read` and `bitmap_unlock_buffer` read the decoded image (the stock loader, `stb_load_image` called with RGBA8888 for both formats at `0x6810cc`, never premultiplies), fitted whole and centred (a non-square cover between black bars), sampled to 160 × 160 over black into the payload's own buffer, and the load is dropped at once with `widget_unload_image`, as stock cover rows do; the image manager's copy is only read. Seven slots (`CF_RING`) hold the covers within three albums of the position; as movement crosses an album the one leaving is dropped and the one entering is decoded. A small library repeats round the ring as the `slide_menu` wraps, each album decoded once. Missing art and a card (an artist's All albums, the rated albums' Sort) use `default_album_big` in Legacy; iPod uses `default_album_flow`, a 160px copy of Home's disc resized with Lanczos at build time and decoded 1:1. Both are decoded once and left in the manager's cache as theme images. iPod clips each cover column to rounded corners at the Now Playing radius ratio (12/166), including the mirrored bottom in its reflection; the insets are computed once, with no alpha processing in the pixel loop. The frame and all eight textures (about 1.1 MB) are allocated together when the covers are built; if either fails, Coverflow keeps the flat stock covers exactly as before. Closing the page destroys the frame and frees the textures.

**iPod artwork.** Home's 290px disc is the master placeholder for album rows, Now Playing, the full-screen art, the lock screen and AirPlay, pre-sized at build time to each stock asset's dimensions, keeping the disc's transparent corners so a selected row's bar shows through them (Now Playing's to the 166px art it fills; the full-screen `default_bigcover` to its `BC_ART` square, the centred art clear of the glass). The artist-person icon keeps its stock image. Library thumbnails (`file:///mnt/mmc/.sldp/`) get rounded alpha at radius min(width, height) × 12/166 in `ringnav_image_add`, before the image manager caches them. The hook clears the cached opacity flag and keeps straight RGB, so stock can blend the corners over wallpaper or selection panels. Alpha is clamped to arc coverage, making reuse idempotent. Stock's temporary playing covers (`coverpic.jpg`, `externpic.jpg`, `album_tidal.jpg` in `/tmp`) are not: Now Playing, Spotify and the full-screen art round them after scaling, in `paint_cover`; Home's Split art is cropped past its corners.

**Touch.** The covers' view takes `EVT_POINTER_DOWN`, and the page (`open_page`) the move, the release and `EVT_POINTER_ABORT`, so a release off the covers still ends the touch (`touch`); the payload moves `SLIDE_OFFSET` itself and finishes through `carousel_glide` (`patch/navigation.c`), the wheel's one retargeted animator split out of `home_step`, with the run's easing set on the animator (`ANIM_EASING`, `+0x38`: `widget_animator_init` stores `easing_get(type)` there, so a reused animator would keep its first). Every finish commits through stock's completion as a wheel step does. On an album's tracks the view is hidden and made insensitive: stock's hit test (`widget_find_target_default`, `0x669c6c`) checks only the enable and sensitive bits (`+0x24` mask 9), not visibility, so a hidden covers' view over the list would take its taps and swipes.

- **Drag.** A move past `CF_SLOP` (8 px) more sideways than up or down is the covers'; one more up or down leaves the touch to stock (the Quick Settings pull-down). From there the offset follows the finger, one album per `CF_DRAG_PX` (90 px, about the centre cover's own travel on screen), starting where it passed the slop so nothing jumps. An artist's albums give past an end as iOS's rubber band, `x · 0.55 / (1 + x · 0.55 / D)` with `D` two fifths of a stride, so the centre never passes half a cover, where `visual` would wrap to the other end. A touch catching the band (or a wheel bounce) starts from its inverse (`unbound`), so the shelf doesn't jump.
- **Release.** The finger's speed is taken over its last 100 ms of moves, by each event's post time (`EVENT_TIME`; moves queued behind a frame are dispatched together) (0 if it rested `CF_STILL_MS`, 60 ms, before lifting; at most `CF_VMAX`, 6000 px/s, so a burst of queued moves can't throw the shelf). The covers glide to the whole cover that speed reaches in `CF_PROJECT_MS` (400 ms), and a flick of at least `CF_FLICK` (250 px/s) never settles back against its direction. The glide is `cubic_out` (`EASE_CUBIC_OUT`, AWTK's `easing_type_t` 5) for `3 d / v` whenever the goal lies ahead of the finger's motion, so it starts at the finger's speed and decelerates, between `CF_SETTLE_MS` (280 ms, also a still release or one that settles back) and `CF_GLIDE_MAX` (1.4 s); an artist's ends clamp it.
- **Tap.** A touch that never passed the slop hit-tests the drawn frame (`coverflow_hit`): the frontmost cover body under the finger, nearest first as drawn, rounded corners (iPod's `cf_inset`, as `column`) and reflections left out, and no cover under a quarter bright. The centre cover at rest opens, as Centre's click; a side cover glides to the centre with the wheel's easing in `CF_TAP_MS` (320 ms). The flat fallback opens its centre cover only.
- **Catch.** A touch while the covers move pauses the animator where they are. Lifting without a drag settles on the nearest cover and opens nothing; a drag carries on from there. A down while a touch is still open (a release the input queue dropped), or any key with the window manager no longer pressed (`coverflow_lost_touch`, in `ringnav`), ends the old one first, so Centre opens the cover the shelf settles on; a touch ending after Centre changed the screen still settles the covers on a whole one, and covers built anew drop it.

ponytail: no MIPS run of the gesture: `test/coverflow.py` drives the C on the host (`-m32`), and `test/patch.py` checks the MIPS renderer byte for byte. The drag scale, reach and glide lengths are `CF_*` in `patch/offsets.inc`, set by eye; tune them on the device.

**Checks.** `test/coverflow.py` builds the renderer on the host and checks the selected cover pixel for pixel, that nothing is drawn outside the frame, mirror symmetry at rest and mid-turn, brightness one and two out, the reflection's rows and fade, that a small step changes little (including where the centre album changes hands), the texture window's loads and releases, the centre click, touch (the hit test against every drawn pixel at eight positions, taps, drags, flicks, a flick dispatched in one batch, a resting release, a vertical move, a catch and a dropped release), one- and two-album libraries, missing art, Refresh, return from tracks and the allocation-failure fallback; `--captures DIR` writes the renderer's frames at rest and a quarter, half and three-quarter turn as PNGs. `test/patch.py` runs the MIPS payload's renderer against the host build byte for byte, and the page's paint, centre click and fallback, and prints the payload's instructions per frame (about 3.5 million for a frame mid-turn under the mocks): a relative measure, not a frame time.

## Position memory

Position memory is a bounded 64-entry table keyed by the audited top-window context in `patch/contexts.inc` and a content fingerprint. Selecting a row updates its entry and moves it to the front; a full table evicts the least recently selected entry. Restoration alone does not promote an entry.

Local folders use the full bounded `g_folder_path`; local music lists use the stock browsing class, saved query and artist/album modes (`g_class_type`, `g_local_classinfo_save`, `g_artist_type`, `album_modetype`). Root Albums, Artists, Composers and Genres lists ignore the saved detail query: opening an artist overwrites it without changing the parent collection. These are the inputs used by the stock folder and local-list loaders, not playback metadata. The builder verifies each object's size against the stock ELF. A changed fingerprint resets selection even if a surviving surface has the same row count, then restores the matching recent position and reveals it, or keeps the stock viewport if none is remembered. Fixed settings menus can restore by window name alone; multi-pane and other dynamic pages without an audited content identity keep their live widget selection but do not restore across recreation.

Memory lasts until power-off, with no filesystem writes. The table occupies 1,280 bytes. Ordinary paints do not search it; repeated selections in the current scope match its first entry and shift nothing. Lookup and promotion are bounded by `POS_MEM` (64).

Non-virtual lists also remember hashes of the selected row's first two text values, read through the stock `widget_get_text` UTF-32 accessor. A matching second text outranks proximity; equal matches choose the occurrence nearest the remembered index. Rows without text fall back to the index within the same content scope. The stock rows expose no stable item id. Both scroll views and virtual music tables protect an interrupted recall glide from silently replacing the remembered selection with a currently visible row. A real tap cancels the glide and selects its target; a wheel step advances from the remembered logical row. Pointer-down and synchronous click delivery load rows without recall, so native touch sees the widget it pressed and a rebuilt list cannot move or rebind that row first. Synchronous table rebinds discard the old row-pool snapshot before resolving the selected entry.

## Touch mode

A session-wide touch-mode flag suppresses only custom drawing, independently of logical selection and gesture state. Touch-down and native clicks (including clicks without touch-down) set it and invalidate the visible window. It persists through settling and page changes. Accepted wheel input, including a turn at a boundary, centre selection or an accepted Return release restores drawing, so the page Return goes back to shows its row; iPod's Home list ignores the flag and always draws its bar; synchronous centre-generated clicks bypass the native-click observer.

## Drawing

The canvas hook intersects the existing clip with the viewport and restores the clip, the LCD fill color and the LCD stroke color; this firmware's `canvas_save/restore` do not save those properties. The outline is one translucent fill (`0x402b2b2b`, a per-color alpha) plus two concentric one-pixel `canvas_stroke_rounded_rect` calls, a dark separator at radius 9 and the 70%-opaque white line at radius 8, with `bg_r = NULL`. The separator is the stock dark surface at `0xb32b2b2b`, so it disappears on the dark theme and only shows over bright artwork. It never calls `canvas_set_global_alpha`, so the shared alpha is untouched. A rounded call that reports failure (either stroke) or a row too small for the corner radius, keeps the square separator-plus-white outline instead.

iPod draws a selection bar instead of the outline. `widget_paint` (`0x65f28c`) calls `widget_on_paint_background`, then the widget's own paint, its children and `widget_on_paint_border`, so the background hook paints the bar behind the rows. The border hook then draws nothing, and the selection is loaded and settled once per frame, in the background hook. The bar spans the full surface width at the selected row's height and position, including the end bump; an entry narrower than half the surface, a grid tile, gets the bar in its own rectangle. It is a flat fill drawn through the existing equal-stop painter as one-pixel `canvas_fill_rect` (`0x634ad0`) bands, which avoids the stock `gradient_t` ABI, followed by a one-pixel top highlight. In Theme: Minimal the fill is `#EEEEEC` under every accent; selected title and metadata colors are resolved from paint ancestry by `ringnav_style_color`, without mutating recycled widget styles, and Home uses a white dot instead of a bar. Theme: Classic draws the accent's top, bottom and highlight (`ACCENTS`) on list rows; grid tiles use the same inset card as Minimal with the accent fill, framing the cover, which both themes inset. Display's Corners (`st.rounded`) picks the pill and rounded tiles, or the full-width bar and square tiles. Classic leaves the ink alone ([ipod.md](ipod.md#theme)). The bar uses the same clip, color restore and touch-mode rules as the outline. Rows only show it where they are transparent: after the iPod style edits, list rows, table rows, grid tiles (`s_btn_listblack`) and the settings rows (`s_btn_listitem`) are.

The iPod border hook draws the chevrons. Windows flagged `DRILL` in `patch/contexts.inc` get the stock `list_into` image on every row, over the bar as well: `widget_load_image` (`0x65c1ec`, served from the image manager's cache) once per paint, then `canvas_draw_icon` (`0x638c9c`: canvas, bitmap, centre x, centre y, relative to the canvas origin like the fills) per row, placed as stock rows place their `img_into`: left edge `CHEVRON_W` (58) from the row's right edge, inside the same viewport clip, which hides rows outside it. A playlist button spans the same 20 to 355 pixels as a category or album button, so the glyphs cover the same screen columns (317 to 327); Local Music's own images sit 5 pixels further right at a fixed x. On a drill window the row layouter lays out as if that image were the last child, so the title and a playlist's "more" button end before it. The widget's own window, found through its parents, decides, so a window painted during a transition keeps its own rows. Rows narrower than half the surface (tiles) get none, and none are drawn while `g_navbar_status` is set, as stock hides its own chevrons in multi-select.

The iPod border hook also draws the fast-scroll letter over the list. A step of more than one row (so the list has more than `SHORT_LIST_MAX` rows and the run has reached `LIST_FIRST_MS`) restarts a one-shot `LETTER_MS` (400 ms) timer. While it runs and the spin run is still past `LIST_FIRST_MS`, the surface draws the selected row's first non-space character, past an article on a library page ([Sorting](#sorting)). The text is the row's first non-empty `widget_get_text`, taken from the same `row_id` walk as the position-memory hashes, and a virtual table finds its logical row in the recycled pool; an offscreen or textless row draws nothing. The box is `LETTER_BOX` (80) square, `canvas_fill_rounded_rect` at radius `LETTER_RADIUS` (12) in the stock dark surface at `LETTER_ALPHA` (85%), or a square fill without a vgcanvas. The glyph is `LETTER_PX` (60) in the system default font (`canvas_set_font` `0x633e6c` with a null name), white (`canvas_set_text_color` `0x633d9c`), centred by `canvas_draw_text_in_rect` (`0x639524`) with the canvas alignment fields (`+0x34` horizontal, `+0x30` vertical) set to centre and middle. The clip, fill and text colors and alignment are restored afterwards; the font is not, since stock sets it before drawing its own text. The expiry repaints the live surface, which then draws nothing.

## Accent

iPod's accent ([ipod.md](ipod.md#display-settings); each theme keeps its own; Minimal's own accent uses Graphite's) replaces Shanling red wherever it is drawn:
the theme's style colors, inline `style:*` colors (a mutable style answers through the same
vtable), decoded images, and the payload's own selection bar and progress fill, which read
`ACCENTS` directly. `accent_map` (`patch/navigation.c`) is a pure function on one `color_t` (bytes
r, g, b, a). Stock uses `#FF1448`, `#7F0A24`, the pressed tint `#3D1920` and `#FF144840`, all
stock red blended with a neutral: each channel is `t * red + k`. Least squares against red with
the mean removed gives `t` (in 1/4096), then `k`. A color within `RED_TOLERANCE` (8) of that
blend, with `t` between 1/16 and 17/16 and `k` not below the tolerance, becomes `t * tone + k`
for one of the preset's tones (`ACCENTS`): the red tone for text colors (a property name ending
`text_color`, `tk_str_end_with`) and image pixels, the light tone for every other color property
and gradient stop; alpha is kept, and anything else, greys and other hues included,
is returned as it was. Anti-aliased edges onto black, white or a transparent background are such
blends, so they follow. No preset's colors are red blends, so mapping twice changes nothing.
Crimson returns every color unchanged, so its theme is stock.

- `style_get_color(color_t *ret, style, name, default)` returns its color through the hidden
  first argument. The hook runs stock through a trampoline and maps `*ret`, comparing the
  property name only when the color maps. Stock first asks `style_get_gradient` for every color
  (`bal` at `0x649fe8`; `style_data_get_gradient` turns a plain color into a one-stop gradient),
  so the gradient hook leaves the call that returns to `0x649ff0` unmapped, and the color hook
  alone picks the tone. The builder pins that `bal`.
- `style_get_gradient(style, name, gradient_t *out)` (`0x649f3c`) is a 12-instruction leaf with
  no GOT setup: null checks on the style and its vtable, then a tail call to the vtable's
  `get_gradient` (+0x18). The builder pins its first three words (`0x10800009, 0, 0x8c820000`)
  and replaces the first two with the jump; the payload repeats the checks, calls `get_gradient`
  through `$t9`, and maps the stops only when it filled the caller's `out`: `gradient_t` is 0x4c
  bytes (`gradient_init`), with the stop count at +8 and up to 8 `{color, offset}` stops from
  +0xc (`gradient_add_stop`, `gradient_get_first_color`). Widget backgrounds come this way.
- `image_manager_add(manager, name, bitmap_t *)` copies a freshly decoded bitmap into the cache.
  The hook maps its pixels first, only for plain theme image names (no `/` or `:`, so covers by
  path, `file://` or online URL are never touched), and only when the format
  is one of the 32-bit ones: `bitmap_t` has w at 0, h at 4, the format as a u16 at 0xe
  (1 RGBA, 2 ABGR, 3 BGRA, 4 ARGB in memory order, 4 bytes each by `bitmap_get_bpp_of_format`),
  its row stride from `bitmap_get_line_length`, and the pixels from
  `bitmap_lock_buffer_for_write`/`bitmap_unlock_buffer`. Premultiplied pixels are red blended
  with black, so they map the same way.

The confirm pop-up's discs (`confirm_ok`, `confirm_cancel` and their pressed images, names
starting `CONFIRM_IMAGE`) go through the same mapping with `CONFIRM_SURFACE` (`#2B2B2B`) as the
tone under every preset, Crimson included ([ipod.md](ipod.md#pop-ups)). The quick settings'
control discs (names starting `DROPDOWN_IMAGE`, `drop_`, except the brightness suns, `DROPDOWN_SUN`)
take the preset's red tone; when that tone is brighter than `GLYPH_LIGHT_MAX` (Graphite's silver),
an image that holds red also maps its neutral glyph pixels to `CONFIRM_SURFACE`, so the white glyph
turns dark ([quick settings](ipod.md#quick-settings)). Grey discs hold no red and are unchanged.
Any other image that holds red and an opaque pure-white pixel (a switch's knob, a disc's glyph; `#7F7F7F`
on a pressed disc) takes
the light tone instead of the red one, as do button backgrounds (names starting `BUTTON_IMAGE`,
`btn_`), whose white label is a widget's text; an image with red alone (a tick, the time page's
colon) keeps the red tone. Only Graphite's two tones differ, so only it scans for white.
The settings rows' category icons (`settings_icons` in `patch/ipod.json`, which the builder
writes to `SETTINGS_ICON_NAMES` in `stock.h`) are never mapped: several use stock red as their
category colour (Network, DLNA, Wi-Fi, Backlight), like the purple and orange ones.
`red_map` is the mapping with an explicit tone;
`accent_map` is it with the preset's tone, and the identity for Crimson.

Changing the accent saves it, sets the progress fill, calls `image_manager_unload_all(image_manager())`
(`0x645024`) and `widget_invalidate_force` on the window manager. Widgets ask for their style
colors, gradients and images on every paint, so the next frame decodes the images again through
the hook and shows every recoloured part; nothing needs a restart.

## Page transitions (iPod)

Stock V1.32 carries AWTK's window animators and uses one: `saverscreen_page.bin` has `close_anim_hint` `slide_left(duration=200,easing=cubic_out)`, the only hint in the stock assets. The window manager builds the animator named by a window's `anim_hint` (`window_animator_htranslate_create`, `0x683a08`, for `htranslate`) when it opens or closes. `navigator_to_with_context` (`0x4ec758`) opens a page above the rest and `navigator_back` (`0x4ec8e0`) is `window_manager_back`, so the page below stays alive and Return reverses the slide.

iPod sets the hint in its assets ([ipod.md](ipod.md#transitions)). `htranslate` animates two snapshots. `window_animator_prepare` (`0x682ee8`) paints both pages into offline canvases, through the payload's paint hooks, before `window_manager_is_animating` (`0x66daf4`) becomes true and while the sliding page is the top window; a hinted page is not painted before that, so its snapshot is its first paint. Each frame then blits the snapshots and paints only the status bar. The payload follows the snapshots in four places (`slides()`, `patch/navigation.c`). A surface whose own window is under a sliding top window draws the row it holds and nothing else: loading, recall and settling read the top page's state. A sliding page reveals its remembered row at once instead of by the glide, so it does not arrive mid-glide. Home's art is checked when Home itself is painted, not only when it is the top window. After a touch, a Return by button ends [touch mode](#touch-mode) on a 0 ms timer instead of at once: the closing page's snapshot, painted inside stock's handling of the release, keeps its row hidden, while the page behind shows its own.

## Status bar (iPod)

The background hook also handles top-level widgets, whose parent is the window manager. The status bar widget, which `system_bar_init` stores in the `system_bar` global (`0xa3a6d0`, a size-checked `CONTEXT_DATA` entry), gets one `canvas_fill_rect` in black, `BAR_COLOR` (`#000000`, both Themes), without a highlight, with the LCD fill color restored.

`label_clock` in the bar shows the device's local time, read once a minute rather than on paint: `clock_show` calls `time` and `localtime_r` (stock libc, with a caller-owned time buffer) for the hour and minute (`struct tm` `+8`, `+4`), applies Auto Light Mode's hour, and arms a timer due at the next minute (`60 - tm_sec` seconds; a minute after a failed read). The label follows stock's 24-hour setting (`g_time24h_flag`): `%02d:%02d` (`18:14`), or `%d:%02d %s`, a 12-hour clock without seconds or a leading zero (`12:05 AM`, `6:14 PM`), through `tk_snprintf`, then `widget_set_text_utf8`. A failed `time` (`-1`) or `localtime_r` (null), or an out-of-range field, shows `--:--`. The minute and the 24-hour setting are kept as a key, so the label is looked up and written only when what it shows changes. Besides the timer, `clock_show` runs at once for a new time zone (`ringnav_timezone`), a time set by hand, and a wake, and the pass after the bar first paints or paints with a new 24-hour setting rearms it at 0 ms; a network time sync while lit shows at the next minute. Nothing else reads the time, so stock threads' `localtime` (one shared static buffer) has nothing to race. Every page and dialog shows it. There is no volume flash: stock `on_wm_keyup_fun` opens `dialog/volume_dialog` on every volume turn (`0x4e8bb0`).

The same paints run `bar_sync`. `systembar_showface` (`0x52f610`) sets `img_bt`'s image every tick: a codec badge (`bar_sbc`, `bar_aac`, `bar_aptx`, `bar_aptxhd`, `bar_ldac`, by `bt_showcoding`) while Bluetooth is linked, otherwise `bar_bt` or `bar_btcon`. It also sets `img_battery`'s image (`bar_charge`, `bar_lowcharge` under 15%, else `bar_battery`), `label_battery`'s `%d%%` text and `progress_battery`'s value, and never the battery widgets' visibility. A badge that differs from the last one arms a `CODEC_MS` UI timer, whose steps set `widget_set_opacity` (`0x659f24`, the widget's `+0x34` byte, which `widget_paint` applies as the canvas global alpha) down and back up, switching the image to `bar_btcon` halfway. After that the bar's background paint, which runs before its children, puts `bar_btcon` back over each badge stock sets again, so no badge frame is drawn. A hidden `img_bt` or a non-badge image other than `bar_btcon` ends the flash. `bar_sync` then hides `img_battery` and `label_battery` and shows `view_battery` with `widget_set_visible`, which returns early for an unchanged state and otherwise marks the parent for relayout (`widget_set_need_relayout_children`). The slot is sized to its ink: a percentage is its digit count times the `'0'` advance plus the `'%'` advance, both measured with `canvas_measure_text` on the first battery paint (`BATT_PCT_W` until then). `view_battery` is painted in the background hook from the level (`label_battery`'s text, since stock sets `progress_battery` to 0 while charging) and the charge state (`img_battery`'s image), and is invalidated when those or its displayed mode change. `BATTERY=3` uses a compact 28×15 solid rounded battery (radius 3; stock squares any radius under 3) with dark 13 px digits inside, omitting the percent sign; its body follows the charge-state colour. Resizing the slot immediately relayouts its parent row.

## Now Playing (iPod)

`playing_page_init` (`0x52ca88`, standard PIC prologue, resumed at `0x52ca94`) registers the page's handlers, runs the page update (`0x52b6f4`) once and starts a 250 ms widget timer (`playing_timer_start`, `0x52c114`) that runs it again. The update finds each widget with a recursive `widget_lookup`, skips any it does not find, and gets the times from `player_playtime_and_length` (`0x514128`): elapsed and total seconds from `mclGetPlayTime` (`0x5ac078`), less a CUE track's start, elapsed clamped to the total, both 0 while stopped. It sets `slider_play` to value = elapsed, max = total, and formats both labels with `toolsTimeItoa` (`0x5bc410`): `%02d:%02d`, or `%02d:%02d:%02d` from an hour. `label_playlen` is its only reference to that name.

The hook runs the stock init, then caches `label_ipod_pos`, `label_ipod_album`, `label_ipod_remain` and the slider, and syncs them. It also caches `img_cover`, whose border paint (after the image) rounds its corners: for each of the `NP_ART_RADIUS` rows of a corner, one `canvas_fill_rect` outside the arc and one edge pixel at the alpha it leaves uncovered, in 1/16 pixel from an integer square root, both in the page's black. Painting the top window or the status bar syncs them again. The position is `MCL_POS + 1` of `deque_size(*mcl_pdeqplaylist)` ("3 of 12"), empty without a current record. The album is that record's `REC_ALBUM` (the record comes from `queue_now`, which Home's art shares), or, once `player_get_id3info` (`0x5146bc`) has parsed that record's file, the album it parsed: `g_play_id3_info` (`0xa4103c`, `0xa9c` bytes) holds the parsed path at `+0`, album at `+0x404`, artist at `+0x504` and title at `+0x684` (`now_tag`). For a CUE track (`mclGetCueFlag`: the playing record has a CUE start or end) stock copies the record's tags instead of reading the file: stock's sheet parser (`0x5c02f4`) gives each track the sheet's `TITLE` as album and the last `PERFORMER` seen (the track's, else the sheet's) as artist. Its copy of the parsed path (`ID3_PATH`, `0x5148e8`), which every fresh parse reaches before Now Playing's title and artist lines are written, becomes `jal ringnav_id3_path`: an empty album or artist there is taken from the file's own tags (`toolsGetMusicInfo`), so a sheet without `TITLE` or `PERFORMER` (common for audiobooks) shows the book and author instead of Unknown Artist. Album artist is filled the same way. ponytail: only the playing track's tags; Library lists and the database keep the sheet's empty fields (Library's Artists and Albums leave audiobooks out anyway), so Home's and Now Playing's art key (artist, album) can miss Coverflow's cache, keyed on the database's values; fill at scan time if that matters. When a folder play (class 1) reaches its last track with the folder-jump setting on (Folder Skip, the byte `0xa3be53`), `mclNextSong` calls `on_player_autochange` (`0x513fc0`), whose `toolsLoadNextDir` and `player_refresh_playqueue(1)` replace the queue with the next folder's untagged file records, and only the parsed tags name the next album. Every stock folder jump tests class 1, so Coverflow plays its albums as stock's album class `0xff10` and Most Played and Shuffle Songs as `0xf001`. `album_memory` also writes the album into `g_memory_info` (`+0x60d` Unknown flag, `+0x611` name), as `player_load_songlist` does for stock lists, so stock resume rebuilds that album with `getMusicByAlbum`. Home's art keys its Coverflow cache lookup the same way. The remaining time is the slider's max less its value, in stock's format with a leading minus, so it matches the elapsed label and follows a touch drag. Each label is written only when its source changes (position, queue length, album string pointer, or those seconds); `EVT_DESTROY` drops the cache. Stock's total label stays hidden.

The V8.6 folder-rollover regression in `test/patch.py` executes stock `player_mainscheduling` → `mclAutoChange` → `mclNextSong` → `on_player_autochange` → `toolsLoadNextDir` → `player_refresh_playqueue` → `mclLoadPlayList`, then stock tag parsing, cover-worker completion and the Now Playing timer (`0x52bffc`). Filesystem enumeration, tag decoding, image decoding and the audio decoder are mocked at their boundaries; the harness does not supply the resulting player metadata globals. Equal-sized and one-track sibling folders, reused positions, missing tags/art, consecutive transitions, and delayed scheduler/cover completion pass in Stock and iPod. iPod's paint hooks also verify album text and Home art before and after completion. This does not reproduce a V8.6 regression, so no additional metadata firmware change is made.

**Info page** (both builds). The page update fills the info page through `0x52a508` (its one call, a `bal` at `0x52baf4`, after the player status checks): `label_album`, `label_genre`, `label_encode`, `label_year`, `label_bits`, `label_kbps` and `label_sample` as `%s:%s` or `%s:%d…` (`Album:Kid A`, `Bit Depth:16bit`, `Sample Rate:44.1kHz`; `%s:%s` is also wpa_supplicant's literal, so stays), `label_size` as `%s:%.2fMB`, and `label_location` as the bare folder with `/mnt/mmc/` as `SD:/` (it looks up `player_location` and never uses it), a missing value as the first `player_unknown`, which en_US has twice ("Unknown Artist", then "Unknown"). The build makes that call `jal ringnav_np_info` (`NP_INFO_CALL`), which runs it, then rewrites each line: `: ` after the label, a space before `MB`, `kbps` and `kHz` and a hyphen before `bit` after a digit, "Unknown Artist" as "Unknown", and the folder after `player_location`'s label (`getFormatString`), only where the text changes. Its labels **Genre** and **Year** are `LABELS` renames of `genre` (which the search page's tab shares) and `player_era`.

`set_repeatpoint`'s marker placement (`0x52a268`) puts the 3x10 A-B markers at x = 50 + t \* 290 / length, y 250. iPod keeps y and patches x and width to `NP_BAR`. The slider's 44px target is centred on the same bar (window y 255), with the page dots moved 6px up, outside the target. The dots' `slide_indicator` gets its own anchor (`anchor_x` `187px`, `anchor_y` `0px`, the point stock's took: the slide_view's centre, above the indicator's middle, so the dots on its top edge). With none, stock centres them on its `indicated_target` as `widget_to_screen` places it, and that subtracts the widget's own `xoffset` property, which `slide_view` sets to the drag during a swipe. The indicator lays its dots out again only after its own layout (any relayout of the window), but then from wherever the slide_view was dragged, so the dots slid with the page until the next layout. Native `slide_with_bar` accepts a press anywhere in that rectangle and grabs the pointer, so a drag stays on the slider outside its bounds. Its dragger is skipped when the `icon` style lookup is empty and `border_color` is transparent, so the asset clears `border_color` in every state and no thumb shows; the fill then ends exactly at the value. `np_fill` sets the fill in every state a press passes through, so a touch keeps the accent.

**Volume.** Outside a scrub the wheel stays on the volume, and stock opens `dialog/volume_dialog`, a full-screen transparent dialog holding `slider_vol` and `label_vol`. Its `highlight="default(alpha=200)"` made the window manager draw everything under it as a dimmed snapshot taken at open, so iPod's `volume_dialog.bin` drops that property (`volume_dialog` in `tools/ipod.py`) and nothing dims. When the paint hook paints that dialog, `vol_paint` draws the volume in the dialog's own paint. With Now Playing directly under it (the window manager's second-to-last child) it covers the band from `slider_play` to `label_playtime` in black, then draws the track (`TRACK_COLOR`, the slider's `bar_size`) and a white fill to `value / max`, both pills like the capsule, and "Volume N" centred in the times' row at `NP_TIMES_PX`. Over any other window, Quick Settings included, it draws a rounded `VOL_PANEL_*` panel in the fast-scroll letter's style (`FILL_RGB` at `LETTER_ALPHA`, `LETTER_RADIUS`) exactly over Quick Settings' brightness row (the build checks it), its rounded corners clear of the glass corners, with the bar as a pill on a `VOL_PANEL_TRACK` grey track (a non-zero fill at least a round dot) and just the number at its right, at `VOL_PANEL_PX`. `slider_vol` and `label_vol` are hidden in the iPod asset before the first paint, so transition snapshots cannot expose the stock full-screen controls; `slider_vol` is moved onto the area drawn, so stock's partial repaints land on it. Those alone stopped after a tick or two on the device, so while the dialog is up a `VOL_POLL_MS` (50 ms) timer compares `slider_vol`'s value with the one last drawn and repaints the whole dialog when it changed; it removes itself once the dialog is no longer on top.

## Visualizer (iPod)

hciplayer's PEQ filter (`patch/peq_player.c`) taps what it plays, after `peq_process`, into `/tmp/q2vis` (tmpfs), a `vis_tap` (`patch/peq.h`): a ring of `VIS_RING` stereo float frames, the frames written (`seq`), the ring's rate and the `CLOCK_MONOTONIC` time of the last write. It keeps the first two channels, and above 48 kHz every `rate / 44100`th frame, so the ring holds about 1.5 s at any rate, room for hciplayer's 500 ms ALSA buffer (ao_alsa's `buffer_time` 500000 at `0x438a2c`, 16 periods). No lock: the writer stores the frames, then the rate and stamp, then `seq`, and the reader tolerates a torn frame. demo makes the file (`open`, `ftruncate`) under `/tmp/q2vis.tmp` and renames it, so hciplayer never maps a short file; hciplayer looks for it (`open`, `mmap64`) once a second of audio (frames counted, no clock call) until it appears. demo maps it read-write and bumps `want` each tick; after a second of audio with no bump the writer stops copying, and the next bump clears the ring, so a return never shows audio from before.

`ringnav_playing` adds the page (`visualizer_attach`, `patch/visualizer.c`): a `s_listitem_black` `list_item` appended to `slide_view` at run time, so a tap clicks it but a swipe still slides, and the page dots get `max` set to the new child count. The page only takes taps: the `slide_view` clips a page added at run time to a sliver when it paints it, so the visualizer draws in the paint hook of Now Playing's window instead, after all its children, clipped to the slide area and with the canvas origin moved there, and its Halo art is an `image` child of the window over the area's centre. That image takes `img_cover`'s image name and draw type, so stock's reload of the cover (load, set, unload) refreshes it too, and shows only while the Halo draws. Painting starts a `1000 / VIS_FPS` ms timer, which removes itself once Now Playing is not on top, the `slide_view`'s `value` is not the last page or the screen is off; each tick invalidates the slide_view. Nothing draws unless settled: no window animation, and the page's own paint, which runs just before the window's in the same frame and draws nothing, last found its canvas at the slide area's left edge (and hides the Halo art the moment it does not), so the page is not being dragged or sliding (`value` only changes once a slide ends); a tap leaves it there, and the timer skips repaints while a finger is down. 1.5 s after the audio stops (everything at rest, the style name faded) it repaints once more, with the track's title and artist (`scrlabel_title`, `scrlabel_artist`, joined by a middle dot) where the style name shows, then stops repainting. A window paint that finds the page on top of the slide_view but not settled (under a closing window such as the lock screen, mid-slide or dragged) arms a `VIS_RETRY_MS` (250 ms) one-shot that invalidates the slide_view once it is settled; paused, nothing else would draw it. Each tick takes `VIS_N` (1024) frames ending `VIS_LATENCY_MS` (480) behind the last write, moved on by the time since it: that buffer, which MPlayer refills a period at a time, less about a period. Bluetooth adds its own delay, so it stays the sync knob. It runs a Hann window and a radix-2 FFT over the mono mix and takes each of 64 log-spaced bands' (40 Hz to 16 kHz) loudest bin, 0 at `VIS_FLOOR_DB` below a full-scale sine and 1 at it (`vis_bands`). Bars rise fast and fall at `VIS_DECAY`, and caps hold `VIS_HOLD_MS`, then fall at `VIS_GRAVITY`. The VU needle is a one-pole follower of the window's RMS (tau 65 ms, 99% in 300 ms) on the meter's voltage scale, with 0 VU at `VIS_VU_REF_DB` dBFS; the LED lights at `VIS_LED_DB` dBFS peak. With nothing playing (`mclGetPlayStatus` not 2, or no write in a second) everything comes to rest. Drawing uses the stock vgcanvas entry points (`canvas_get_vgcanvas`, which clips to the canvas, then its path, fill and stroke calls; `float_t` is a float and `color_t` passes by value), translated to the slide area.

The tap sees only what the filter sees. Boot instantiates the filter only when an EQ was left on (`PLAYSET/EQFLAG`, see [Parametric EQ](#parametric-eq)). So with the EQ off the page's first timer tick (outside the paint) while something plays calls `peq_chain` (`patch/crossfade.c`), which calls `peq_attach` (`patch/peq_ui.c`) once a boot, unless the EQ is on or any `peq_stock_eq` this boot has already put the filter in (`peq_chained`). `peq_attach` does what the editors' Off does, `peq_stock_eq`, which instantiates the filter in bypass, after making sure the [EQ state](#3eq) is saved Off, so a mode saved on while the stock flag is clear stays silent. Bypass is bit-exact, so 16- and 24-bit streams reach the output unchanged through the float conversion. DSD passes through the filter untouched, so during DSD the page rests. Boot itself is left alone: a listener who never opens the page keeps the stock chain.

## Boot resume (iPod)

Stock `home_page_init` resumes when Memory Playback (`g_memory_play`) or car mode (`g_carmode`) is set. `memeory_startplayer` (`0x5164e0`) reads the saved state, rebuilds the queue into a new deque, sets the saved position with `mclSetStartSeekTime` when `g_memory_play` is 2 or in car mode, and returns the track index; it neither plays nor opens a page. Home then calls `navigator_to_with_context("playing_page", ctx)` (the `jalr` at `0x523de0`) with `ctx` `{queue, index, class, mode}`, mode 2 in car mode and 3 otherwise. `playing_page_init` passes `ctx` to `player_start` unless the class is `0xff`; `player_start` loads the queue, starts the player and pauses it (`mclSetPause`) unless the mode is 2.

iPod makes that `jalr` a checked `jal ringnav_boot`; the delay slot still stores the mode. In car mode `ringnav_boot` makes the stock call unchanged. Otherwise it makes the page's `player_start` call without the page, so the queue and track come back paused and Home stays; the saved time is restored in Location mode, while Track starts at the beginning. Home destroys its deque afterwards, as before, since `mclLoadPlayList` copies it. The page's two resets before `player_start` (key-seek mode and a countdown) are page state, set again when it opens. `hciplayer` touches `/tmp/notfile` or `/tmp/notsupport` for a missing or unsupported track; the window manager's `on_wm_timer_dialog` (`0x4ea784`) turns them into a `msginfo_dialog` toast on any page, and `playing_page_init` only deletes stale ones, so a bad restored track still shows its toast on Home.

The English stock labels are Memory Playback: Off / Track / Location and
System: In-Vehicle Mode. Home's Now Playing action (`on_home_playing_onclick`
at `0x5237d0`) calls `on_backplay_click` (`0x4e805c`), which opens the player with
class `0xff`; this skips `player_start` and preserves the paused state. Play/Pause
resumes audio.

## Scrub (iPod)

**Stock audit.** `playing_page` is not a navigation context, so a centre release (218, the power key) passed through to `on_wm_keyup_fun`, whose `screen_action` (`0x4f77d4`) turns the screen off, and a wheel release to the same handler, which changes the volume and opens `dialog/volume_dialog`. The page's own key-up handler (`0x52c248`) only knows Return and keys 222/223. Those drive stock's key seek: a long press sets `0xa3a6c0` (`0x529df0`), key-down (`0x52c754`) calls `playing_timer_clear` (`0x52c634`), sets the mode byte `0xa3a6c1` to 1, steps `slider_play` 5 seconds within `[0, max]` and writes `label_playtime`, and key-up calls `player_seek_time` (`0x51454c`) with the slider value, then `playing_timer_start` (`0x52c114`). A touch drag takes the slider's handlers from the page's `widget_foreach` visitor (`0x52ae6c`): value-changing (`0xf`, `0x52c950`) writes the label and, in mode 0, clears the timer and sets mode 2; value-changed (`0xe`, `0x52c5fc`) seeks the same way only in mode 2 (`0x52c364`). `player_seek_time` takes track seconds: it adds the current record's CUE start (`+0x48`), pauses DLNA, calls `mclSetSeek` (`0x5ab1ac`), then polls `player_playtime` every 10 ms, up to 2 s, until it reaches the target. `player_seek_start` (`0x5143a8`) is DLNA's and not used. `slider_set_value` emits will-change (`0xd`) and changed (`0xe`), never changing, so a set value in mode 0 neither seeks nor writes the label.

**Scrub.** On the top `playing_page`, with no window animation or pressed pointer, the key-up hook takes the centre button. A release arms a `DOUBLE_CLICK_MS` timer, and a second release before it ends cancels it and starts scrub. While scrubbing, `center_press` arms no timer: a release ends the scrub at once, and a release within `DOUBLE_CLICK_MS` after that one is swallowed, so neither a single nor a double press turns the screen off. Otherwise the timer, a single press, replays the centre release to stock `on_wm_keyup_fun` (`0x4e8934`, which reads only the event's key there), so the screen turns off as on any other page; a release after an overdue timer runs it first and then passes through to stock. Starting calls `playing_timer_clear`, reads the slider value as the target and sets the slider's fill (`fg_color` in the normal, pressed, over and focused states) to white. While scrubbing, a wheel release moves the target by `SCRUB_STEP` (5) seconds times the 100 ms ramp's step (`ramp` keyed on the slider with `WHEEL_RAMP_MS`, so 5 to 40 seconds; the lists' gentler curve does not apply: every tick moves the target), clamped to `[0, max]`. It sets the slider value, writes `label_playtime` in stock's format and syncs the remaining label, marks the target moved if the step changed it (a tick against an end does not), rearms the `SCRUB_MS` (3 s) end and swallows the release; nothing seeks while ticks come in, however far apart. Ending seeks once, with `player_seek_time(target)`, the stock key path's commit, so CUE tracks stay track-relative, and only if the target moved; it then restores the accent's light-tone fill. Paused (`mclGetPlayStatus` 3), `player_seek_time` would resume the track (`config_outputchannel`, then `mclSetResume`) to reach the target, so the scrub sends the seek alone, `mclSetSeek(target + the record's CUE start)`, as `player_seek_time` adds it: hciplayer's `mcl-seek` handler (`0x42c5b4`) mutes the DAC and flags the seek, and its seek (`0x42b010`) leaves the DAC muted while paused, so the track stays paused and plays from the target at Play. Unlike `player_seek_time`, it does not pause DLNA first, so a paused seek is not reported to a DLNA controller. After a seek, `playing_timer_start` waits for `settle_poll` (every `SETTLE_MS`, 250 ms): paused, the player's second stands still until Play, and the page's timer would put the bar back on the old second. The bar keeps the target until `player_playtime_and_length` is within a second of it, the track changes, the player stops, or `SETTLE_PLAY_MS` (2 s) of playing pass without it; a new scrub, the lyrics' wheel or an exit drops the wait (an exit that ends a scrub keeps the one its seek starts). A paused seek is remembered with the player's second when it was sent: reopening Now Playing on the same track, still paused and at that second, puts the bar back on the target and waits again, so a new scrub starts from it; playing or another track forgets it. Without a seek the timer restarts at once. It ends on a centre press, the timeout, Return (swallowed on the page), a touch (before the slider sees it, so a drag seeks the stock way), another top window, or the page's `EVT_DESTROY`, which drops the target. After the playing track changed (queue position and path, recorded at the start) the target is dropped: the next tick ends the scrub without moving it, and any other end does not seek. `player_seek_time` can block the UI thread briefly, as stock's key seek does; it now runs once per scrub instead of after every pause of 150 ms between ticks. The mode byte stays 0 throughout. Outside scrub the wheel stays on the volume.

**Lyrics.** The page's 250 ms timer also keeps the lyrics in view: when `mclGetLyricSize()` is positive it finds the current line among `scroll_lrc`'s children (`0x52bcc8`), sums the rows' heights up to three lines before it (stock creates a row 20 px tall, or 25 px a line when its text is wider than 225 px and wraps, `0x52a0c8`), and calls `scroll_view_scroll_to(scroll_lrc, 0, y, 10)` whenever the view's y offset (`+0x84`, `SCROLL_Y`) differs, so a moved offset lasts one tick. That y is a row top, but `scroll_view_scroll_to` (`0x5f0178`) clamps it to the end, content height (`+0x7c`, `VIEW_CONTENT_H`) less the view's 178 px, which is no whole number of rows, so near the end the top row was cut through. The call (`jalr` at `0x52bed0`, `LYRIC_FOLLOW`) becomes `jal ringnav_lyric_follow` in both builds: `lyric_pad` first grows the content to the first row top at or past that end plus the view's height, so the end is a row top and under a row of blank space shows below the last line. A content no taller than the view is left as is. The page's refresh (`0x52b6f4`) rebuilds the rows (`0x529f0c`, which ends with `scroll_view_set_offset(scroll_lrc, 0, 0)`) whenever `g_forcerefresh_flag` is set: Play/Pause (`playpause_quick_click`), the player's state changes and the page's return to the front (`EVT_WINDOW_TO_FOREGROUND`, `0x12b`) all set it, so the lyrics jumped to the top until the next follow scrolled back. Its one call (`bal` at `0x52b8f8`, `LYRIC_ROWS`) becomes `jal ringnav_lyric_rows`, which puts back the offset the view had; the follow moves it if the line changed. Limit: the rebuilt content is its natural height until the next follow pads it, so near the end the relayout's clamp can cut the top row for up to 250 ms, as above. Limits: scroll_view's relayout (`0x5f17a8`) recomputes the height from the children and clamps the offset, which cuts the row again until the next follow, at most 250 ms on. `ringnav_playing` keeps `slide_view` and `scroll_lrc`.

**Lyrics wheel.** Every UI loop pass, `lyric_poll` (iPod, from `ringnav_sleep`) checks for the lyrics page in front: `slide_view`'s value is 1, `playing_page` is the top window, `usable()`, no window animation or pressed pointer, `scroll_lrc` has children and `mclGetLyricSize()` is positive. While it is, `wheel_raw` is set, as for [Vortex](#games), so the wheel posts no ticks and each pass takes the raw travel from `wheel_travel` (200 units a turn). Setting or clearing it zeroes the travel, so nothing from before carries over, and the wheel is back on ticks and the volume within a pass of leaving. The travel moves a target, in sixteenths of a pixel so that slow turns keep their fractions: `LYRIC_ROW_PX` (20, stock's row; a line, `LYRIC_SLOT`, on the [lyrics page](#word-timed-lyrics)) per tick's worth of travel at the Wheel Sensitivity (`WHEEL_TICK_UNITS` x 50 / `wheel_pct()` units), clockwise down, within `[0, content height - view height]` after `lyric_pad`. A turn moves the lyrics as far as its ticks did, but a pixel at a time instead of a row. Each pass then glides the offset towards the target (`lyric_step`, `scroll_view_set_offset`): a `dt / (dt + LYRIC_EASE_MS)` share of the way left (50 ms, `dt` capped at it), at least a pixel, so the motion eases in whatever the loop's pace. Starting a glide stops any stock scroll animator on the view, a drag's fling among them. While a finger stays on the wheel the lyrics stay where its travel put them; once it lifts (`wheel_held`, which `wheel_sample` sets at a finger's first sample and clears at its lift, raw or not) after travel moved them, the glide's target becomes the row top nearest it (`lyric_snap`), so no row is left cut at the top; the end is a row top already (`lyric_pad`). No key event reaches stock, so the travel itself sets `ringnav_input`'s idle stamp and calls `reset_poweroptions_timer(1, 1, 1)`, which keeps the loop at full pace and the screen on; `np_animating` keeps the pace while a glide moves. A tick that reaches the page before a pass has taken the wheel raw goes to `np_lyrics`, ahead of scrub, which glides to the next or previous row's top from the glide's target, so quick ticks add up.

**Lyrics hold.** Moving the lyrics, by wheel travel, a tick, or a finger that dragged `scroll_lrc` (a press that came down on the lyrics page, with `playing_page` on top, and the offset different at its release; stock's scroll animator stops at the press, so only the finger moves them, and a swipe that brings the page in holds nothing), ends any scrub (`scrub_end`, committing a moved target) and holds them (`lyric_hold`, keeping the playing track's `np_track`). Stock's timer keeps running, so the bar, the times and the highlighted line go on, but `ringnav_lyric_follow` returns without scrolling while the lyrics are held on that track, and while a pointer is pressed. The hold ends when `slide_view` leaves the lyrics page (a swipe to the art, info or visualizer page) or the page is destroyed. Another song (`np_track`: queue place and file, checked every pass while the page exists, on top or not) drops it too and puts the lyrics at the top at once, with no glide, stopping stock's animator (`lyric_top`). So does `ringnav_lyric_rows` when it rebuilds the rows for another song than the last rows' (`lyric_rows_song`): it leaves stock's offset 0 instead of putting back the old one, whatever moved the view since the song changed. Stock's `scroll_view_scroll_to` animates for 300 ms whatever its duration, ignores a new target while an animation whose goal is the top or the end runs, and an animation started with the screen off can still be moving when it comes back on: a follow sent with the last song's rows could otherwise carry the new song's lyrics back to the old place, or move them under a swipe onto the page. Off the page the follow is stock's `scroll_view_scroll_to`, so the lyrics are on the current line again when you swipe back; on the page it glides there instead (`lyric_glide`). Another window on top, the screen off or a touch leave the hold alone. Any other page, or no lyrics, leaves the wheel on the volume. Before 1.4.6 the wheel jumped a row a tick and stopped stock's timer, bar and highlight included, until `SCRUB_MS` after the last tick or a touch, when the page jumped back to the current line. Limits: a drag on the screen stops anywhere, so it can leave the top row cut, and one that ends where it began holds nothing; a song repeated on its own (Repeat One) is the same song, so lyrics held on it stay held.

**Lyrics search.** Now Playing's ⋯ lyrics match (`onlinematching_page`) runs stock's `online_lyricthd` on its own thread: `online_setkeyword_info` has filled `keyinfo` (`0xa40424`: file name, title at +0x200, album at +0x400, artist at +0x500) from the playing track's tags; the thread copies it (`online_getkeyword_info`, under its lock) and searches `title/artist` with `kugou_obtainlyric` (`0x510744`: `mobilecdn.kugou.com` search, then `krc.php`), then `com163_obtainlyric` (NetEase `api/search/pc` and `song/media`, which now want a phone-bound account and return no lyric). Each writes the reply to `/tmp/oslyric_<thread>`, which the page shows. Accepting it (`0x524f20`) turns the file name into `<name>.lrc` (`modifystringtolrcsuffix`, `0x511df8`: its last `.` on made `.lrc`) and, with a card (`/tmp/mmc_add`), `snprintf`s `cp -rf /tmp/oslyric_N "/mnt/mmc/.downpic/<name>.lrc"` (format `0x772630`) for `system()`, then reloads the lyrics (`mclLoadExLyric`); it never saved beside the song. That call (`jalr` at `0x525484`, `LYRIC_SAVE_CALL`; the format's `addiu` before it is pinned) becomes `jal ringnav_lyric_save`: for the queue's local song (the playing one first) whose file name stock turns into that `<name>.lrc`, not a CUE track's, it copies the file to `<path without extension>.lrc` beside the song through `.part`, removes `.downpic/<name>.lrc`, which the loader would show first, and leaves the command `:`, so `system()` does nothing and the reload finds the new file; otherwise stock's command. Limits: a song whose file name another song shares is saved beside the first of them in the queue; stock's "no lyrics" choice still removes only `.downpic`'s copy. Kugou counts as found whenever the transfer completes, so NetEase ran only offline. The entry of `kugou_obtainlyric` jumps to `ringnav_lyric_search` (`patch/onlinematch.c`): it copies `keyinfo` the same way, asks `https://lrclib.net/api/search?track_name=&artist_name=` (percent-encoded, `lrclib_url`) through libcurl with the bundled CA file (stock's `curl_http_request` turns certificate checks off), parses the reply with demo's cJSON and writes the first result's `syncedLyrics`, less its opening credit lines (`lrc_body`: each line before the first sung one whose text holds `:`, as NetEase's `作词 : X` or `Mix Engineer : Y`, with any blank line among them; a tag line such as `[offset:...]` ends the search and stays, and a result with no sung line is skipped), UTF-8 with a BOM as Kugou's, to the same file; without one it writes nothing, and the page finds no lyrics. When the search finds no synced lyrics and taking the accents off the title or artist changes them (`lrc_fold`: each Latin letter of U+00C0-U+017F and U+1E00-U+1EFF, Vietnamese's among them, and `ơ`/`ư` as its plain letter, strokes and ligatures too, `ł` l, `ø` o, `đ` d, `æ` ae, `ß` ss, `þ` th, combining marks dropped), it searches those once more: LRCLIB's search already matches `é` to `e` but not `ł` or `ø`, and holds some songs only under the plain names ("Hataałii" finds none of its "Hataalii" entries). A reply that isn't a results array (LRCLIB's 503 `ServerOverloaded`, below) is asked again 2 s later, 3 requests in all, as Download Album Lyrics does (`LRC_BUSY`). LRCLIB is the only source: the hook never resumes Kugou's search, whose first result was often another song's (a song LRCLIB lacks got unrelated Chinese lyrics), and returns nonzero so that `online_lyricthd` skips NetEase too, whose `api/search/pc` now answers code -462 (bind a phone) and finds no song. Limits: no duration check, so a live or remastered version's timing may come back; plain-only results are skipped, since stock's loader drops lines without a stamp; the reply is capped at 512 KB (`LRCLIB_MAX`); a sung opening line with `:` in it is cut as a credit, while credits written `作词：X` (no spaces), after a tag line or after the first sung line stay.

**Cover search.** The same page's cover match runs `online_searchcover` (`0x511f40`): it clears stock's cover list (`p_deque_onlinecover` `0xa39bec`, under `ponline_mutex`), adds `scover_default` (the track's current cover) and, when `/mnt/mmc/.downpic/<file name>.jpg` exists, that path, then starts `online_coverthd` (`0x511974`). The thread copies `keyinfo`, builds the keyword `title&artist` (`artist&album` without a title), URL-encodes it and calls `kugou_obtaincover` (`0x5112c4`), `com163_obtaincover` (`0x5117bc`) and, while the list holds under 12, `baidu_obtaincover` (`0x510f2c`), then sets `onlinematch_cover_threadflag` (`0x99f621`). Each source adds image URLs with `online_addcoverlist` (`0x510af8`: up to 0x200 bytes, a duplicate string is dropped); Kugou's parser (`0x511070`) takes only a `.jpg` URL, only while the list holds under 12, and only if a HEAD (`getDownloadFileLength`) reports under 1 MB or fails. Once the flag is set the page makes one 110 px `onlinecover_%d` image per entry, three to a row, and queues them all (`online_coverlist_init`, `batchadd_onlinecover_task`): stock's cover thread downloads each URL with `curl_http_request` (redirects followed, at most 3, 5 s in all, no certificate check) to `/tmp/online/ori%d.jpg` and sizes it to 330 px `thumb%d.jpg` (`toolsTidalSpecCover`). Picking another than the first sizes that thumbnail into `.downpic/<file name>.jpg` and `.downpic/s_<md5>.jpg` (`toolsThumbSpecCover`, under `parse_cover_mutex`, `0x525244`); picking the first removes both. The entry of `kugou_obtaincover` jumps to `ringnav_cover_search` (`patch/onlinematch.c`), which adds its own candidates first, then runs stock's Kugou search. With the album (else the title) and artist from `keyinfo` it asks MusicBrainz `ws/2/release/?query=release:"ALBUM" AND artist:"ARTIST"&fmt=json&limit=10` (Lucene-escaped and percent-encoded, `mb_url`; no artist clause without one) and, for each new `release-group` `id`, at most 4, sends a HEAD to the Cover Art Archive's `release-group/<id>/front-500` (5 s each): a redirect (307 today) names the group's front as a 500 px JPEG thumbnail on archive.org (`REDIRECT_URL`), which is added; a 404, no art, adds nothing. Then iTunes Search `search?entity=album&limit=5&term=ARTIST ALBUM` (`itunes_url`): each `artworkUrl100` with its last `100x100` made `600x600` (`itunes_art`), a JPEG. Ours stop once the list holds 10 (`COVER_FILL`), so stock's sources still fill it to 12, and fill it alone when ours find nothing. Requests use libcurl with the bundled CA file (DigiCert Global Root G2 signs MusicBrainz and iTunes, ISRG the Cover Art Archive), `Q2POD_UA` (`Q2Pod/<version> (repository URL)`, as MusicBrainz asks), 10 s to connect and 20 s in all (the HEADs 5 s), replies capped at 256 KB (`COVER_MAX`), on stock's cover thread; the images themselves are fetched by stock's thread as Kugou's. One MusicBrainz request per search keeps under its 1 request a second. Limits: a failed MusicBrainz request skips iTunes too, as a likely sign of no internet, so with Wi-Fi up but none the page waits one 10 s connect timeout more than stock; MusicBrainz answers a busy server with an error, which adds none of its covers; archive.org can take more than stock's 5 s download, which leaves that tile on the default art; the same cover from two services shows twice; only release groups' chosen fronts are offered, not each release's.

**Album lyrics.** Now Playing's ⋯ page (`playermore_page_init`, `0x528c14`, hooked by `ringnav_playermore` in `patch/onlinematch.c`) builds 8 rows, each button named by its position; its row click (`0x528a70`) `atoi`s the name into a table of kinds (`PLAYMORE_ROWS`, `0x7728a0`: 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 0, 1), so Download Cover & Lyrics, kind 4 in `get_playmore_showinfo`, is third. For a playing song on the card with an album tag (not an online queue, `MCL_TYPE` `0xf1`/`0xf2`), the hook adds **Download Album Lyrics** under it in `list_row`'s widgets, with stock's `player_onlinesearch` icon and its rows' 20 px, 253 px label; the button has no name, so stock's click never sees it. A press needs Wi-Fi (`get_wifisignal`), then copies the album's tracks off stock's records (`coverflow_album_tracks`, as Go to Album: the album's songs in disc and track order; a CUE track is left out, as it shares its file's `.lrc`) and starts one worker thread (`album_lyrics`), which outlives the page. A 500 ms timer shows "Downloading N of M" on the row while the page is open, keeps standby and auto power-off waiting (`reset_poweroptions_timer(1, 1, 0)`), then joins the worker and toasts the summary (`album_note`). A press while it runs cancels it ("Cancelling…"); USB storage (`ringnav_usbmode`) cancels and joins it first, quietly. Each track is on its own: a file gone from the card since the library's scan is passed over; one with a `.lrc` or `.LRC` beside it is left as it is; any other is searched exactly as the single search above searches LRCLIB (`lrclib`, shared: the record's title and artist, the first result with synced lyrics, less its opening credits), its lyrics written to `<name>.lrc.part` and renamed to `<name>.lrc` once whole, and then stock's `.downpic` copy for it (`lrc_name` of its file name), which would hide it, removed; a song LRCLIB lacks keeps that copy. LRCLIB answers some uncached searches with a 503 `ServerOverloaded` object (`Retry-After: 1`) instead of a results array, and the same search a moment later with results; the single search and the job both ask again 2 s later (`LJ_RETRY_US`), up to 3 requests a track (`LRC_BUSY`, `LJ_BUSY_TRIES`). Once LRCLIB has answered in the job, a failed request is retried once, 2 s later; two tracks in a row unanswered or still busy end it ("Can't reach LRCLIB", or "LRCLIB stopped answering" after some answered), as does a card that can't be written. A cancel stops a transfer at curl's next progress call (`XFERINFOFUNCTION`). However it ends, no `.part` stays and the lyrics saved before stay. Limits: one album at a time; a song without a title tag counts as not found; LRCLIB down while the internet is up reads as offline; as the single search, a live or remastered version's timing may come back.

**Lyrics from tags.** Stock's `mclLoadExLyric` (`0x5aac90`) clears the list and, unless the queue is online (`MCL_TYPE` `0xf1`/`0xf2`), loads the playing record's `<path without extension>.lrc` or `.LRC`; before them, for source types 3, 6, 12, 13, 15, 21 and 23 (the word at `0xa40000 - 0x413c`, mask `0xa0b048`), `.downpic/<name>.lrc` (`/mnt/mmc/`, or `/tmp/` without a card), and for play type `0xf0` `/tmp/hifi/`, so a stale `.downpic` copy hides the `.lrc` beside the song (`0x5a9718`). It keeps only lines whose `[` is followed by a digit. `ringnav_lyric` runs it first; when `mclGetLyricSize()` is still 0 for a local song (`REC_TYPE` 8, an absolute path), it opens the file with `taglib_file_new` and reads `LYRICS`, else `UNSYNCEDLYRICS`, with libtag_c's `taglib_property_get`, which demo does not import: its address is the one that call bound in demo's GOT (`TAGLIB_NEW_GOT`) minus `TAGLIB_FILE_NEW`, plus the pinned library's offset, and an entry still holding demo's stub reads nothing. Each line (CR, LF or CRLF), without the spaces at its ends, goes into stock's `stLyricInfo` list (`MCL_LYRICS`, under `MCL_LYRIC_LOCK`) at time `0x7fffffff`, which no track reaches: the page's timer then keeps the first line current and the view at the top, the wheel scrolls it as above, and the line `0x4d1748` shows in `label_title` stays the default (both read from the disassembly, `0x52bd3c` and `0x4d1820`). No temporary file is written. Limits: unsynced text only, so an ID3 `SYLT` frame is not read and `[mm:ss]` stamps inside a tag show as text; lines starting past the first 64 KB (`LYRIC_TAG_MAX`) are dropped; the file's tags are parsed once more on every track start without a `.lrc`, on the thread that starts it.

## Word-timed lyrics

iPod's lyrics page (`patch/lyrics.c`; [ipod.md](ipod.md#now-playing)): large lines, the playing one centred and lit word by word.

**Times.** Stock's `.lrc` parser (`0x5a9718`, inside `mclLoadExLyric`) keeps each line's whole seconds (`toolsTimeConvert`, `0x5bc28c`) and the text after its last `]`, A2 word stamps and all. Its `jalr fopen` of the file it parses (`0x5a9c5c`, `LYRIC_OPEN`; after the encoding probe and any conversion, so the file is UTF-8) becomes `jal ringnav_lrc_open` in both builds, which opens it as stock asked and keeps a copy of its first `LYRIC_TAG_MAX` bytes. After stock's load `ringnav_lyric` parses that copy as it parses a LYRICS tag (`lrc_parse`): leading `[mm:ss.xx]` stamps to the millisecond (`.x`, `.xx`, `.xxx` or `:xx`), and A2 `<mm:ss.xx>` word stamps cut from the text, kept for a line with one stamp as each word's millisecond and byte offset; a UTF-8 byte-order mark is skipped. Every line loses the spaces at its ends (ASCII's space, tab, vertical tab and form feed, no-break, ideographic and zero-width spaces, a byte-order mark; `lrc_trim`, its word stamps' offsets moved with the text), and a blank one goes: an empty line between verses, a line of spaces, a stamp alone (stock's `" "`, an instrumental break) and the empty line CR CR LF line ends leave between every two. So both lyrics pages, and Legacy's, keep an even spacing between lines; lyrics that are all blank leave stock's result. Synced lines replace stock's list (`deque_clear`, then each line at its whole seconds, under `MCL_LYRIC_LOCK`), so the list never holds word stamps and is in millisecond order; unsynced ones leave it. Every load then sends its synced lines, or none, to the page (`lyrics_publish`): their UTF-32 text, starts and word stamps, and an FNV-1a sum of each line's whole seconds and characters as stock's rows will hold them.

**Rows.** The page's refresh still rebuilds `scroll_lrc`'s labels (`0x529f0c`, `LYRIC_ROWS`), and so does `playing_page_init` (`0x52cc2c`, before `ringnav_playing` knows the page), so `ringnav_playing` takes the rows its init built too (`lyric_init`). `lyrics_rows` takes the sent table when its sum matches the labels' (`lrc_time` and text), keeps the last one when that matches, and otherwise builds one from the labels (whole seconds, no word stamps), so the page never shows another song's. It lays every line out with `widget_get_canvas`'s canvas at 34 px (`LYRIC_PX`): rows of `375 - 2 * LYRIC_X`, breaking before a space or beside a CJK character, else mid-word, up to `LYRIC_ROWS` (4, which still fit the view) of them, the rest cut. Each label's text is then emptied and it becomes the view's width and its line's rows high, 38 px apart (`LY_PITCH`), plus `LYRIC_GAP` (`LYRIC_SLOT` less a pitch, 21 px, so a line of one row is `LYRIC_SLOT`, 59 px). `lyric_init` gives `scroll_lrc` the list layout `list_view(x=0,y=LYRIC_SLOT,s=0)` (`tools/ipod.py` gives `view_lrc` and `scroll_lrc` the full 375 px), which stacks the rows after a blank slot. A line's follow target centres its row in the view (`lyric_centre`); `lyric_pad` makes the content the last row's plus the view's height, so the last line can reach the middle. `ringnav_paint` draws each row's line (`lyrics_paint`, the row found among the children), its rows centred in it, `LYRIC_X` from the left, in the system font (`canvas_set_font(canvas, NULL, px)`) drawn `LYRIC_BOLD` (2) more times a pixel apart for weight (laid out that much narrower); a row the view's edge cuts takes an alpha of 255 times the square of its share in view, and none under 8 is drawn. The playing line is drawn `LYRIC_LIT` with the clip ending at its lit width and `LYRIC_DIM` with the clip starting there; other lines `LYRIC_REST`. With no memory for the table the rows stay stock's, and the [lyrics wheel](#scrub-ipod) moves them as before, a row a tick.

**Follow.** Stock's follow (`ringnav_lyric_follow`) does nothing on the page. Every UI pass with the page in front and lit, else every `LYRIC_FOLLOW_MS` (250 ms, stock's timer's pace), `lyric_follow` finds the playing line, the first of those starting when the last started line did (lines sharing a stamp light together), and targets the offset that centres its row (line 0's before the first starts): glided to on the page, set at once off it, never while held or while a pointer is down. Wheel travel moves `LYRIC_SLOT` per tick's worth instead of `LYRIC_ROW_PX`. `lyrics_now` reads the clock on those passes, so its bounds narrow on each second's change; off them (another page, another window on top, the screen off) the follow goes by the whole second (`lyrics_sec`, as stock's), which lets the bounds go, so the clock starts again, wide, when the page is back in front. While `playing_page` is on top and lit, the playing line's rows repaint when its lit width changes, at most every `LYRIC_PAINT_MS` (66 ms, about 15 a second; a word-timed line's width moves nearly every pass, and the idle loop passes every 25 ms), and the last line's when another line starts. Limit: the lit sweep moves in steps of up to `LYRIC_PAINT_MS` of the song, a few pixels at a fast word; the value is a guess, not tuned on the device.

**Clock.** `player_playtime_and_length` reports whole seconds, which stock's status thread (`mclSyncPlayerStatus`, `0x5a91c4`) asks hciplayer for every 200 ms (`{media-info\get-music-info}`, its fourth field). `lyrics_now` keeps the offset from `time_now_ms` to the position as an interval: the first second seen, or one after a jump (a seek, another song), puts it anywhere in that second plus a poll (`LYRIC_POLL_MS`, 250); the next second's arrival while playing puts the position at most a poll past that second, and the two intersect, or restart from that change when they disagree. Paused (`mclGetPlayStatus()` not 2) it holds; on Play it goes on from where it stopped until the next second replaces that guess. It never runs more than a poll past its reported second.

**Lit width.** A line without word stamps is lit whole from its start (`ly_lit`). One with them is lit through them, from stamp to stamp, each at its character's distance along the line's rows laid end to end; before the first, from the line's start; past the last, at `LYRIC_UNIT_MS` a letter (90 ms; three for a CJK character; `LYRIC_MIN_MS`, 800, at least), done by the next line's start. Limits: the clock knows the position to about a poll, 0.1 s either way, and doesn't allow for the output's latency (Bluetooth's); a line longer than four rows is cut.

## Pull-to-search

Pull-to-search starts from the existing touch hook only on a real pointer-down (`0x100`; the stock window manager registers `on_wm_tsdown_before_fun` for that type), while the separate move-before callback (`0x102`) tail-calls the same entry point. It applies to Local Songs (`localmusic_page`) in iPod only, and to Internet Radio (`radio_page`) in both: Folder view has no working stock search, so it is excluded. It checks the screen coordinates, list bounds and initial scroll offset, then uses page-owned move/up-before-children callbacks (`0x102` / `0x104`). A downward drag of at least `PULL_CLAIM_PX` (8), greater than its horizontal travel, claims the pointer. The native abort event (`0x107`) clears pressed descendants; a widget-owned suppression flag also rejects late row clicks until the next touch. A floating, non-interactive label overlays the page without changing the list or its indices. Release at `PULL_SEARCH_PX` (48) invokes the stock Local Songs search callback `0x5241c4`, which opens `dialog/search_dialog` without replacing the browsing page; on Internet Radio it calls `radio_search` (radio.c) instead. The stock search dialogs quit on Return even while their edit's keyboard is open, which destroys the edit under the keyboard and freezes demo; `search_key` (navigation.c's `ringnav`) reads the editing target from the input method (`IM_WIDGET`, `+0x04`, pinned against stock's `input_method_request`), since the keyboard is a separate top window and its focus chain does not contain the search edit. It closes the keyboard on the first Return (`widget_set_focused(search_edit, 0)` plus `input_method_request`, since the searchbox dialogs' edit does not close on blur), and the next Return runs stock's quit with the keyboard already shut. Moving back below the threshold cancels activation. Page/surface destruction, backgrounding, close and input interruption clear the gesture. The stock quick-settings start region (`y < 31`) is excluded.

`patch/ipod.json` records the added private prologue and native event ABI instructions alongside the compact sites and assets. The builder checks them against the pinned stock executable and checks the byte sizes of all referenced global flags/values.

## Manual skip

Manual Next and Previous run `player_change_music` (`0x51503c`), not `mclNextSong`. At queue index `n-1` (or `0` for Previous) it loads the next (previous) folder itself (class 1 with Folder Skip on), or in List Play wraps to the other end with `mclSetPlayPos`. In List Play, `ringnav_next` and `ringnav_change` stop a library queue (`0xfxxx`) at its last track instead (`list_end`), so the end of an album stops on a manual Next as it does at a natural end; `ringnav_change` returns 0, since its caller toasts -1 (no storage) and -5. In Repeat All with Folder Skip off, every branch calls `mclNextSong(0)`/`mclPrevSong`. While advanced playback is active, `ringnav_change` runs stock with `MCL_MODE` 3 and Folder Skip 0, then restores both, so the hooked `ringnav_next`/`ringnav_prev` choose the track and stock's refresh tail (tags, notifications, Bluetooth headset info) still runs. It writes `MCL_MODE` directly, since `mclSetPlayMode` would end advanced playback. `ringnav_mode` (the hook on `mclSetPlayMode`) ends advanced play through `playback_clear` for a user's stock mode choice, except stock Shuffle (2) while Shuffle Albums/Folders is on, which keeps the groups. `ringnav_prev` clears the `seen` flag of the track it leaves, so the next Next returns to it instead of skipping it.
