Shuffles draw from `rand_below` (`playback.c`), an xorshift32 seeded once from `toolsRandnum` and `time_now_ms`, because stock's generator alone repeated itself between shuffles started close together (the hold menu lists Next/Previous Album, first, only while `playback_option(0) >= 3`). `player_initconfig` (`0x513374`) also calls it, re-applying the saved mode whenever hciplayer connects (`player_reconfig`, set by `mclParseCommand` on accepting its socket), after a boot resume too; that is no mode choice, so `ringnav_mode` passes it straight to stock, told apart by its return address (`INITCONFIG_MODE_RET`, checked by the build). Earlier at boot, `config_init` calls `config_playmode` (`0x4f999c`) before Home restores the queue. That call goes through `ringnav_bootmode`, which marks it as restoration so `ringnav_mode` applies the setting without deleting the snapshot; explicit mode choices still clear advanced playback.

Memory Playback's `ringnav_memory` restores the saved snapshot using `getAllMusic(3)`, which avoids the pinyin title sort. A temporary index (8 bytes per library row) is sorted by file path and CUE start/end, then binary-searched for each saved occurrence. Equal identities retain the query's first row. The snapshot still controls queue order, history and playback position; missing files are skipped and unindexed files retain the stock directory fallback. Index allocation failure frees the temporary state and falls back to stock resume without rewriting the snapshot. `test/playback.py` checks those cases and bounds path comparisons for a 256-track restore so a full-library search per track cannot return unnoticed.

## Bluetooth boot reconnect

Stock `platform_init` starts `btctl_create_daemon_thread` (`0x512598`) before `config_bt_init` calls `openBluetooth`. Registering callbacks at the radio's GPIO write can still miss connections discovered by that thread during startup. `headset_bt_init` initializes the stock Bluetooth lists and installs the stock prompt and A2DP callbacks and `headset_bt_status` (below) before starting the control thread, preserving its thread pointer and return value. The later `initBluetoothParam` call in `config_bt_init` is removed so it cannot erase links discovered during startup. Stock's later registration remains for Bluetooth toggles, with `BT_STATUS` registering `headset_bt_status` there too. `test/patch.py` checks the patched call site, callback order and successful/failed thread-start forwarding.

A headset that connects to the Q2 while stock's link thread waits for another device was lost until Bluetooth was toggled. At boot and at Bluetooth on, `autoLinkBluetooth` (`0x4f17a0`) appends the last device (`BTLINKMAC`) and starts the link thread (`0x4f2068`), which waits up to 15 s for it. Stock's device-status callback (`0x4f1a50`) ignores a Connected event (3) from a device not in the link list while `bt_connectstatus` is set, and BlueZ sends that event once. If the incoming event arrived first, auto-link appended its target after it, and `checkBluetoothStatus` judges only the last item: the absent target's failure ran `clearBluetoothStatus`, which wiped the incoming link too. `BT_AUTOLINK` turns `autoLinkBluetooth`'s `bal checkPairDev` into `jal headset_bt_autolink`, which also skips while a listed device is connected (status 3). `headset_bt_status` wraps the stock callback; `headset_bt_init` registers it, and `BT_STATUS` turns `openBluetooth`'s registration into `jal headset_status_register`. A Connected event from a paired, unlisted device is adopted only while the thread waits (`bt_connectstatus` 1, not 2 once it ended or 3 while `checkBluetoothStatus` judges) for the saved device (its target, the last item, equals `BTLINKMAC` at `0xa38168`) and the thread has taken that target and is connecting it (status 5: not 1, before the thread reads it, nor 3, already connected). The device is appended with status 3 and the target set to 4, so the wait ends and stock's success path keeps the incoming device and switches the output. Everything else goes to stock. While the thread waits, the Bluetooth page ignores taps (`0x4bf314`) and stock adds no items, so the wrapper is the only writer besides the thread. Limits: no global holds the thread's mode, so a connect or pairing the user starts to the saved device from the Bluetooth page is treated like auto-link (a pairing still switches only when its 15 s wait ends), while one to another device still loses an incoming link as stock does. The target's BlueZ Connect is still pending, so if it powers on within its page timeout both may link; that includes the target connecting between the wrapper's check and its update, which then marks it 4. An incoming link whose event arrives after the thread ended but before `checkBluetoothStatus` ran is dropped as in stock. `test/patch.py` checks both patch sites and the wrapper's adopt and pass-through cases.

## Bluetooth LDAC quality

The Quality page's rows are LDAC HQ, LDAC Standard, LDAC Connection (auto), aptX HD, aptX, AAC, SBC HQ and SBC (`g_btcoding`, `[SYSSET] BTCODING`). `switchBtPriority` (`0x4f0314`) sends `btctl_set_ldac_quality` over D-Bus to `bluealsa` (`SetLDACQuality`). bluealsa maps 0x15, 0x16 and 0x17 to the encoder's eqmid HQ, SQ and MQ, and maps any other value to adaptive bit rate. Stock sends 0x16 (SQ, 660 kbps, or 606 at 44.1 kHz) for both HQ and Standard. `LDAC_HQ` rewrites the jump-table dispatch to keep `0x15 + row` in the frame's free `0x14($sp)` slot and loads it in place of the constant. HQ then sends 0x15 (990 / 909 kbps), Standard 0x16, and auto still 0x18. Turning Bluetooth on applies the saved row again, and Rockbox plays through the same daemon.

Auto already uses Sony's native ABR controller, including the intermediate 492 and 396 kbps rates (48/96 kHz), alongside 990, 660 and 330. These are discrete encoder frame sizes, not arbitrary 1 kbps increments. The shipped `libldacBT.so.2.0.19` supports them through `ldacBT_alter_eqmid_priority`; `libldacBT_ABR.so.2.0.0` selects them from queue congestion and raises quality after recovery. Bluealsa's `0x416388` maps Auto to an initial SQ encoder with ABR enabled; its encode loop calls `ldac_ABR_Proc` at `0x41ab00` with the queued packet count, using a 40 ms interval and thresholds 12/8/4. The HQ fix changes the fixed HQ row only; Auto could already recover to HQ. `test/ldac.py` executes the shipped encoder's five rates, the daemon's mode mapping and the ABR controller's congestion/recovery paths. No custom rate controller is added. This does not establish a cause or fix for sustained distortion requiring reconnect, or USB-dongle heat; those need device reproduction.

## Bluetooth AAC

When a headset opens the A2DP stream itself (AirPods taken out of the case), it picks AAC at 44.1 kHz. Stock `bluealsa` is still fed 48 kHz audio and drops about 8% of the AAC frames, so the audio is choppy until Bluetooth is toggled; a connection the Q2 opens picks 48 kHz. The build clears the 44.1 kHz bit in `bluealsa`'s AAC source capability, `c0 01 84 84 e2 00` at file offset `0x317b7` (object type, sampling frequencies, channels, VBR and bitrate). Octet 1 holds 8 to 44.1 kHz with 44.1 kHz in bit 0, so `0x317b8` goes from `01` to `00` and headsets pick 48 kHz, which every AAC sink must support. `ZIP_SHA` pins the stock binary, and `test/build.py` audits the allowed byte changes and checks the manifest's `bluealsa_sha256`.

Manual skips also call ALSA DROP. Stock acknowledges it after sending signal 6, but the encoder flushes PCM later (`0x4147ac`), potentially discarding the next track’s first samples. The patched DROP (`0x40cb50`) flushes the nonblocking FIFO under the PCM fd mutex before sending that signal and acknowledging the request. The encoder’s shared DROP handler discards each audited caller’s partial PCM input, restores full input capacity, and resets its pacing clock through the existing OPEN/RESUME path instead of flushing again. Natural gapless transitions do not issue DROP. `test/bluealsa.py` executes both MIPS paths with a delayed signal and empty, normal and oversized old queues; stock loses new audio and the patched binary preserves it.

A manual skip also left the A2DP stream without packets. On a Next mid-song, hciplayer either sends Drain (the PCM still buffered, about 440 ms, drains, then Drain's 100 ms poll times out) or, when demo pauses it first, PAUSE, which stock answers by polling again without a timeout. About 300 ms later it sends DROP, closes the PCM and opens the next one. Traced on a Q2 (stock 1.5.4, AAC), no packet left for 404 ms. The headset's buffer ran dry, and OPEN/RESUME restarted the encoder's pacing (asrsync) from zero, so the new song played on with the buffer near-empty and crackled. A phone keeps its stream running through a skip. `tools/bt_silence.S`, linked at `BT_SILENCE` in bluealsa's payload, patches the shared `io_poll_and_read_pcm` (`0x4145d0`), which every encoder loop calls (all six callers are A2DP encoders). The build points the signal jump table's PAUSE entry (`0x42d09c`) at Drain's case (`0x4147a0`, a 100 ms poll). When that timeout ends and real PCM was read within `BT_SILENCE_SAMPLES` (192000 interleaved samples: 2 s at 48 kHz stereo, 1 s at 96 kHz), the function still wakes Drain's waiter but returns the requested samples as zeros, and from then on it polls without waiting. The encoder's own pacing sets the rate and catches up the 100 ms in a burst that refills the headset's buffer. The next real PCM ends the fill and re-arms the budget; once the budget is spent it behaves as stock. Without a Drain, a poll still blocks as stock, so a stall mid-song is caught up as before. Traced again, a skip leaves 110–131 ms without packets, which is the 100 ms wait. The small click left at a skip is heard with stock as well; the PCM on either side of the skip had no step in it. ponytail: one budget and fill flag serve every encoder thread, which is fine for one headset at a time. A second stream, or a stream torn down while filling, shares or inherits up to the budget's silence. A pause or stop streams up to the budget's silence before the link goes quiet. Unchecked on LDAC Auto: whether the catch-up burst makes its ABR step down a rate at each skip. `test/bluealsa.py` runs the patched function: stock returning nothing at Drain's timeout and polling unbounded after PAUSE, PAUSE's 100 ms poll, the fill for S16 and S32, Drain's wake, unpaced polls and pacing init, a closed PCM, the budget running out and re-arming, and stock behaviour without a budget.

## Bluetooth keep-alive

`hciplayer` closes its ALSA PCM between files, and stock starts `bluealsa` (`/usr/bin/bluealsa2`) without `--keep-alive`, so the A2DP transport is released and every song begins with a fresh AVDTP start. True-wireless buds start the primary bud (often the right) first and the other joins a moment later, so one ear began early on every track. The build adds `--keep-alive=5` to that launcher (`BT_KEEPALIVE`): the transport stays open 5 s after the PCM closes, which covers a track change. ponytail: inferred from bluealsa's option and the report, not traced on a Q2; the headset stays in streaming state up to 5 s after a stop, and a pause longer than that still restarts the stream.

## Clock

The timezone picker keeps stock's saved indices (`g_time_zone`, 0-26) and appends the missing offsets and a few populous zones. `TIMEZONES` in `tools/build.py` holds every index's label and `TZ`. `toolsSetTimeZone` (`0x5bc4f4`) is only `setenv("TZ", name, 1)`, and `ringnav_timezone` replaces stock's mapping (`select_timezone`, `0x4f93b0`) for every index, so glibc resolves an IANA name under `/usr/share/zoneinfo` or, failing that, reads a POSIX rule. The rootfs tzdata is older than 2022, so Mexico City (`CST6`), Tehran (`<+0330>-3:30`) and Fiji (`<+12>-12`) take POSIX rules without the daylight saving they have since dropped. Stock's GMT-12 row set Pacific/Kwajalein, which is +12, so it is `<-12>12` now. Its +6 Almaty row is Asia/Dhaka, since Almaty is +5 from 2024 and the rootfs still says +6. `tools/build.py` writes the labels as literal text (no `strings/*.bin` key, so every language shows them as written) into the existing label table at `0x76b5d0`, compacted to 32-byte slots, and extends the row counts and boot validation. The page's row builder (`0x4bcd1c`, `(item, i)`; row 0 is the switch) labels, names (`atoi` in the click handler, less one, is the saved index) and ticks a row by the index it is given. As row 1 is built, `ringnav_timezone_row` sets each zone's `TZ` in turn and reads its offset from UTC now (`localtime_r`'s `tm_gmtoff`), then sets the saved zone again; row i is handed the i-th zone west to east by that offset (ties in `TIMEZONE_ORDER`: standard offset, then name), plus one, and its label is rewritten as that offset and the city (`TIMEZONE_CITIES`), so New York reads GMT-4 in summer and GMT-5 in winter, as its clocks do. Labelled by standard offset, a New Yorker in summer took GMT-4 Halifax, an hour ahead. ponytail: another thread's `localtime` while row 1 builds may read one of the other zones, as `TZ` is process-wide. Stock's `set_hscroll_label_attribute` scrolls every too-long label once as the page opens; the hook makes the row's label focus-only, so one that does not fit keeps its ellipsis. The labels are sized to fit the 246 px label at 24 px. The table's labels state the standard offset; the rows show the one in effect. ponytail: the rules are fixed in the build and the rootfs tzdata stays as it is, so a zone that changes its rules later needs a new row or rule here.

Stock loses the time zone across a power-off. Setting the time, by hand or from the network (`toolsSetRtcTime`, `0x5bc760`), runs `date -s` and then `hwclock -w`. BusyBox 1.31.1's `hwclock` writes local time unless `/var/lib/hwclock/adjtime` says `UTC`, and the rootfs has no such file, so the RTC gets the wall time of the `TZ` the player exported (`toolsSetTimeZone`). At boot the kernel (`hctosys`) reads the RTC as UTC and the player adds the time zone again, so the clock comes back off by the zone's UTC offset until the next network sync. The build changes the literal at `0x777928`, `hwclock -w` and two NULs, to `hwclock -wu`, so the RTC holds UTC as the kernel expects. An RTC last written by stock still holds local time, so the time has to be set once after updating.

The status bar, Auto Light Mode and timezone picker use `localtime_r` with their own `struct tm` buffers. Stock's background time conversions can overwrite `localtime`'s shared buffer while the UI reads it, briefly displaying another time. The status bar's clock and Auto Light Mode read the time once a minute: `clock_show` writes the label, applies Auto's hour and sets a timer for the next minute, and runs at once for a new zone (`ringnav_timezone`), a time set by hand (`time_click`) and a wake, and on the pass after the bar first paints or paints with a new 24-hour setting. Before, every paint of the bar or the top window read the time, and Auto Light Mode's check read it on every UI pass (125 a second). ponytail: a network time sync while lit shows at the next minute. The payload resolves demo's imported `tzset` once (it writes no shared buffer), then calls the pinned libc's `localtime_r` at its checked offset from it. Timezone changes explicitly call `tzset`, since `localtime_r` does not reload `TZ` itself.

## Lock screen

Stock's lock window (`saverscreen_page.bin`, 375x320, fullscreen, so no status bar) shows `g_screensaver_picture` (`SYSSET SCREENSAVERPATH`): the asset `screensaver_default`, else the file if `access()` finds it, else the name as an asset again. The picker on **System settings → Display → Lock screen** (`screensaver_page`) lists `p_deque_screensaverlist`, a libcstl `char*` deque that `get_screensaver_image` fills: up to five card images from `lockscreenimage` (`toolsGetScreenSaverPic`, 375x320, 1 MB), then the default pushed in front. It also resets a picture that is neither the default nor a file. Its list builder (`0x4c9134`, rebuilt when the page's switch turns) makes Time show and About, then, with the switch on, the 3-column grid of 110x94 `image_%d` thumbnails: the default from its asset, the rest by `file://`.

**Wallpapers** (`patch/lockscreen.c`). Six built-ins are drawn by the payload, since the Stock rootfs has about 25 KB to spare: Aurora, Sunrise, Graphite, Tide, Ember and Black. `wall_render` evaluates each one's colour field (inverse-distance mesh gradients, waves, glows) every 5 pixels, interpolates bilinearly and adds a 4x4 ordered dither so gradients don't band; Graphite adds a brushed per-row grain. Their names (`q2pod:aurora` … `q2pod:black`) are no files. `lockscreen_images` lists them after the default and before the card images: demo has no `deque_pop_front`, so the card images come off the back and go on again, and a rebuilt list never doubles them. When stock reset a built-in, it is put back and saved. `lockscreen_rows` clears those thumbnails' `file://` names, and `lockscreen_init` clears the lock window's picture name, so nothing is looked up on each paint. `lockscreen_paint`, from `ringnav_paint` after stock painted the widget, draws the wallpaper over it: 375x320 rendered once per picture and size and kept between lock windows (Black needs none, the window is black), thumbnails on first sight. A thumbnail's bitmap goes with its widget (`EVT_DESTROY`), and both widget pointers are forgotten then, so a stale pointer never matches.

**Cover.** The lock window's 20 ms timer reloads `img_playcover` (`0x52d844`) once `g_playcover_finishflag` says a new track's cover is parsed, by the same `file://` name, and stock never invalidates it: the old art stayed until something else repainted there (a scrolling title's edge, a tap). `lockscreen_cover` invalidates it after stock, outside any paint.

**Wake (1.4.4 only; removed in 1.4.5).** With the lock screen on (`g_screensaver_mode`) and no lock window open (`g_lockscreen_pageflag`), stock's centre release from dark (`on_wm_keyup_fun`) and its Standby resume (`device_standby`) call `enable_fb` (`FBIOBLANK` unblank, which lights the panel) and then `navigator_to("saverscreen_page")`. The panel showed the page left at screen-off, and the lock window stayed unpainted: `ringnav_wm_paint` paints nothing while `g_backlight_status` is clear, and only the lock window's 20 ms timer sets it, on its second tick (a countdown of 2 from `saverscreen_page_init`), after a 50 ms `usleep` and before `screen_action(1)`. With the screen off a UI loop pass takes 100 or 250 ms (Battery), so those ticks came late. The build turns both `bal enable_fb` into `nop` (`LOCK_WAKE`), and the timer's `jalr usleep` (`0x52da54`) into `jal lockscreen_light` (`LOCK_LIGHT`), reached only when the countdown ends with the screen still dark. It sets `g_backlight_status` (stock sets it right after), paints the whole screen, unblanks with `enable_fb` and then sleeps stock's 50 ms. The panel stays blank until the lock screen is drawn, and stock's backlight follows. Without the lock screen, wake is unchanged. ponytail: the panel lights two dark loop passes after the press (about 0.2-0.5 s). If the lock window never opens, the panel stays blank until a stock `awake_screen` (a held centre press, for one) unblanks it.

**Clock.** The Lock screen page's **Clock: Standard / Large** row (`Q2POD LOCKCLOCK`, default Standard) follows stock's Time show, 78 high as stock's rows. Large re-lays out `view_timeinfo` when the lock window opens: the date (22 px) centred on a top line at y 14 with the battery percent at its right end when the Battery setting shows a percentage (the icon hidden; Battery: Icon hides both), and the time (116 px) centred across the screen under it, all above the now-playing card (y 190). Time show off still hides it all. `clock_time` is a `digit_clock`; in 12-hour mode stock sets its format to `HH:TT` (`0x52d840`), which `tk_date_time_format_impl` writes as the padded hour, a colon and AM/PM ("05:PM"). The build rewrites that literal, in its 8-byte slot after the 24-hour `hh:mm`, to `H:mm` (`LOCK_CLOCK_12H`): the hour 1-12 unpadded and the minutes ("5:07"), as the status bar's clock, in both builds.

Checks in `test/patch.py`: the list's order and the kept built-in, the row's place, text and saved value, the thumbnails, and both clock layouts. `wall_render` is plain C, so it also builds on the host (`-DPEQ_HOST`).

## Parametric EQ

**Stock presets.** Stock's preset slots are `reset_user_eq`'s (`0x4f91a4`) constant 12 × 10 float table, modes 1-3 the zeroed custom slots and 4-11 Pop, Rock, Dance, Blues, Metal, Vocal (the `eq_scene` image), Classical and Jazz in its `eq_*` image order, in dB over the same ten bands (`set_equalizer_value` stores them plus 6). `patch/peq_ui.c` keeps modes 4-11 as `stock_gains`; picking one loads `peq_default` with those gains, bands with a gain on, and Auto preamp into the editor as a saved preset does. Nothing is written until Apply.

The PEQ adds two demo hooks: `playset_equalizer_page_init` (`0x4b642c`) builds the editor from `patch/peq_ui.c` (ten band rows, plus a spare past the last used band, up to 30), and `set_equalizer_value` (`0x4f9230`) keeps the stock equalizer filter instantiated, then sets `g_equalizer_flag` to the PEQ on/off state so the status-bar EQ icon (`systembar_showface`) follows it. The editor's ON/OFF writes the stock switch's `write_int_config(flag, "PLAYSET", "EQFLAG")`, which boot reads before instantiating the filter, so the state survives a restart. Frequency and Q open a value menu with an AWTK `edit` created at run time over the title (page y 2-28, as the search fields'), keyed and styled as the stock playlist dialogs' (`kb_default_t9`, `text`, `#2B2B2B` rounded), but with action text `done`. T9's window starts at page y 30, so the keyboard neither covers the edit nor moves the page; as the list's first row (page y 52-92) it sat under T9, showed none of the typing and lost the keys (the cause of the loss inferred, not traced: stock `edit_on_event` drops `EVT_IM_COMMIT` on an edit without its focused flag). Row 0 names the field and, like Centre on it, focuses the edit and asks for the keyboard; on a Frequency or Q edit still focused after Done, which sends no `EVT_FOCUS`, it starts the number-page timer below itself. The rename edit is placed the same way. Return with the keyboard up cancels either edit (`search_key`, see [Internet Radio](#internet-radio)): an empty entry restores the value or name. The 123 page is set on the input method's own keyboard (`IM_KEYBOARD`, `+0x0c`, while `IM_WIDGET` is the edit), not a window looked up by name, which could be the previous keyboard still closing. The edit's action text is `done`: stock `edit_on_event` acts on the keyboard's OK (`EVT_IM_ACTION`) only for `done` (close the keyboard, then `EVT_VALUE_CHANGED`) or `next`, and the dialogs' `OK` relies on their own handlers, so with `OK` the value only reported on blur; its `EVT_VALUE_CHANGED` applies the text through the preset parser's number reader, clamped like Raise/Lower, and its `EVT_FOCUS` (`0x10e`) starts a 16 ms timer that switches that keyboard's `panel` pages to `symnum`, the number keys, trying up to 20 times since the keyboard can open a moment after the focus, and selects the edit's value (`edit_set_select`), so the first key replaces it. The edit uses text input so stock's numeric validation and auto-fix cannot rewrite unfinished decimals or zero digits; `peq_number` validates the completed text and the editor clamps it to Q 0.1–10 or Frequency 20–20,000 Hz. Auto preamp is minus the largest combined response peak of either channel at the rate actually playing (`peq_auto`, `patch/peq.c`): the player works it out from the coefficients it has just compiled for the negotiated rate (`prepare`, from `peq_reset` at each track's or rate's negotiation and `peq_update` for an applied edit), on the playback loop, never in the PCM callback, and plays it through the usual 20 ms ramp. The sweep is 1024 log-spaced points from 1 Hz to Nyquist, plus DC, Nyquist and the band centres; it never boosts (a flat or cut-only preset gets 0 dB) and is capped at −60 dB. It is one gain for every channel, applied with balance as a set preamp is, so the filters and the left/right balance are unchanged; loudness can differ between tracks at different rates (the reported 26-band preset: −12.35 dB at 32 kHz, −9.25 at 44.1, −8.81 at 48, −7.87 at 96). It is a sampled steady-state peak, not a limiter: an unsampled narrow peak (0.003 dB on that preset at 44.1 kHz) and transient or intersample overshoot can still need more. `peq_preset.automatic` records which preamp it is: 1 Auto (the preamp picker's Auto row, a stock preset), 2 a value picked from the preamp list or an import's own `Preamp:` (`peq_parse`; an import without one is 1, Auto, so its boosts can't clip), 0 never picked (a preset or state saved before 1.3.6, or imported before 1.4.4), which the editor and PEQ playback (`eq_effective`) take as Auto: a preamp picked before 1.3.6, or an older import's, must be picked again (or the file imported again). A pick on the Preamp picker is also written to the saved preset the draft was loaded from (`remember_preamp`: its `automatic` and `preamp` only, found by the loaded name in `/mnt/data/peq-presets`), so an import switched to Auto stays Auto when loaded again; the card's `.txt` is only ever read. Keyed on the name, a stock preset named like a saved one would write to that file. A picked preamp plays exactly as set at every rate, even when it equals an Auto figure. On Auto the saved `preamp` is the 48 kHz cut, a fallback the player does not use, so a rate change rewrites nothing and opening the page offers nothing to apply, whatever number an older version saved. The Preamp row and its picker's Auto row show the draft's cuts at two reference rates, `Auto (44.1: -9.2 / 48: -8.8)` (`autos`, cached until a band changes), one decimal; other rates play their own. The row's value is 16 px there, as two double-digit cuts at 20 px overrun the iPod's 280 px column beside the caption and chevron (16 px leaves 6 px). A band edit, including a gain picked from the wheel list, recomputes it only on Auto, so a picked preamp stays put. The editor handles Return (key 170) itself, since it replaces the page init that registered `on_common_keyup`. On the main and band screens a `PEQ_GRAPH_H` view under the title shows the draft's response (`peq_response`, the same `|H|^2` as Auto preamp, at 48 kHz, one point per column, 20 Hz-20 kHz, ±`PEQ_GRAPH_DB`), drawn by `peq_paint` from the paint hook: a grey grid with 100, 1k and 10k labels, a 2-pixel vgcanvas line in the accent's light tone (white in Stock) with a fill that fades toward 0 dB, the other channel's line dimmer when left and right differ, a ring per band in use, the edited band's larger and filled; grey with no fill when PEQ is off. Rows show the caption on the left and the value right-aligned in grey, left of stock's `list_into` chevron (x 302) on rows that open a screen or picker (`INTO`: Balance, Preamp, Presets, the bands, Frequency, Gain, Q and the Presets menu's five); **Enabled** and a band's **Band** row are switches (`list_toggle`, navigation.c), Channels and Type cycle in place; off bands and display-only rows are grey. Band captions use 16 px, leaving room for the 20 px gain and chevron even with a channel-specific shelf at 20000 Hz. The **Presets** row's value names the preset loaded from there, and keeps the applied one's name until the active preset changes. **Rename a preset** lists the saved presets; a pick opens the name in an edit (letters, as the value menu's edit otherwise), whose `EVT_VALUE_CHANGED` renames the file (`rename_preset`): spaces and dots trimmed from the end, as FAT drops them; refused when empty, starting with a dot, holding `/ \ : * ? " < > |`, or naming another preset (`access`, a case-only change allowed); the editor's and the applied preset's names follow it. The **Reset to defaults** row puts `peq_default`'s ten bands, all off, and 0 dB preamp and balance into the draft, keeps Advanced's On/Off and clears the loaded preset's name; Apply changes activates it. In `/usr/bin/hciplayer` (modified MPlayer 1.3.0, pinned by `PLAYER_SHA` in `tools/peq.py`) the equalizer descriptor's open pointer at `0x893e0c` is redirected to `patch/peq_player.c`, loaded through the player's unused `PT_NULL` header at `0xe10000`. Stock skips the equalizer above 48 kHz (`slt` against 48001) in two places: the EQ-on command (`beqz` at `0x42ced0`, to the `===setequalizer off===` log) and the per-stream insert (`bnez` at `0x42fd84`, around the add-equalizer call `0x4291ac`). The build makes the first a nop and the second unconditional (`EQ_RATE_GATES` in `tools/peq.py`, pinned), so the filter runs at every rate; `peq_compile` accepts 8-384 kHz. DSD is the exception: it reaches the chain as integer PCM, possibly DoP, whose bits must arrive unchanged, so config accepts the format as it comes and `play` leaves every block alone when the playing codec (`mpctx`, a pointer at `0xaf9e30`, `+0x2c` `sh_audio`, `+4` `codec`) has driver `dsdiff` (`+0x3e0`; the vendor's decoder for dsf, dff and SACD iso) or a name starting `ffdsd` (`+0x3d0`); so do rates outside 8-384 kHz and unknown formats. It never detaches (2): the per-stream insert (`0x4291ac`) adds it with `af_add` (`0x44d098`), which returns the new filter even when its reinit (`0x44c1c4`) detached and freed it, and the insert then sends the gain set to the freed instance. The player re-adds the filter at each file load (`0x4224cc` sets the insert state back to pending), so the next PCM track is equalized again. The player reads what plays from the [EQ state](#3eq) in `/mnt/data/peq-active` on the playback loop, never in the PCM callback; `patch/peq.c` holds the shared parser, storage and biquad DSP.

**Float headroom.** `peq_process` preserves samples above ±1 for downstream software volume and mixing; a picked preamp and balance are applied exactly. Non-finite input is still zeroed while EQ runs, and steady bypass stays bit-exact. The stock float volume path multiplies before clamping (`0x45c104`); the signed PCM converters saturate at final conversion (16-bit `0x451c14`, 24-bit `0x4528b0`, 32-bit `0x452474`). With hardware volume or full software volume, a preset with insufficient headroom can still clip there: lowering the DAC's analog volume cannot recover those peaks. No limiter is added: Auto is a fixed gain for the stream's rate.

A band's `enabled` is 0 off, 1 both channels, 2 left only, 3 right only (stream channels 0 and 1); `balance` (dB) attenuates the left above 0 and the right below, so it never adds clipping. APO `Channel:` sections set the scope of the filters after them, and per-side preamps become the louder side's common preamp plus `balance`. Out-of-range OFF filters, the blank slots some exporters write as `Fc 0 Hz … Q 0`, are skipped. Presets are `Q2PEQ02`; ten-band `Q2PEQ01` files load with `balance` 0 and default bands 11-30, and `Q2PEQ02` files from before 1.3.6, 8 bytes shorter (`PEQ_PRESET_V2`, no `automatic`), with `automatic` 0. Firmware before 1.3.6 rejects the longer files, so a downgrade loses saved presets and the EQ state. The DSP runs bands only up to the last one in use, or still draining memory carried from the previous preset.

## 3EQ

3EQ is the **Simple** tab of stock's **Audio settings → EQ** page, which `peq_page_init` builds with two tabs (`ui.tab`), opening on the tab it was last on (Simple after a boot, as `.scratch` is zeroed); **Advanced** is the PEQ editor above. On Simple and on Advanced's main screen the 48 px title label is the tab bar (`ui.bar`, drawn by `tabs_paint`): the open tab white over an accent line, the other grey. A message takes the bar until the next action. A press on the bar picks a tab by its half (`tab_to`, rendered from a timer, never inside the press). Switching tabs changes nothing that plays. Simple is one view that `e3_paint` draws from `ringnav_paint`: the switch, **Presets** and Reset (**R**, a small box; `e3_button`) under the bar, then three sliders over a 0 dB line. `eq_key`, from `ringnav` after `xfade_key`, takes Simple's wheel and Centre: the wheel moves through the bar, switch, Presets, R, Low, Mid and High; Centre (`center_press`, so a double press still turns the screen off) switches tab, flips the switch, opens Presets, resets or takes a slider, whose gain the wheel then steps by 0.5 dB until Centre or Return; the page's key-up handles Return. On Advanced's main screen the list keeps the wheel, except a turn up from its first row (or with none selected), which `eq_key` takes to put the bar in focus (`ui.tabs`, the list's `_ringnav_index` -1); a turn down returns to row 0 and Centre opens Simple. A press on Simple's view (`EVT_POINTER_DOWN`) picks a control; the page's move and up handlers (`EVT_POINTER_MOVE_BEFORE`, `EVT_POINTER_UP_BEFORE`, as pull-to-search's) drag a slider in 0.5 dB steps.

**Presets.** Simple's Presets (`e3_presets`) opens Advanced's Stock presets screen (`STOCKS`) with `ui.from_simple` set; Return there goes straight back. When `action` lands back on Advanced's main screen, it returns to Simple with Presets focused: on Return, with nothing changed, or on a pick (`load_draft`), which `apply(1)` saves as the playing PEQ (3EQ off, `hold` cleared) under its name. Like a load in Advanced, a pick replaces unapplied Advanced edits. While PEQ plays (`ui.draft.bypass` clear) the switch shows it on, lit, under `ui.applied` ("Advanced" when unnamed), cut to 12 bytes on a character boundary (16 px past 9), and the sliders are dimmed; the switch then turns the EQ off with no `hold`, since hold plays 3EQ's preamp. Taking a slider while PEQ plays, by Centre or touch, turns 3EQ on first (`e3_switch(1)`); PEQ's preset stays saved. Leaving the page from that screen reopens it on Simple.

**Filters.** `eq3_bands` gives the shared engine three bands, Q 0.707: a 155 Hz low shelf, a peak at 900 Hz for a cut or 1,750 Hz for a boost (0 dB is an identity band), and a 5 kHz high shelf, each -6 to +6 dB. The filter types and rough frequencies follow [Listener's measurements of Apple's AirPods EQ](https://headphones.com/blogs/features/apples-airpods-eq-is-it-any-good); the Q, the 155 Hz within its reported range and the ±6 dB limits are Q2 Pod's own, not Apple's coefficients, and its volume-dependent limits are not copied. The preamp is saved with the gains and played as saved (`automatic` 0, never PEQ's per-rate Auto): `headroom` takes the loudest of PEQ's Auto cuts at the standard PCM rates (8, 11.025, 12, 16, 22.05, 24, 32, 44.1, 48, 64, 88.2, 96, 176.4, 192, 352.8 and 384 kHz); nonstandard rates are not covered. A change crosses over in the engine's 20 ms ramp, including Mid's frequency moving across 0 dB. A shelf above Nyquist is skipped at that rate, as any band is.

**State.** `/mnt/data/peq-active` holds one `eq_state` (`patch/peq.h`), written whole (`.tmp`, `fsync`, rename) with the magic `Q2PEQ03`: the mode (`EQ_OFF`, `EQ_3`, `EQ_PEQ`), `hold`, the last applied PEQ and the 3EQ. `eq_load` reads it, or upgrades a `Q2PEQ01`/`02` preset from before 3EQ (PEQ on unless it was bypassed, 3EQ flat); 1.3.5's record, two presets without `automatic`, loads with it 0; any other short, long or invalid record reads as none (Off, PEQ's defaults). `eq_effective` is what plays: the mode's preset, bypassed when Off; PEQ's never-picked preamp (0) becomes Auto (1) there, and only there, so 3EQ and the hold below keep their saved preamp. The player reads it through `peq_load_active` on the playback loop, as before. Saved presets in `/mnt/data/peq-presets` stay `Q2PEQ02`. Every mode change goes through `eq_commit`, which saves the record, writes `PLAYSET/EQFLAG` when on/off changes and calls `peq_stock_eq`, which sets `g_equalizer_flag`, the status-bar icon, to the mode not being Off. Turning either EQ on selects it and turns the other off; PEQ's Apply keeps the mode unless PEQ is on. Slider edits are saved no more than every 250 ms (`E3_SAVE_MS`) and at once when an adjustment ends, and the player is told only while 3EQ plays, so editing an Off 3EQ changes no sound. A failed save says so in the title and keeps the gains to save again. **Hold:** turning 3EQ off on its page saves Off with `hold` set, which plays the 3EQ's preamp alone (`count` 0), so comparing against bypass is not suddenly louder; closing the page clears it. `peq_boot`, called by `ringnav_bootmode` during `config_init` before queue restoration, also clears a saved hold left by a shutdown while that page was open; the selected EQ mode and both presets stay as saved. Live player updates retain the comparison until the page closes.

**Track changes.** hciplayer negotiates a filter for every file, a new instance or the same one again, and `peq_reset` starts its cascade with zero memory. At a gapless change that zeroed memory under a continuous signal is a step: an audible click on any band with gain, which on music can read as a gap (#137). The instance that played last (`last_id`, set by filtered and passed-through blocks alike) leaves its settled filter memory in `carry` when it is freed or renegotiates (`leave`), unless it is mid-ramp, bypassed, never played, or was last filtered more than `XF_LEAD_S` (2 s) before its length, by crossfade's position and length. The next negotiated instance takes that memory once, on its first block (`adopt`), when rate, channels and preset match and that last block was filtered under 3 s ago (`CARRY_STALE_NS`, as crossfade's `XF_STALE_NS`). A skip or stop mid-track, or a pause near the end, starts the next track from zero: it begins from silence, and the old memory would only ring into it. The position is read only for blocks the EQ filters and the clock only near a track's end, so with EQ off playback reads neither. ponytail: a skip in a track's last two seconds carries, and with Gapless off a track played to its end carries into the next if it starts within 3 s; both start after a cut or silence, where the carried memory is usually small. Gate on stock's Gapless flag if that is heard. A different preset or format starts from zero as before. ponytail: this relies on hciplayer freeing the old instance before the new one plays its first block, which is what the device showed; a new instance that plays first starts from zero. `test/peq.py` (`carry_check`) runs two instances and a renegotiation against one uninterrupted DSP, and checks that a skip mid-track, a pause or a passed-through track in between starts from zero.

## Bluetooth resampler

Over Bluetooth hciplayer's ALSA output runs at 48 kHz, so for a file at any other rate `af_init` appends MPlayer's `lavcresample` (`af_info` at `0x8a4540`: open `0x4ac2c0`, control `0x4ac5a8`, play `0x4ac35c`, uninit `0x4ac534`), libswresample at filter size 16 and cutoff 0.8. The player builds a new chain for every file, gapless changes included, and the `SwrContext` holds the last half filter of input back until more arrives. Freed with the instance (`swr_free`, `0x41e2d0`), that tail never plays: the next file starts about 10 output frames (0.2 ms) early, a click on a continuous tone at every change, at any source rate. Wired output plays the file's own rate without one. `RS_OPEN_SLOT` in `tools/peq.py` points the descriptor's open at `rs_open` (`patch/peq_player.c`), which runs stock's and wraps play and uninit. An instance freed when its last block was near its track's end (`rs_end`, the EQ's `carry` test: within `XF_LEAD_S` of the length, under `CARRY_STALE_NS` ago, read in `rs_play` itself since the PEQ filter is left out of the chain with the EQ off from boot) keeps its context; the next instance, on its first block, swaps it for its own fresh one when its `ctx_*` fields (rates, filter size, phase shift, linear, cutoff: what stock's reinit compares before rebuilding) and channels match and the context was kept under `CARRY_STALE_NS` ago, and frees the other. The output is then what one instance playing both files would put out. A skip or stop mid-track, a pause, and a rate or channel change start from a fresh resampler, as stock; wired playback has none, so it reads neither the position nor the clock for this. ponytail: as the EQ's carry, a skip in a track's last two seconds, or a track played to its end with Gapless off and the next started within 3 s, carries the old tail's 0.2 ms into the next track's start; gate on stock's Gapless flag if that is heard. `test/bt_resample.py` runs the built player's lavcresample and libswresample under Unicorn: a tone split across two instances matches one instance sample for sample, stock's loses 10 frames, and a skip, pause or rate change starts fresh.

## Large MP3s

Stock `hciplayer` reads an MP3's Xing or VBRI header for its frame count, as MPlayer does, and then, when it found one, walks every frame of the file before playing (`0x4a2d5c`, called from `demux_audio_open` at `0x491de0`): it recounts the frames for the duration, stores the first `0x5000` frame offsets (about 8 minutes) in a table at `0xd30eb0` and sets MPlayer's `hr_mp3_seek` (`0xb152b8`). That reads the whole file off the card, 140 MB for a two-hour yt-dlp mix and 700 MB for a six-hour one, so the track starts late or seems not to start, and the gapless preload of the next track does it again. A seek past the table walks frames from its last entry, as slowly. An `Info` (CBR) header skips all of it.

The build makes the branch to that walk (`bnez` at `0x48fb3c`, `VBR_SCAN` in `tools/peq.py`, pinned) a nop, so a VBR file takes the path `Info` files already take: the duration is the header's frame count, `hr_mp3_seek` stays 0 and a seek goes to `movi_start + seconds * average bytes per second` at once. On its own that seek is as exact as the bitrate is even: on the card's files the worst case was 9 s in a 2.4-hour jazz mix and 6 minutes in a 5-hour game soundtrack whose tracks differ widely.

The Xing header's 100-point table fixes that. The audio demuxer's descriptor (`0x89fd90`) has its seek slot (`0x89fdbc`, `SEEK_SLOT` in `tools/peq.py`, pinned to stock `demux_audio_seek` at `0x48d1ac`) pointed at `mp3_seek` in `patch/peq_player.c`. For an MP3 (`priv->frmt` 1, `priv` at `demuxer+0xc70`) without `hr_mp3_seek` and not a factor seek, it reads the first frame once per file with `lseek64`/`read` on the stream's fd (`stream_t +0x14`) and restores the fd's offset, since stock's `stream_seek` can reuse its buffer without seeking. `mp3_toc` then parses the Xing header: MPEG 1, 2 or 2.5 Layer III, frame count and table both present; `Info` (CBR) and VBRI keep the stock seek. The target second (`priv->next_pts`, a double at `+8`, plus the offset for a relative seek) becomes a fraction of the track and is read off the table, interpolated between its points. Stock then seeks with `SEEK_ABSOLUTE | SEEK_FACTOR` to that fraction of `movi_start..movi_end`, and `next_pts` is set to the target second, so the shown time is exact. The landing is within the table's resolution, about 1% of the bytes between two points.

## MP3 gapless playback

`patch/mp3_gapless.c` reads Xing and Info headers and the LAME-compatible extension written by LAME, Lavc and Lavf. It follows the optional Xing flags to find the extension, bounds every field to the first MPEG frame, and accepts MPEG 1/2/2.5, mono/stereo and both protected-frame layouts. Missing, truncated, unknown or unusable tags keep stock playback. No silence detection is used: intentional silence stays in the recording.

The encoder records two packed 12-bit sample counts. The decoder adds its own 529-sample delay, so the leading trim is encoder delay + 529, and the trailing trim is max(encoder padding − 529, 0), per channel. Unlike [FFmpeg's MP3 demuxer](https://github.com/FFmpeg/FFmpeg/blob/master/libavformat/mp3dec.c), MPlayer's `demux_audio` sends the Xing/Info frame to the decoder as audio, so the initial trim also includes that frame's 1152 or 576 samples. The layout and delay correction agree with [LAME's tag writer](https://github.com/gypified/libmp3lame/blob/master/libmp3lame/VbrTag.c) and [minimp3's gapless reader](https://github.com/lieff/minimp3/blob/master/minimp3_ex.h).

The checked allocation call at `0x48fb90` becomes `mp3_private_alloc`: the original 16-byte audio-demuxer private prefix is preserved, with zeroed gapless state appended. The `af_play` call at `0x47f8e0` goes through `mp3_filter_entry`, which passes the caller's `sh_audio` from `$fp` to `mp3_filter`. This runs before every audio filter, independent of EQ, with both raw MP3 decoder paths. The first block reads up to 2048 bytes at `movi_start` through the stream fd and restores the fd offset. PCM trimming uses two fixed tail workspaces of at most 3565 frames each in one allocation (at most 57,040 bytes per track). It shifts PCM in place, so larger blocks need no further allocation and cannot lose held audio on allocation failure. Tracks with no trailing trim need no allocation or PCM copy. The tail is discarded at EOF. It does not estimate the end from Xing duration or a seek's approximate byte position. Preloaded tracks have independent state. `mp3_seek` clears held audio on every seek and reapplies leading trimming only at the start; stock's resync flag (`0xb152b4`) already drops the metadata frame on a seek to zero, so it is not trimmed twice. The audio demuxer's close slot (`0x89fdb8`, stock `0x48b7e4`) frees the buffers before stock frees its private state. Allocation failure disables trimming for that track. libmad decodes a frame only with `MAD_BUFFER_GUARD` (8) bytes after it, and `demux_audio` hands `ad_libmad` whole frames, so at EOF stock's `read_frame` (`0x4b23a0`) dropped every MP3's last frame: 1152 samples of the track's end, which the trailing trim then shortened further, an audible tick into a continuous next track (#135). Its `demux_read_data` call (`0x4b248c`) goes through `mp3_read`, which answers the first EOF of an audio-demuxer MP3 with 8 zero bytes so libmad decodes that frame; a seek re-arms it. This applies to untagged MP3s too.

Limitations: iTunSMPB, VBRI-only gapless metadata, free-format MP3 and unknown encoder extensions are not interpreted. Padding below 529 is clamped to zero as in the usual decoder-range calculation; recovering the remaining samples from specially split continuous streams would require feeding an extra compressed frame. This removes encoded padding; physical track handoff still uses stock's Gapless setting and output pipeline.

`test/mp3_gapless.py` covers parser bounds, optional fields, malformed tags and PCM blocks from one sample to a whole track, and compares 16 generated LAME/FFmpeg files with FFmpeg's gapless output sample for sample. It feeds compressed frames directly to libavcodec for the untrimmed reference, avoiding FFmpeg demuxer's automatic metadata-frame removal, and verifies libmad's output lengths and sample alignment too. `test/mp3_gapless_player.py` executes the built MIPS hooks with modelled libc, fd I/O and downstream filters, including preload isolation, seeks before/after the first block, untagged bypass, allocation failure and cleanup. It checks that steady playback does not repeat file I/O or allocations, and that zero-tail trimming uses no PCM copies or buffers. Both suites run during release validation. Hardware listening and battery measurements are still required to assess actual track handoff and power use.

## MP3 ReplayGain

Stock **Audio settings → ReplayGain** (`mcl-replaygain`) scales by the gains FFmpeg's `ff_replaygain_export` (`0x538764`) finds, which the vendor changed to store them in a global (`0xafc790`: track gain, track peak, album gain, album peak, gains in 1/100000 dB, `INT32_MIN` for none) that the replaygain filter's setup (`0x428260`) takes at the next file. lavf demuxers call it, but MPlayer probes MP3 with its own `demux_audio` first, whose `demux_audio_open` hands a leading ID3v2 tag to a vendor frame walker (`0x48e6b0`, called at `0x48f704` and `0x4923d8`). That walker stops after 2 KB of frames, reads ID3v2.4's syncsafe frame sizes as plain ones and keeps a UTF-8 frame's encoding byte at the front of the TXXX description, so `\x03REPLAYGAIN_TRACK_GAIN` matches nothing. Embedded cover art or a long comment ahead of the gains, or a UTF-8 TXXX (what most ID3v2.4 taggers write), and the track plays without its gain; only Latin-1 or UTF-16 gains near the front of the tag reach the filter.

The build points both calls (`ID3_CALLS` in `tools/peq.py`, pinned) at `id3_gain` in `patch/peq_player.c`. It runs the stock walker, then reads the whole tag again through the stream's fd, as `mp3_seek` does, restoring the fd's offset: ID3v2.3 or 2.4, an extended header skipped, frame sizes syncsafe in 2.4. Each TXXX of up to 256 bytes is decoded by `id3_txxx` (Latin-1, UTF-8, UTF-16 with either BOM, UTF-16BE) and put into an FFmpeg dictionary with stock's `av_dict_set` (`0x6ea744`), which goes to stock's `ff_replaygain_export` as the walker's does, so the gains are parsed the same way and stored only when one is there. Tags at the end of the file (APEv2, as `mp3gain` writes, or ID3v1) and the LAME header's gain are not read. `test/id3.py` runs the built player's walker, dictionary and export under Unicorn on tags of each kind.

## MQA detection

At each audio (re)init, `reinit_audio_chain` (`0x42e834`) probes an eligible stream: FFmpeg FLAC or non-AIFF PCM (codec types 13 and 11 at `sh_audio+0xd4`), stereo, 16 or 24 bit, at 44.1, 48, 88.2, 96, 176.4 or 192 kHz. The probe is skipped only when `mcl-mqadecodemode` stored 2 at `0xb10e44`; demo's sender for that command (`0x5ab554`) has no caller, so stock always probes. The probe (`0x42e5dc`) pre-decodes `0xc00` bytes (`0x20000` on output way 2), looks for the MQA sync word (`0x319207d5` in L xor R) and returns 2 for MQA, otherwise 1. Either result creates the vendor PCMDecoder (handle `0xb10d74`) and is stored as isMQA (`0xb10c54`).

Stock `fill_audio_out_buffers` (`0x42f3f4`) then passes every chunk through that decoder (`0x72f9fc`, the library's decoder and renderer) whenever isMQA > 0 (`blez` at `0x42f8c0`). It plays the library's output only when isMQA == 2 (`0x42fc48`). With 1 it plays the original `a_out_buffer`, and the converted buffer is never read. All that work does is let the library's status (`0x42f6b8`) switch a track to MQA mid-play. On the Q2, playing ordinary 16-bit 44.1 kHz FLAC, the side chain was 79% of the player thread's samples in a ptrace PC profile, and hciplayer took 12.8% of a core.

The build changes the gate to isMQA == 2 (`MQA_GATE` in `tools/peq.py`, five words), so non-MQA streams skip the side chain and what reaches ALSA is unchanged. With that, the same playback takes 2.3% of a core. MQA tracks run the decoder as before, so MQA now has to be found at the start of the track. The detector (`0x730354`) searches at most 96000 frames before it settles on not MQA, so the probe now reads that much (`MQA_PROBE`, `0x300000 >> (codec ^ 15)` bytes instead of `0xc00`): `0xc0000` for FLAC, which FFmpeg decodes to 8-byte frames at 24 bit (98304 frames, 2.2 s at 44.1 kHz), well within its `a_buffer_size` (1,732,608 bytes). PCM gets `0x30000`, as ad_pcm's `a_buffer_size` is only `0x30800` and a longer probe would fail its prebuffer and leave isMQA 0. The probed audio is played, not discarded; the probe runs once per track, not on seeks. ponytail: an MQA PCM file whose signal starts after `0x30000` bytes, or any MQA file whose signal starts after 96000 frames, plays as plain PCM; probe longer if one turns up.

The long probe runs inside `reinit_audio_chain` before the track's first block goes out, at a gapless change too, so decoding and searching about 2 s of 24-bit FLAC there can outlast the audio still buffered and leave a short gap between tracks (#76). **Audio settings → MQA** (`mqa_row`, `mqa_click` in `patch/crossfade.c`, stock's `playset_dsdoutput` icon, after DSD Output) reads `MQA: On` or `MQA: Off` and toggles `Q2POD MQA` in `config.ini`, default 0. `mqa_sync` sends stock's own `{mcl-mqadecodemode\N}` on the player socket (`MCL_FD`): 2 while off, which makes `reinit_audio_chain` skip the probe (`0x42eabc`) and take the branch a stream that isn't probed takes, so isMQA stays 0 and nothing of the MQA library runs; 0 while on, hciplayer's `.bss` default, which probes as above. hciplayer keeps the mode until it restarts. `ringnav_mode` sends it again whenever `player_initconfig` calls it (`INITCONFIG_MODE_RET`), which happens on every connect, and a toggle sends it at once. A change applies at the next `reinit_audio_chain`, which is normally the next track's start. With MQA off, MQA files play undecoded, as on a player without MQA. ponytail: `player_reconfig` waits 200 ms before calling `player_initconfig`, so a track started in that window after hciplayer connects is still probed (a slower start, never a gapless gap); sending the mode on connect itself would close that window if it matters.

## Crossfade

**Audio settings → Crossfade** (`ringnav_audioset`, stock's `playset_gapless` icon, fourth after the reorder below; `patch/crossfade.c`) reads `Crossfade: Off` or `Crossfade: 5 s` (`Q2POD XFADE` and `XFADESEC`, 1-15, default 5, in `config.ini`). It opens stock's Boot volume page re-dressed: `playset_bootvol_page_init` (`0x4b4a80`) binds `img_bootswitch`'s click (`on_bootvol_switch_onclick`), `img_dec`'s and `img_add`'s (`0x4b4624`, `0x4b45a8`) and `slider_bootvol`'s `EVT_VALUE_CHANGING`/`CHANGED` (`0x4b48f4`, `0x4b4300`), each with the window as context; `xfade_open` unbinds them with `widget_off_by_func` and binds its own, so Boot volume is never written. The title reads Crossfade and `view_bootvol` (the slider, the minus and plus and `label_vol`, "Off" or "5 s") is always shown and enabled (stock disables it with the view hidden while Boot volume's switch is off, which left touch dead on the slider and the minus and plus, #140): its slider runs `slider_set_min`/`max`/`step` 0, 15, 1 (AWTK takes doubles), 0 being Off, so the page needs no switch. A drag shows and plays each value at once but saves only at its release (`slid`: the slider's value-changed, not value-changing), or when the page closes mid-drag, so a quick drag is one config write, not one per value. Stock's slider sends no value-changed for a drag released where it began or an aborted press, so any later step, the switch, or the close saves a value a drag left unsaved (`set_value`'s `held`). That matters on iPod, where stock creates the title and `img_bootswitch` inside `view_navbar`, which `navbar_only` hides; the switch still toggles where it shows. Going Off keeps the length, which Centre brings back. `xfade_key`, from `ringnav` after `spot_key`, gives the page Centre (a single press toggles, a double press turns the screen off as on any page) and the wheel (a second a tick, down to Off).

**Settings order.** Playback and System are restacked by use after stock builds them and the payload adds its rows (`reorder`, `playset_order` and `sysset_order` in `patch/navigation.c`): Play Mode, EQ, Gain, Crossfade, Gapless, ReplayGain, Max and Default Volume, Balance, Mono Audio, Swap L/R, Filters, Memory Playback, Resume Songs, Artists, Year Order, Artist Album Order, the folder rows, Enlarged art, DSD Output, MQA and USB output; and Wireless, Display, Power, Date & Time, Buttons Lock, Key Tone, Network, Language, In-Vehicle, Boot to (with Rockbox), Update, Reset, About. Stock names each row's button by its table id (`"%d"`) and its click handler (`0x4b9364` for Playback) reads the id back from that name, so moving a row keeps its action; a page with an unexpected row count is left as built.

**Policy.** `xfade_poll`, from `playback_poll` each UI pass, writes the next change's fade in ms to `/tmp/q2xfade` (an `int`, made whole under `.tmp` and renamed like the visualizer's tap, then mapped shared), only when its inputs change: Crossfade on, stock's Gapless on (the byte `0xa3be54`, `mclGetGaplessMode`), and the next track another album's (`album_cmp`, so an album, a CUE image or an untagged folder stays gapless). The next track is `playback_next`: `playback_successor(1)` while advanced play runs, else `pos + 1` in modes 0 and 3, which is what stock preloads (`mclAutoChange` preloads under 6 s from the end, too late to decide on, so the poll predicts it instead). While a fade is set, each pass puts the PEQ filter in the chain (`peq_chain`) when PEQ is off, as the visualizer does.

**Fade.** The fade runs in `patch/peq_player.c`'s filter, after the PEQ and before the visualizer's tap. Each block reads the decoder's position, `written_audio_pts` (`0x44641c`, a leaf of `sh_audio` `mpctx+0x2c` and `d_audio` `mpctx+0x34`; `playing_audio_pts` at `0x42a134` subtracts the output's delay from it), and the length, `0xb10e84`, the whole seconds `{media-info}` reports (`mclGetPlayTime`). From `2 × fade + 2 s` before the end the filter keeps what comes in in a 6 MB ring and puts out half as many frames, oldest first, until the fade's length is held, then as many as come in: the decoder runs at twice real time meanwhile, and nothing is skipped or reordered. The next track's filter is a new instance (the player re-adds it at each file; each gets `player_state.id`), which mixes the held tail under its own first frames, `in × sin(p) + out × cos(p)` over the held length, so the sum keeps its power. Should the player keep one instance across the change, a position that starts over near 0 right after the end counts as the next track as well; the hold keeps the length it began with. A position jump over 1.5 s while holding is a seek and drops the held audio; so does a tail not followed within 3 s (stop) or followed at another rate or channel count. The hold is capped by the ring: 15 s up to 48 kHz stereo, 7.5 s at 96 kHz, 3.7 s at 192 kHz. While a fade is held, the elapsed time runs ahead by what is held, so it reaches the length as the next track fades in. DSD passes through the filter untouched, so a fade into or out of DSD does not happen. ponytail: a manual skip during the hold mixes the held tail into the chosen track; a rate change at the switch drops it.

Checks in `test/peq.py` (host build of the filter): the hold window, half-rate emission and order, the equal-power mix and its length, no fade asked, a seek while holding, another rate after, the same instance starting over, and an unknown length; `test/patch.py`: the row and its saved length.

## Mono Audio

**Audio settings → Mono Audio** (`ringnav_audioset`, stock's `playset_balance` icon, right after Balance; `mono_row` and `mono_click` in `patch/crossfade.c`) reads `Mono Audio: On` or `Off` and toggles `Q2POD MONO` in `config.ini` (default 0). `mono_sync`, from `playback_poll` each UI pass and on each toggle, writes it to `/tmp/q2mono`, an `int` made under `.tmp`, renamed and mapped shared, as `/tmp/q2xfade` is. Nothing is created until Mono Audio is first turned on, and a missing file reads as off. Every reader maps the file and picks up a change within one block:

- **hciplayer** (local music, Tidal; DAC, Bluetooth and USB): the PEQ filter's `play()` sets both channels of each stereo frame to their mean, after `peq_process` and before the crossfade and the visualizer's tap. Per-channel bands and the EQ's balance shape each side first. Stock's Balance acts after the mix. The file is looked for once a second of audio until it appears (`watch`, shared with `xfade_ms`). Boot leaves the filter out with the EQ off, so `mono_sync` calls `peq_chain` while Mono Audio is on, as a fade does. ponytail: `peq_chain` waits for play (status not 1, stopped) for all three callers, since whether an attach while stopped sticks is unknown.
- **q2video** (Videos, Internet Radio, Spotify's sink): `writer` mixes each S16 chunk after the soft volume (`mono`), looking for the file every hundredth chunk until it appears.
- **Spotify on the headphone DAC** normally plays through `aplay`. With Mono Audio on, `spot_output` writes `plughw:0,0 hVOLUME` to `SPOT_OUT`, so `aplay.sh` runs `q2video -s` there instead; the `h` keeps the volume on the DAC, and `sound_open` readies the DAC (`DAC_PCM`, `DAC_MUTE`) for any `plughw:0,0`, volume or not.

ponytail: DSD, DoP and native DSD output pass through bit-exact (`dsd()`) and stay stereo, and so does stock's AirPlay receiver. On the headphone DAC, switching Spotify's sink between `aplay` and `q2video` waits for the sink's next start (pause and play), like a new output device does. q2video already playing on another output follows the switch at once.

Checks in `test/peq.py` (host builds): the filter's mix once the file appears, live, and exact again when cleared; `mono` on S16 at both ends of the range. `test/patch.py`: the row, its place after Balance, the saved key, and the shared int set live, mapped once.

## Resume

`ringnav_sleep` (both variants, the UI thread) polls at most once a second. It reads the queue's playing record (`queue_now`) and `player_playtime_and_length` (`0x514128`): elapsed and total track seconds, CUE-relative, and -1 while nothing plays. A track is long when its total is at least **Audio settings → Resume Songs** (`Q2POD RESUMEMIN`, minutes; `crossfade.c` `resume_min`, read once), or its extension is `.m4b` (case-insensitive) anywhere on the card, or it is under [Podcasts or Audiobooks](#podcasts-and-audiobooks), and its record has no CUE start or end (`+0x48`, `+0x4c`). A CUE track (either set) is long only when spoken (`.m4b`, or under those folders), so a music CUE image is left alone. Places are kept by the path's FNV-1a hash in a ring of `RESUME_SLOTS` (512, a 4 KB file), most recent first, as file seconds: a CUE track's place is its start plus its elapsed seconds, so a book's chapters share one place, and the same file played whole resumes there too. The ring is read from `/mnt/data/ringnav-resume` on the first long track and written whole, to a `.tmp` then renamed. That happens every `RESUME_SAVE_S` (60) seconds of movement, once the time stands still for two polls (a pause), and on a track change. A place within `RESUME_EDGE_S` (30 s) of either end of the file is forgotten. A CUE track saves nothing within its own first `RESUME_EDGE_S`, keeping the book's place, so a chapter played becomes the book's place once past its first 30 s; only the last CUE track (no end set) knows the file's end, where the book is finished and its place forgotten. A track change, `listen_key` (the path's hash with a CUE start mixed in, so moving to the next chapter counts), waits one poll before it counts, so the previous track's time never belongs to the new one. When a long track with a saved place first plays (elapsed at least 1 s) with under `RESUME_START_S` (5 s) elapsed, `player_seek_time` jumps there, as Scrub commits. A track paused at 0 waits for Play, so Memory Playback's paused boot does not seek, and its Location restore, already under way, is left alone. A CUE track never jumps when played: a chapter picked is played from its start. Instead the [Queue menu](#queue-menu) of a spoken CUE song row whose file has a place starts with **Resume** (`book_place`): it copies the page's songs (type 8) and plays them as a library queue (`play_folder(..., 0xf001)`, as Most Played) from the last chapter of that file starting at or before the place, with the seconds into it waiting for that chapter's `listen_key`, for up to 8 s. The poll seeks there once that chapter plays under `RESUME_START_S`, so the previous position the player still reports, or a queue that switches a poll or two late, does not consume it; the chapter already playing works the same way. With no place left at the pick, the menu says "Queue unchanged". The ring's format is unchanged, so places saved before stay valid: an earlier release's 32-slot file loads as the first 32 places, and an earlier build reads the first 32, the most recent, of the bigger one. A hash collision only means a wrong resume point.

**Resume Songs** (`ringnav_audioset`, stock's `playset_memplay` icon, after Memory Playback in the reorder) reads `Resume Songs: 30 min` or `Resume Songs: Off` and opens Crossfade's re-dressed Boot volume page (`xfade_open` ctx 2): no switch, the slider and wheel stepping five minutes through 20-120, below 20 Off; each change is saved (a drag at its release, as Crossfade) and live on the next track. `RESUMEMIN` is 30 unless `0` (Off) or a multiple of five in 20-120; anything else reads as 30. Off stops music tracks keeping a place; spoken audio (`.m4b`, Podcasts, Audiobooks) still resumes at any length. The default was a fixed 20 minutes before this row, so a track of 20-30 minutes no longer resumes until the row is lowered.

## Folder entry

Both editions open Folder directly at `/mnt/mmc`, layer 2, when `g_otg_status` is clear and the card is present on entry (`/tmp/mmc_add` readable, stock's `check_tfstatus` at `0x4f8978`). After stock initializes the page, `ringnav_folder` stores `/mnt/mmc` in `media_root`, sets the path and runs the existing reload, title and refresh functions, the same block a shortcut uses at layer 3. Back where `g_folder_path` equals `media_root` sets layer 1 before stock Back decrements it to 0 and leaves the page, even if OTG was connected meanwhile. With OTG connected or no card on entry, the storage selector and stock navigation remain, and `media_root` is cleared. Nested folders and playback use the existing handlers. Podcasts/Audiobooks shortcuts take precedence and retain their own root exit.

## Folder Root

**Playback settings → Folder Root** (`ringnav_audioset`, stock's `playset_folderjump` icon, after Folder skip in `playset_order`) reads `Folder Root: Card` or `Folder Root: <name>`: the string `Q2POD FOLDERROOT` in `config.ini` (`toolsReadConfig`, `toolsWriteConfig`), missing or empty for the card. Centre or tap (`root_click`) reads `/mnt/mmc` once and saves the next top-level directory (`d_type` 4, or 0 unknown, as `media_find`) after the saved one in `strcasecmp` order (FAT and exFAT hold no two names differing only in case), dot-folders left out; "" (Card) gives the first, after the last it saves "", and a saved name no longer there moves on to the next name after it. ponytail: names over `ROOT_MAX` (200) bytes or starting or ending with a space are not offered, since stock's config line (`toolsWriteConfig`'s `sprintf` into 257 bytes, `toolsReadConfig`'s 255-byte `fgets`, then trimmed) can't keep them.

On a Folder visit with a card (`/tmp/mmc_add`), `ringnav_folder` opens `/mnt/mmc/<name>` at layer 3 through the media roots' block when it is a directory (`__xstat`; a name with `/` or a leading dot is ignored); a pending Podcasts/Audiobooks root wins, and without the folder the visit is as in [Folder entry](#folder-entry). `media_root` stays `/mnt/mmc` without a drive, so the root is an ordinary folder: stock Back (`folder_back`) strips the last name to `/mnt/mmc` at layer 2, titled `SD` as stock's, and Back there leaves the page; with a drive it reaches stock's storage selector. Every root's open (the card, Folder Root, Podcasts, Audiobooks) first runs stock's `folder_reset_variable` (`0x50727c`), as Back to layer 0 does: layer, path, `g_m3u_path` and the `g_yoffset_save`/`g_index_save` stacks cleared, and `g_navbar_status` and `g_allselect_flag` zeroed (the multi-select state, which stock's `folder_enter`, `folder_back` and page inits clear too). A visit left without Back (Home, a song opening Now Playing) keeps them, and `folder_back` pops a non-empty stack into the page's position and, inside an M3U (`g_m3u_path` set), reloads the same path instead of stepping up, so Back from the root would have landed on a stale position or stayed in the root a layer short. With the stacks empty the step up keeps the back handler's zeroed position, the top of the card's list.

Checks in `test/patch.py` (stock `folder_back` and `folder_reset_variable` executed): the root at layer 3 after a visit left deep and inside an M3U, Back to `/mnt/mmc` (layer 2, title, position untouched), then out of the page or to the selector with a drive; a gone, file, hidden or path root, no root and no card as before; Podcasts' own root; the row's cycle, order, skipped names (a UTF-8 name with a space kept), saved value and a gone root.

## Podcasts and Audiobooks

Folder's row-name formatter treated names as printf formats, crashing on `99% Invisible` even with Folder Album Cover off. `FOLDER_NAME` in `tools/build.py` changes the call at `0x522394` to `tk_strncpy(buffer, name, 511)`, keeping its 512-byte buffer terminated and displaying percent signs literally in folder and file names. The real row callback is exercised in `test/patch.py` with percent signs, format directives and an overlong name.

`ringnav_localmusic` reads `/mnt/mmc` (`opendir`/`readdir`) and adds a row for a directory (`d_type` 4, or 0 unknown) named `Podcasts` or `Audiobooks`, ASCII case aside, last, with build-added 52 px icons drawn in stock's style in `assets/icons/` (`local_podcasts`, purple with a microphone, and `local_audiobooks`, orange with headphones). A press looks the folder up again and `navigator_to("folder_page")`s, as Home's Folder card (`on_home_folder_onclick`) does. Stock's folder browser keeps its place in globals: `g_folder_layer` (1 the storage list `folder_load_top` builds, 2 a storage root, 3 on deeper folders), `g_folder_path`, `g_navbar_name` (the title) and the `g_yoffset_save`/`g_index_save` stacks, which `folder_enter` pushes and `folder_back` pops. `folder_page_init` (`0x523330`, hooked as `ringnav_folder`) always starts at layer 1 (`folder_enter(-1, 0)`). After it, a pending press copies the path in, sets layer 3 and runs stock's `folder_reload_data` (`toolsLoadDirectory` into `p_deque_showlist`), `folder_reinit_navbarname` (layer 3 and on: the path's last name) and the page's navbar and table rebuild (`0x52176c`, `folder_refresh`), as stock's back and refresh do. Every check on the layer elsewhere is `< 2`, so the root is an ordinary folder: entering, song presses (`player_load_songlist(..., 1)`, folder play), covers and the refresh on return from Now Playing are stock's. `folder_back` (`0x507ac8`, `ringnav_folder_back`) has one caller, the page's back handler (`0x5218e4`, the title arrow and the Return key); at layer 3 on that root it sets layer 1 first, so stock's step down reaches 0, resets the browser and the handler closes the page, back to Local Music. Any other folder_page open clears the root, so the Folder view's own `/mnt/mmc/Podcasts` climbs as before.

An M4B track anywhere on the card, or a track whose path is under either folder (`/mnt/mmc/<name>/`), is resumed at any length (a CUE track by its file's place and the hold menu's Resume, see [Resume](#resume)) and skips the listen count, so it never reaches Most Played or the scrobble log. The stock speed control (`PLAYSPEED` in `playerjumpinfo_page`) now saves its 0–3 mode by exact parent path; a track with no folder (a Tidal stream) takes it for that track only, unsaved. `ringnav_speed` hooks `mclSetPlaySpeed`; calls returning to `player_initconfig` at `0x51339c` restore rather than save. `ringnav_start_player` (`playback_track_speed`) sets the folder’s mode and sends it to the running decoder before opening a track, since the decoder otherwise keeps the previous track’s speed, and `ringnav_open_preload` declines gapless across a speed boundary. Files under `/mnt/data/ringnav-speed-<path hash>` contain the full, zero-terminated 1024-byte parent path and a 32-bit mode; reads validate both, writes use `blob_io`’s temporary-file rename. Missing or invalid preferences default to normal. Failed saves show a toast and reload the saved preference. The old global speed is not migrated because it does not identify a folder. shortcut: genre-only Podcast/Audiobook tags do not grant spoken-audio behavior; add a genre source if needed. Every local track start (`ringnav_start_player`) and gapless preload (`ringnav_open_preload`) first checks `.m4b`, `.m4a` and `.mp4` files with `mp4_fits` (`patch/chapters.c`). FFmpeg's mov demuxer, which hciplayer uses for MP4 and `/usr/bin/ffmpeg` uses for Chapters, indexes every sample before it plays: a 24-byte `AVIndexEntry` and a 4-byte `stsz` size each, so a 47-hour book's millions of AAC frames outgrow the Q2's 128 MB and the device stalls, again at boot through Memory Playback. `mp4_index_bytes` walks `moov/trak/mdia/minf/stbl` with `fopen`/`fseek` and sums the `stsz`/`stz2` sample counts at 28 bytes each. A file `fopen` cannot open because it is 2 GB or more (`EOVERFLOW`, demo has no large-file I/O) counts as too long. The index must fit `MemAvailable` from `/proc/meminfo` less `MP4_SPARE_KB` (16 MB); a failed start returns -1, as stock's own checks do, a gapless preload is declined with stock's failed-attempt state so automatic change ticks do not repeat the scan, and the next UI pass (`playback_poll`) toasts it, since `mclStartPlayer` can run off the UI thread. Chapters refuses the same files with its own toast. ponytail: the estimate leaves out chunk tables and the decoder's buffers, and MemAvailable is read while the previous track may still be open; tune `MP4_SPARE_KB` from device measurements. `test/chapters.py` checks the estimate on synthetic and real MP4s; `test/patch.py` checks the Chapters refusal.

All Library music views (Songs, Albums, Artists, Composers, Genres, Hi-Res, Frequent, Recent and Recently Added, including their detail lists) exclude `.m4b`, `/mnt/mmc/Audiobooks/` and `/mnt/mmc/Podcasts/` paths with the same case-insensitive SQL predicate as Coverflow (`MUSIC_WHERE`). `ringnav_localclass` scopes `ringnav_library_query` around category loads, split-album queries and Unknown-row checks; the artist page and music album staging use the same scope. The query hook substitutes a filtered `songtable` subquery before grouping, so shared music/spoken-audio tags keep music artwork and spoken-only Unknown categories disappear. Favourites and playlist contents bypass `songtable`: Favourites keep spoken tracks; `music_records` filters only playlists' displayed song copies with `spoken`, preserving music order, playlist containers and every stored entry. Most Played, Long Songs, category Play Next/Add to Queue and Shuffle Songs, Albums and Folders use music-only loads too. Scoped loads restore the caller's filter state, including empty or failed queries. Ratings, podcast and audiobook folder browsing, playback and folder queue expansion stay unfiltered. My Fav, Frequent and Recent (`0xf00a`, `0xf008`, `0xf007`) list stock's copies of songs as added or played; a copy with an empty title, as a song played from Folder has, takes the library's title by path (`fav_titles`), so its row no longer reads as the file name with its track number.

## Buttons lock

Settings' Buttons Lock rows and the quick-settings lock tile offer Off, Everything, Buttons only and Volume only. They save stock's `SYSSET LOCKFLAG` and `LOCKMODE`: 3 for Everything, 2 for Buttons only, 1 for Volume only. Stock's key-down and key-up filters already apply these modes only while the screen is off: mode 1 rejects wheel keys 172/173; mode 2 rejects Return, Play/Pause and skip keys 222/223; mode 3 rejects both sets. A mode outside 1–3 is saved as 3. Centre remains the wake key. `KEY_LOCKOUT` is independent, the short debounce after a button press. `ringnav_keylock_rows` replaces the stock row builder; `ringnav_quick_lock` replaces the lock tile callback, and `ringnav_quick_settings` labels the current choice on open.

The lock is the buttons' alone. SyncLink (Eddict Player) has no path of its own to the player: `synclink_mainscheduling` returns a play control as `0x65`, `0x66` or `0x67`, and `on_wm_idle_status` posts it as a press and release of Play/Pause (171), 222 or 223 with `main_loop_post_key_event` (`0x68b5fc`, its only caller), so stock's dark filters dropped them like the buttons (#113). `ringnav_post_key` hooks the poster and keeps each post's key and the `time_now_ms` window around it, which the queued event carries as its stamp (`EVENT_TIME`, low word; four posts kept, a match spent); `ringnav_input` dispatches an event matching one with `g_keylock_flag` cleared, then restores it. A button event never matches: it needs the same key stamped within the same millisecond or two.

## Photos

The iPod classic keeps Photos on its main menu; here it is the last Local Music row, **Photos**, made like Podcasts' (`media_find` kind 3, stock's 52 px `playset_covermode` copied as `local_photos`) for a top-level `Photos` folder, case aside. iPod Home is seven fixed rows filling the client area and the Stock build's carousel needs card art per entry, so neither takes more entries without a new layout; [Books](#books) and [Videos](#videos) follow it. A press calls `photos_open` (`patch/photos.c`), which opens a runtime `photos_page` (`window_create`, `CTX_DYNAMIC` in `contexts.inc`, as `coverflow_page`) with three views, shown one at a time:

- **Albums**: Coverflow's `page_list`, "All Photos", then each subfolder holding a photo, by name (`qsort`, at most 200; deeper folders are not albums). Without subfolders the page opens on the grid.
- **Grid**: the album's photos (`.jpg`, `.jpeg`, `.png`, ASCII case aside, not dot files such as macOS's `._` copies), by path, at most `PHOTOS_MAX` (500, under ringnav's 512 rows); All Photos is the folder's own, then every album's. A bare `scroll_view` (a `list_view` would lay its children out as full-width rows) holds `PH_COLS` (4) `s_listitem_black` tiles of `PH_TILE_W` × `PH_TILE_H` (93 × 80), three whole rows under the title. The wheel moves through them in order; iPod's bar and the Stock outline frame the tile, round a thumbnail of at most `PH_THUMB_W` × `PH_THUMB_H` (85 × 72), `PH_GREY` until it exists.
- **Viewer**: a `slide_menu` with an empty `image` child per photo, as Coverflow's covers: items the page's height square with the spacer making the stride 375, so ringnav's carousel step (`carousel_page`) moves one photo a tick with its retargeted animator, and touch drags it. The paint hook draws the photo at the live offset (`visual`) and, mid-slide, the one coming in, fitted into 375 × 290 without enlarging, over the black page. Centre (the child's click) toggles a caption, "3 of 40" and the file name, on a rounded `#000000B3` band clear of the glass corners; "Loading…" and "Can't open this photo" show without it. Return goes viewer, grid (`ringnav_select` puts the bar on the photo last shown and the grid scrolls to it), albums, Local Music.

**Cache.** Opening a grid starts one pthread, as Coverflow's art build. For each photo, the viewer's first, then its neighbours, then the rest in order, it reads the first 4 KB for the EXIF orientation (APP1 `Exif`, IFD0 tag `0x112`, either byte order), then, unless cached in `/mnt/mmc/.photos/<fnv(path)>`, writes `.jpg`, `toolsThumbSpecCover` to `PH_SHOT_W` × `PH_SHOT_H` (sides swapped for orientations 5 to 8, so the upright photo fits the screen), and `t.jpg` from that copy at the thumbnail size, each through `.tmp` and a rename, under `parse_cover_mutex`. Stock fits landscape sources to the width and portrait ones to the height, keeping the aspect. A file neither stock nor the ffmpeg fallback can decode gets an empty `.bad` marker; a vanished card leaves none. Below 16 MB free (`statfs`) nothing new is written.
