A 250 ms timer repaints as photos finish and joins the thread; leaving the grid or the page cancels and joins it, a wait bounded by one photo. ponytail: keys are paths, so an edited photo under the same name keeps its old copy, and copies of removed photos stay; deleting `.photos` rebuilds it.

**Drawing.** `photos_paint`, called from `ringnav_paint` like `coverflow_paint`, decodes a cached copy with `widget_load_image`, turns it upright into a payload `bitmap_create_ex` RGBA8888 slot over black (`photo_orient`: orientations 5 to 8 swap the sides, 2, 3, 7 and 8 mirror x, 3, 4, 6 and 7 mirror y), unloads it at once and draws the slot with `canvas_draw_image` (`photo_place`: fitted, centred, never enlarged). Thumbnails keep `PH_TILE_SLOTS` (32) slots and the viewer three, the photo farthest from the one wanted giving way; the viewer decodes both neighbours once it settles, so a slide draws from memory. About 2.6 MB of slots at most, freed with the grid.

Checks in `test/patch.py`: file names, EXIF orientation (byte orders, other segments, truncation, PNG), all eight orientations against their EXIF meaning, channel order and alpha, placement; then on a mocked card the Local Music row, albums, the grid's tiles, the worker's sizes and order and the bad marker, the upright thumbnail, the viewer's fit, a mid-slide frame, the caption, the wheel step, Return's selection and the slot release.

## Books

**Books** is the last Local Music row (`media_find` kind 4, `assets/icons/local_books.png`, tan with an open book), for a top-level `Books` folder, case aside. `books_open` (`patch/books.c`) opens a runtime `books_page` (`CTX_DYNAMIC`) with two views:

- **List**: Coverflow's `page_list`: the folder's `.txt` and `.epub` files and those of its subfolders (one level, not dot files), by path, at most 500, each named by its file name without the extension. ponytail: an EPUB's `dc:title` is not read.
- **Reader**: a bare `view` (the sheet) the paint hook fills, and Photos' caption band. `books_paint`, called from `ringnav_paint` like `photos_paint`, sets the system default font at 20 px (`canvas_set_font(canvas, NULL, 20)`, as `draw_centred`) and white text over the black page, then lays out `BOOK_ROWS` (10) lines 26 px apart from y 6, 24 px in from either side, every line clear of the glass corners, with `canvas_measure_text` per character and `canvas_draw_text` per line. Lines wrap at the last space, else mid-word; a line break at the top of a page and a byte order mark are dropped; `\f` starts a new page. Text is UTF-8, and any byte that is not valid UTF-8 is read as Latin-1. The same paint finds the page before: pages laid out again from a line start up to 3 KB back until one reaches this one (`book_back`), so paging back may realign to a paragraph start. `ringnav()` hands the reader's keys to `books_key` before the surface lookup (the sheet is not a list): the wheel moves to the next or previous page, Centre toggles the caption (how far in, as a percentage of the file, and the title), and Return (the page's key-up, as Photos') goes back to the list, its bar on the book.

The file is read through a 32 KB window starting 8 KB before the page, read again once the page is within 4 KB of its start or 8 KB of its end, so no book is loaded whole. The page last read, a byte offset, is kept per book (FNV-1a of the path) in `/mnt/data/ringnav-books`: 64 key/offset pairs, the most recently read first, written whole through coverflow.c's `blob_io` when the reader closes, and reopened there.

**EPUB.** The first open converts the book on one pthread into `/mnt/mmc/.books/<fnv(path)>.txt` (through a `.tmp` and a rename), showing "Preparing…" in the caption; a 250 ms timer joins it, and later opens read the text at once. Leaving cancels between documents and joins. demo imports nothing from zlib (`libz.so.1` is loaded, but neither `inflate` nor `dlopen` is in its GOT), so `book_inflate` is a raw-deflate decoder after zlib's `puff`: stored, fixed and dynamic blocks, every length and distance checked against the output and the input. The zip's end record (in the last 64 KB) gives the central directory (2 MB at most); an entry is read by name through its local header, stored or deflated, 8 MB at most either way; encrypted and ZIP64 entries are refused. `META-INF/container.xml`'s `rootfile` `full-path` gives the OPF; each spine `itemref`'s `idref` is looked up in the manifest (`item`, any namespace prefix, either quote), and its `href`, relative to the OPF, `%XX` decoded and without a `#fragment`, is read. Images in the spine (`media-type` `image/…`) are skipped. `book_xhtml` turns each document into text in place: tags dropped; `p`, `div`, `br`, `li`, `tr`, `hr`, `dt`, `dd`, `h1`-`h6`, `blockquote` and `section` as line breaks (at most one blank line); `head`, `style`, `script` and comments skipped; `&amp;` `&lt;` `&gt;` `&quot;` `&apos;` `&nbsp;` and numeric entities as UTF-8; white space runs as one space. Documents are joined by `\f\n`, so each starts a page. A book with an `encryption.xml` naming a content document (DRM; obfuscated fonts alone are fine), or whose container, OPF or a spine document cannot be read, says "Can't open this book". ponytail: each itemref scans the manifest, quadratic in the items; an id index if large books prepare slowly.

Checks in `test/peq.py` (host build of `books.c`): inflate against zlib at every level and strategy, a short output and a truncated stream refused, garbage that never runs out of bounds; XHTML to text; EPUB conversion from stored and deflated zips with a prefixed OPF, a `%20` href, a fragment, a cover image and an empty document; obfuscated fonts against DRM, a missing container, truncated and bit-flipped zips, cancel; UTF-8 with the Latin-1 fallback; wrapping, `\f`, and paging back exactly in a short text and to a page reaching the current one in a long one. `test/patch.py`: the Local Music row, the list, the reader's lines (white, default font, inside the glass at every row), the wheel's pages both ways, the caption, Return, the saved page, an EPUB converted by the MIPS worker and read, and a broken one.

## Videos

**Videos** is the last Local Music row (`media_find` kind 5, `assets/icons/local_videos.png`, blue with a play screen), for a top-level `Videos` folder, case aside. It opens Folder view at that folder (as Podcasts and Audiobooks do, `media_click`), so each subfolder is its own row and nothing is flattened into one list. Its `.mp4`, `.m4v`, `.mkv`, `.avi`, `.mov` and `.mpg` files play from there by a row click: its row click (stock's `0x521bc4`, hooked as `ringnav_folder_click`; it reads the row's index into `p_deque_showlist`, then `folder_enter`, whose 8, a file, it would queue as songs, so a video failed as one) hands a song record whose `REC_PATH` names a video to `books_video`, which plays it through `video_play`, reading the resume marks first since no Books page holds them; select mode (`g_navbar_status`) and every other row stay stock's. Only other extensions are left to stock, which queues them as songs. Stock's folder listing (`toolsLoadDirectory`) takes audio extensions only, so those rows exist only because its extension check (`ringnav_audio_extension`, at its `0x5c2054` call, told apart by the return address) also takes a video's while `g_folder_path` is in the card's Videos folder (`folder_videos`); elsewhere, and in the library scan, videos stay unlisted. Without it a Videos folder showed stock's empty-folder "File Not Found" (#128). A song played there would queue them too, so `ringnav_load` hands stock's `mclLoadPlayList` a copy of the tracks without videos (`no_videos`), the start index moved to the same song, and leaves the caller's showlist whole; a list of videos alone loads an empty audio queue. Folder hold actions also exclude videos, and video rows have no audio hold menu. A row (`play_video`, `video_play`) calls stock `player_stop` (`0x515b94`: hciplayer keeps its PCM, `hw:1,0`, open while paused), readies the headphone output on the DAC as stock's `player_start` does before each track (`dac_take`, shared with Spotify's `spot_take`: `config_outputchannel(g_headset_output, 2)`, which powers the DAC with its firmware in headset mode, or `mclSetDacPwr(1)` for a line out the DAC-off timer powered down; then PCM mode, unmuted, and the volume), then `fork`s and `execl`s `/usr/bin/q2video DEVICE PATH [VOLUME]`, the path a plain argument: nothing passes through a shell. `DEVICE` is `plughw:0,0`, the headphone DAC (the CS43131, the only ALSA card; there is no card 1), whose hardware volume (`mclSetVol`, ioctl `0xc0044d00` on `/dev/shanling_dac`) stays the music's. When `mclGetOutputWay` is 1 (Bluetooth) it is `plug:bluealsa`, the PCM hciplayer opens for that way (`0x439858`, string `0x88ed30`): demo writes `defaults.bluealsa { device "XX:XX:..." profile "a2dp" }` for the connected device to `/mnt/data/asound.conf`, which `alsa.conf` loads, so the name needs no address. There the volume is hciplayer's soft volume: `device_set_volume` (`0x4f44d4`) calls `mclSetBtVol(v, v)`, sending `{mcl-setvol\%d}` with `v << 8 | v`, and hciplayer's handler (`0x42c770`) calls its `mixer_setvolume` (`0x428744`), which sets an `af_volume` level of `0.1 + 0.018 (v - 50)` from 50, `v * softvol-max (20) / 10000` below, and removes the filter at 100 (no `snd_mixer` is imported, so the soft path is the one taken). So the payload passes the effective `headset_gain()` as `VOLUME` (full gain after accepted headset volume, otherwise `g_volume`, 0-100), and the helper scales its samples by the same curve (`bt_gain`, in 1/65536) and leaves the DAC's mute alone. With a USB DAC (2) `DEVICE` is `usb`, which the helper turns into `plughw:N,0` for the card `/proc/asound/cards` lists as `USB-Audio` (card 1 behind the headphone DAC's 0, as stock's `mclUsbAudioSetVol(…, "hw:1")` has it; hciplayer's `hw:2,0` string, `0x43975c`/`0x88ed40`, is no card on the Q2), and the plug converts the helper's 48 kHz stereo to what the DAC takes. Its volume is `device_set_volume`'s: `mclUsbAudioSetVol(v, v, "hw:1")`, or 100 when `g_usbvol_mode` (Audio settings' USB volume) is 0, fixed. That sets the DAC's own volume control; a DAC without one leaves `USB_MIXER` (`0xa3be6c`, no symbol) at -2 and sends hciplayer `{mcl-softvolflag\1}`, whose set-volume handler then applies the same `mixer_setvolume` curve as Bluetooth's. So `VOLUME` is plain `g_volume` (the helper's `bt_gain`) only for a variable volume on a DAC without its own control; otherwise it is `h` and the volume, which the helper shows on its bar and never applies.

While the helper runs, demo's loop goes on (timers, threads, the status bar) but `ringnav_input` drops every input event before the window manager, sending a key's release (`0x114`) to the helper as a datagram on its `AF_UNIX` socket `/tmp/q2video.sock` (`video_key`): Return `q`, Play/Pause `p`, the side buttons 222/223 `b` and `f`. The wheel is the volume, as on Now Playing: a tick steps `g_volume` as stock's `volume_dialog` keys do (`0x4a2044`: by 1, up only below 100 and `g_maxvolume`, down to 0), calls `device_set_volume(v, 1)` (the DAC's `mclSetLocalVol` ioctl, so the DAC follows at once, and for Bluetooth `mclSetBtVol`, so hciplayer has it after) and `write_int_config(v, "PLAYSET", "VOLUME")`, without stock's dialog, and sends `v` and the volume byte, at an end too. Centre toggles the wheel to seeking (`s`, then `b` and `f` per tick) and back (`v`); `video_poll` also puts it back on the volume `SCRUB_MS` (3 s) after the last tick or Centre, like Now Playing's scrub. And `ringnav_wm_paint` skips the window manager's paint. `video_poll`, from `ringnav_sleep`, calls `waitpid(WNOHANG)` every pass, meanwhile `reset_poweroptions_timer(1, 1, 1)` (`0x4f43dc`, the screen, standby and auto-power-off counters `on_wm_idle_status` and `check_to_poweroption` count down) and zeroes `g_dacoff_time`; a pause that lasts `VIDEO_PAUSE_QUIT_MS` (30 s) without a key sends `q`, as Return, since input belongs to the helper and the hold would otherwise keep a paused video (a dropped headset pauses it) lit until the battery runs out. At the helper's end it closes the socket and repaints the whole screen. Music stays stopped. A video picks up where it was stopped: `play_video` writes the second kept for its path (FNV-1a, in Books' `BOOK_MARKS` ring, through the same `mark`) to `/tmp/q2video.place`, which the helper reads as its first `-ss`; on its end it writes there the second a Return left it at (`resume_at`: 0 within `EDGE_S`, 30 s, of either end, as [Resume](#resume)'s `RESUME_EDGE_S`, and 0 when it played to the end), and `video_poll` keeps that for the path, unless it and the start were both 0, so a video never resumed takes no slot from the books. A seek still pending at the Return counts.

**q2video** (`patch/video.c`, about 10 KB) is linked by `tools/build.py` against the rootfs's own `libc-2.28.so`, `libpthread` and `libasound.so.2` (no MIPS sysroot: the file declares what it uses), `nan2008`, `fp64` and `/lib/ld-linux-mipsn8.so.1` as the stock binaries, with its own `__start` like glibc's. It runs stock `/usr/bin/ffmpeg` (FFmpeg 4.2): `-ss` the start, `-vf scale=375:320:force_original_aspect_ratio=decrease:flags=fast_bilinear,format=bgra,pad=375:320:(ow-iw)/2:(oh-ih)/2,transpose=clock` (the picture fitted into the landscape 375 × 320 view, then turned clockwise onto the portrait framebuffer, as the boot logo is stored), `-r 25 -f rawvideo pipe:3`, and `-ac 2 -ar 48000 -f s16le pipe:4`. `/dev/fb0` is 320 × 375, 32 bpp with red at bit 16 (ffmpeg's `bgra`) and two pages: `soc_fb.ko`'s `init_var_info` gives every 32-bit format red 16, green 8, blue 0 and `yres_virtual` = `yres` × `layer0_frames` (2 in `module_driver/soc_fb.sh`, `is_rotated=0`); stock `display_logo` (`0x400bf0`) refuses under 18 bpp and stores each pixel as the word `0xff000000 | R << 16 | G << 8 | B` (`0x400f2c`), the 320 × 375 JPEG unturned; demo's AWTK flush thread pans between the two pages (`FBIOPAN_DISPLAY` at `0x6265bc`, then `FBIO_WAITFORVSYNC`). The helper exits without drawing if `FBIOGET_VSCREENINFO` reports anything else. A thread writes the sound with `snd_pcm_writei` in 512-frame chunks (200 ms buffer) and keeps the frames heard (written less `snd_pcm_delay`); frame n goes to the framebuffer at n / 25 s of that clock, a whole frame late is dropped, and it draws the page demo is not showing and pans (`FBIOPAN_DISPLAY`), panning back to demo's page at the end. The video pipe is 4 MB (`F_SETPIPE_SZ`), so ffmpeg is paced by the sound. A `v` sets Bluetooth's gain to `bt_gain` of its volume. `s`, `b`, `f` and `v` show a bar for 1.5 s (`OVERLAY_MS`), drawn on the page after each frame's copy and before the pan, and redrawn with the next frame when paused: `BAR_H` (8) px high, `BAR_Y` (22) px above the picture's bottom and `BAR_X` (40) px in from its ends, clear of the 80 px glass corners, white for the volume of 100 or the position (start plus frames shown plus pending seek) of the length, black for the rest, by byte fills (white and black in BGRA). The length comes from a second `ffmpeg -nostdin -hide_banner -i PATH` started at once, its stderr read on the main loop's poll until it ends (or 4 KB) for `Duration: HH:MM:SS`; without one the position bar is not drawn. Pause holds the writer (the sound buffer plays out, then the clock stops); a seek restarts ffmpeg once 400 ms after the last tick, at the position plus 10 s per tick; without a sound stream ffmpeg fails at once and it runs again silent on a monotonic clock. After hciplayer's stop it opens the PCM (retrying 2 s; bluealsa takes one client per PCM, so this also waits for hciplayer's close), sets the DAC to PCM (`0xc0044d1b`, 0, as hciplayer does per PCM track, so a DSD track's mode is undone) and unmutes it (`0xc0044d1f`, 0); at the end it mutes it again, as a stopped hciplayer leaves it. ponytail: the frame rate is fixed at 25 and decoding is software, so sources much above the screen's size can fall behind; a smaller encode then plays smoothly.

Checks in `test/peq.py` (host build of `video.c`): ffmpeg's argv with and without sound, the frame pacing, Bluetooth's gain curve and sample scaling, the `Duration` parse, `resume_at`'s edges, and the bar's pixels on a padded-line frame. `test/patch.py`: the Local Music row, the list, the child's `execl` argv after `player_stop`, the DAC power-on, Bluetooth's device and volume, silent USB, every key's datagram, the volume steps and their limits through `device_set_volume` and `write_int_config`, Centre's seek mode and its timeout, no other input or paint while playing, the held timeouts, and the end.

## Games

**Games** (iPod only) is the Library row after the media rows, always shown, with a 52px controller icon (`assets/icons/local_games.png`) in the same centred slot as the neighboring Library icons. Its purple disc becomes grey in Minimal through the existing category-icon mapping. `games_open` (`patch/games.c`) `calloc`s the whole state, opens a runtime `games_page` (`CTX_DYNAMIC`) with Coverflow's `page_list` chooser (Sudoku, Vortex, Solitaire, Snake) and a bare `view` sheet, and frees it all when the page closes; the scratch keeps only the pointer. `games_paint`, called from `ringnav_paint` like `books_paint`, draws the sheet with `canvas_fill_rect`, rounded boxes and frames (`canvas_fill_rounded_rect`, `canvas_stroke_rounded_rect`, square where the canvas declines), `caption` (Solitaire's suits through `draw_centred`, the default font's ♠♥♦♣) and, for Vortex and Snake's apple, vgcanvas arcs and dots, in `accent_tone(2)` (so both themes), and puts the fill colour back. Everything stays clear of the glass's 80 px corners ([Rounded corners](ipod.md#rounded-corners)): Sudoku's centred board already does; Vortex's score, best, level and lives sit `HUD_X` (24) px in; Solitaire's 45 px cards start `SOL_X` (18) px in and `SOL_Y` (6) down, and its two outer columns close their fan up to end `SOL_LOW` (35) px above the bottom instead of 4.

**Keys.** `games_key` runs in `ringnav()` right after the rating picker, before stock's key filter, while a game is showing and the screen is usable. It takes every key, so the wheel, Centre, Play/Pause and the side buttons never reach the volume, `media_key` or the double-Centre screen-off. A held Return's release (`RETURN_RELEASE_LATCH` set) and Power stay stock, so a long Return still goes Home. In the chooser it takes nothing; Return there is the page's key-up (`return_key`), back to the Library.

- **Sudoku**: the wheel moves through the cells in reading order, Previous/Next move a row, Centre on a blank or entered cell opens the digit (the wheel picks blank or 1-9, Centre writes it, Return cancels), Play/Pause starts a new game (asking first over an unfinished one), Return goes back to the chooser. Clues are on grey; a digit that repeats in its row, column or box is red and underlined. Every cell holding the cursor's digit, or the digit being picked, shows it dark on the tone.
- **Vortex**: Centre launches the ball from the paddle, Play/Pause pauses and resumes, Return pauses with Continue/Quit (the wheel picks, Centre confirms), and after the last life Centre plays again.
- **Solitaire**: the wheel walks the 13 piles (stock, waste, the four foundations, then the seven columns) and Previous/Next jump between a top-row pile and the column under it (the gap's column to the waste). Centre on the stock draws (an empty stock takes the waste back); on another pile it picks up its top card, or a column's whole face-up run, framed in the tone, and the cursor turns white. Centre on another pile drops what fits there, and nothing happens where nothing does; Centre on the same pile again sends its top card to a foundation, or puts it back. Return drops the held cards, then goes back to the chooser. Play/Pause starts a new game, asking first unless this one is won.
- **Snake**: the side Next button turns right and Previous turns left, one turn a step and a second waiting for the next (any more are dropped). The touch wheel is ignored during play and in the pause menu; Previous/Next pick Continue/Quit. Play/Pause pauses, Return opens Continue/Quit, and after a crash Centre plays again.

**Sudoku.** 24 bundled puzzles, 4 bits a cell, each with exactly one solution. A new game picks one and relabels its digits, shuffles rows within bands, columns within stacks, the bands and the stacks, and maybe transposes it, all of which keep the solution unique. ponytail: fixed puzzles, no generator, pencil marks or hints.

**Vortex.** Three rings of 16 bricks round the centre (40-76 px out) and the paddle, a 45° arc at 118 px. All integer: positions and velocities in 16.16 px per 1/60 s step, angles 1024 a turn, sine and arctangent from tables, so the same inputs give the same game. A brick reflects the ball off its radius (ponytail: never off a brick's side); the paddle sends it back towards the centre, turned up to 45° by how far off-centre it hit. Missing the paddle costs one of three lives, shown as grey balls under Best. A broken brick flashes white and fades over `POP_MS` (240 ms), and the ball trails its last `TRAIL` (4) frames' positions in the tone; `frame()` records both, so the game logic is unchanged, and repaints only while the ball moves or a flash fades. The game-over banner says "New best!" when the game raised Best. Clearing every brick is the next level: one of three layouts in turn (ponytail: fixed layouts), each 15 px/s faster from 120 to a cap of 270, under a ring's 12 px a step, so the ball never crosses a ring without touching it.

**Solitaire.** Klondike, draw one, unlimited passes through the stock. 13 piles of up to 24 bytes, a card `suit * 13 + rank` plus `UP` when face up. A column turns its top card when the one on it leaves. A held run drops the one part of it that fits (only one card in a run can go on a given card, and only its King on an empty column), so there is no depth to choose; a foundation takes only the top card, any foundation an Ace. Once the stock and waste are empty and every card is face up, `sol_finish` sends everything home at once (the lowest card left is always a column's top). Columns fan face-down cards 6 px and face-up ones 20 px (the rank and suit's strip), closed up to fit the sheet, the two outer columns `SOL_LOW` short of it; ponytail: at the deepest (6 down, 13 up) a label shows 12 px of its 16 px in an inner column, 9 px in an outer one. ponytail: no undo, score, timer or draw three.

**Snake.** A 20 × 20 board of 11 px cells under the score and best, clear of the glass's corners (`SN_X`, `SN_Y`). The body is a ring of cell indices from the tail, the head last; a step moves the head one cell, and the tail follows unless it ate, so moving into the cell the tail is leaving is allowed. A wall or the body ends the game. An apple adds one to the length and the score and lands on a free cell picked with `rand_below` over the free count. Vortex's `frame` timer runs it: Snake's branch adds the milliseconds passed and takes a step per `snake_ms` (160 ms, 4 ms less an apple, 70 at most), two a frame at most, so the speed doesn't follow the frame rate. Only a record is saved (`/mnt/data/ringnav-snake`, `SNK1`, sealed as Vortex's best); an unfinished game isn't. ponytail: no wrap mode, levels or obstacles.

**Wheel.** Ticks are too coarse for a paddle (about seven a turn), so while Vortex plays `wheel_raw` is set (as on Now Playing's lyrics page, [Lyrics wheel](#scrub-ipod)) and `wheel_sample` (`patch/playback.c`) posts no ticks: it adds the raw travel (200 units a turn) to `wheel_travel` atomically, and nothing is queued for a menu meanwhile. Each frame reads and zeroes it; `VORTEX_GAIN` (percent, 100 a turn for a turn) is the paddle's calibration knob.

**Frame timer.** A 33 ms `timer_add`, only while Vortex plays, runs 60 steps a second (at most 4 a frame) and repaints only when the steps changed something (the paddle, the ball, the state), so waiting to launch paints nothing; only the wheel turning holds the screen timeout off, so a ball left in play lets the screen dim and go off like any page, which pauses it. A key the game ignores paints nothing either (`games_key` compares the state before and after). The screen going off or another window on top pauses the game, clears `wheel_raw` and ends the timer; so do Play/Pause, Return's menu, leaving and closing.

**Saves.** `/mnt/data/ringnav-sudoku` (the grid and the cursor) and `/mnt/data/ringnav-solitaire` (the piles and the cursor) are written only when changed: a written digit, a move, a draw or a new game marks its game dirty and arms one `SAVE_MS` (2 s) timer from the first unsaved change, not per move, and leaving to the chooser or closing the page (Home too) writes whatever is dirty, a moved cursor included; ponytail: a power-off loses the moves of the last 2 s, and a cursor moved since the last write (only moves arm the timer). `/mnt/data/ringnav-vortex` holds the best score, written when a game ends above it. All go through `blob_io`: each a struct whose first word is a magic and last the FNV-1a of the rest, so a short read, another magic or a bad checksum is a fresh game. A failed write shows "Couldn't save".

Checks in `test/peq.py` (host build of `games.c`): every bundled puzzle and 200 shuffled ones have exactly one solution; clues refuse writes, conflicts by row, column and box, completion; the save seal; Vortex's paddle wrap, launch, bricks both ways, the paddle's return, lives to game over, the next level, the speed cap, every launch angle at full speed hitting the outer ring first, and the same inputs giving the same state; Solitaire's deal, draw and redeal, foundation and column rules, a run's part that fits, turning, home, the finish, and 100 seeded random games keeping every card once and every pile sound (some won). `test/patch.py`: the Library row, the chooser, Sudoku's keys and save with no key reaching stock's filter or `media_key`, the confirm, Return game → chooser → Library, Vortex's timer and raw wheel through Play/Pause, screen-off and Quit, Solitaire's draw and save, cursor, pick up, refused and good drops, Centre twice home and put back, Return, the confirm, and closing mid-game, the Solitaire resuming. `test/playback.py`: `wheel_raw` posts no ticks.

## Spotify

**Spotify** is Streaming's row after stock's (`ringnav_stream`, after `stream_page_init`'s rows in `scroll_view_streamsset`, a `library_into` row with `assets/icons/stream_spotify.png`, Spotify's logo as a 52 px palette PNG with `list_tidal`'s inode metadata). It opens the browser (below; without `librespot` on the card, `spotify_page` saying so), a runtime window (`patch/spotify.c`), for librespot, a Spotify Connect receiver built from the [q2-librespot](https://github.com/DiamondBond/q2-librespot) fork (branch `shanlingq2`; hard-float, linked against the stock glibc, 19.5 MB, so it stays on the card). Nothing of it is in the firmware: the card's `/mnt/mmc/.spotify/` holds `librespot`, its launcher `run`, the sink `aplay.sh`, the saved login `cache/` and the logs.

**Starting.** `spot_launch` runs `system("/bin/sh /mnt/mmc/.spotify/run")` once per boot, when the row is first pressed, and only then: until it has, `spot_poll` returns at once and nothing reads the card or the state file. The login (`cache/credentials.json`, written after the first Connect handoff) only saves the phone's pick. `run` passes `--cache-size-limit` from `.cache-size` ([Streaming configuration](#streaming-configuration)), `2G` without it. `run` backgrounds a loop that restarts librespot after any exit, waiting 5 s and doubling to a minute while runs keep failing (mDNS needs Wi-Fi up), and leaves the loop's pid in `/tmp/q2-librespot`, which also makes a second run exit. librespot's process is not demo's child, as bluealsa's isn't. Once it has played, `SPOT_IDLE_MS` (30 s) without playing (paused, stopped or handed to another device) while the browser is shut makes `spot_poll` stop it as Home's Rockbox row does (`spot_stop`: the loop's pid, `killall -9 librespot aplay`, the pid file, the state file and `SPOT_OUT` removed), so the keys are local music's again; the row starts it again. Before it has played, the wait is `SPOT_CAST_MS` (150 s), time to cast to the Q2 from the phone; it counts from the launch or the last poll with the browser open, whichever is later. An open browser (pairing prompt and Now Playing on it included, and so any request in flight) restarts the wait every poll while the screen is on, but holds no timer and no DAC: only playing does (`spot_take`). Left open with the screen off, it holds nothing: once the wait runs out, `spot_poll` closes the browser (`window_close`, as Back would) and then stops librespot, so a Q2 left dark on it, with Standby off, doesn't keep librespot connected and its state files read twice a second. ponytail: waking after that finds Streaming, not the browser; its row starts librespot again. Without `librespot` on the card `spotify_page` says so and nothing runs.

**Contract.** librespot runs with `--backend subprocess --device "/bin/sh ./aplay.sh" --volume-ctrl fixed --initial-volume 100 --control-socket /tmp/q2-librespot.sock --status-file /tmp/q2-librespot.state` (the last two are the fork's):

- The status file is rewritten whole (a `.tmp`, renamed) on each player event: `state=` `none` (no Connect session: the phone hasn't chosen the Q2, or chose another device), `stopped`, `paused` or `playing`; `track=` (the Spotify id), `title=`, `artist=` (joined by `, `), `album=`, `duration=` and `position=` in ms, `at=` the `CLOCK_MONOTONIC` ms the position was taken at, and `cover=1` once `/tmp/q2-librespot.state.jpg` holds this track's cover, which librespot fetches itself over its own TLS (the smallest Spotify image at least 160 px wide: 300 px). The payload does no TLS.
- The socket takes `AF_UNIX` datagrams, sent `MSG_DONTWAIT` as Videos' are: `p` play/pause, `P` play, `s` pause, `n` next, `b` previous, `S` and a position in ms to seek. librespot ignores them without a session.
- The subprocess sink starts `aplay.sh` when playback starts and kills it when it stops, pause included, so the DAC's PCM is free while librespot is paused. `aplay.sh` is the stock `aplay -D plughw:0,0 -f S16_LE -r 44100 -c 2`, retried for 3 s: aplay opens the PCM before it reads its input, so a busy PCM costs no audio. Each try first reads `/tmp/q2-librespot.out` (`SPOT_OUT`): when it holds a device and a volume, it runs `/usr/bin/q2video -s DEVICE VOLUME` instead (below). Without the file, which older Q2 Pods never write, it is aplay as before.
- **Output.** Every poll `spot_output` asks `output_device` (books.c, Videos' and Internet Radio's choice through `video_start`) for the output in use: none on the headphone DAC, `plug:bluealsa` with `headset_gain()` for Bluetooth (`mclGetOutputWay` 1), `usb` with `g_volume`, or `h` and it when the DAC applies the volume, for a USB DAC (2). When the line changes it writes `DEVICE VOLUME` to `SPOT_OUT` (a `.tmp`, renamed) or removes the file, and sends `v` and the effective gain to `/tmp/q2sink.sock`. `q2video -s` opens the device at 44.1 kHz, S16 stereo, with Videos' `sound_open` (retrying 5 s while hciplayer lets go), plays stdin through Videos' writer at `bt_gain` of the volume, as hciplayer's soft volume would (the DAC's mute is left alone), and takes `v` and `q` on that socket as radio's helper does. It ends with its input (the sink's kill closes librespot's pipe) or when a write fails even after `snd_pcm_recover`, a headset gone: librespot's subprocess sink then restarts `aplay.sh` once, which reads the file again. ponytail: otherwise an output switched while playing takes over at the sink's next start (pause and play), as Videos and Internet Radio keep their device until they end.

**Polling and power.** `spot_poll`, from `ringnav_sleep` every `SPOT_POLL_MS` (500 ms), reads the file and parses it when it changed. On the change to `playing` (`spot_take`) it does what stock's AirPlay page does for its receiver (`airplay_page_init`, `on_airplayset_onclick`): `player_stop` unless hciplayer is stopped (it holds the PCM even paused), `config_outputchannel(g_headset_output, 2)` (what a headset insert runs: `mclSetHeadsetMode`, `mclSetDacPwr(1)`, which also loads the DAC's FPGA firmware, and `g_dacoff_time` 0), `mclSetPcmMode`, `mclSetMute(0)` (`player_stop` mutes) and `device_set_volume(g_volume, 1)`. With Bluetooth or USB (`g_headset_output` 2 and up) it only powers the DAC on if `check_dacoff_state` turned it off; the sink plays there itself (Output, above). While `playing`, every poll calls `reset_poweroptions_timer(1, 1, 0)` (`0x4f43dc`: its arguments are standby, auto power-off and the backlight, in that order) and zeroes a counting `g_dacoff_time`, so neither standby nor `check_dacoff_state` ends playback, while the screen still turns off on its timer. On the headphone DAC the volume is the DAC's own: librespot's is fixed at 100%, and `device_set_volume` (stock's volume dialog, the screen-off wheel) sets the DAC with `mclSetLocalVol` whether or not hciplayer plays. ponytail: Low Power still takes CPU1 offline with the screen off; librespot decodes on one core, and an exception like Videos' is the upgrade if that falls short.

**Keys.** `ringnav()` hands Play/Pause, previous and next (171, 222, 223) to `spot_media` right after stock's key-lock filter (`on_wm_keyup_before_fun`), on any page and with the screen off, while Spotify is the active source: playing or paused, and no local track has played since. They become `p`, `b` and `n`, and stock's own key-up never sees them. On the page Centre works as on Now Playing's [scrub](#scrub-ipod) (`spot_key`): a release arms the same `DOUBLE_CLICK_MS` (250 ms) timer (`center_press`, shared with Now Playing), and a second one before it ends starts a scrub; the wheel then moves it `SPOT_STEP_MS` (5 s) a tick and the bar turns white, and a centre press (at once, swallowing a quick second one, as on Now Playing), Return or `SCRUB_MS` without a tick seeks there with `S`. Outside a scrub the timer, a single press, replays the release to stock `on_wm_keyup_fun`, so the screen turns off. Otherwise the wheel is stock's volume and `spotify_page` is not in `contexts.inc`, so `ringnav()` leaves it to stock.

**Hand-over.** Local music → Spotify is `spot_take` above. Spotify → local music: `mclStartPlayer` (`0x5ad6ac`), which every local track's start runs before hciplayer opens the PCM, is hooked (`ringnav_start_player`). With librespot playing, `spot_yield` sends `s` and waits, 20 ms at a time up to `SPOT_YIELD_MS` (1.5 s), until the file no longer says `playing`. The fork's sink stops (aplay killed and reaped) before the `Paused` event, so the PCM is free once it does. The keys are local music's again from then on. Home's Rockbox row kills the loop and librespot (`/tmp/q2-librespot`'s pid, `killall -9 librespot aplay`), as Rockbox takes ALSA.

**Page.** `spot_open` builds Now Playing's layout from `tools/ipod.py`'s `NP_*` in both variants: "Spotify" in the top row with a play or pause glyph at its end, the art 166 px at x 16, y 50, the title (22 px white), artist and album (16 px `#AAAAAA`) in a 165 px column at x 194, the bar at `NP_BAR` and the times under it. The art is librespot's cover sized once a track by Coverflow's `thumb` (`toolsThumbSpecCover` under `parse_cover_mutex`) to `/tmp/q2spot.jpg` and loaded with its `image_show`, `default_album_big` (Legacy) or `default_album_flow` (iPod) until there is one; iPod rounds its corners as Now Playing's (`paint_cover`). The position is `position + (now - at)` while playing, clamped to the duration. A 250 ms timer, idle with the screen off, writes the times when the second changes and repaints the bar, which `spot_paint` draws as Now Playing's capsule: `TRACK_COLOR` with the played part in the accent's light tone (iPod) or stock red (Stock). The remaining time is whole seconds less whole seconds, as Now Playing's. Only a `login` reply or a sign-out shows "Open Spotify on your phone / and choose Q2"; a playing or paused track, or any `ok` reply, confirms the login, and `state=none` makes it unknown again. A successful play shows "Loading…" until a track plays: the one asked for, or any other than before it (a relinked id). After `SPOT_WAIT_MS` (15 s) it says "Playback didn't start", without sending play again, until any track plays. A refused key ends the wait sooner. The play's `ok` notes the length of `.spotify/spot.log` (librespot's stderr, which `run` appends to), and while it loads `spot_poll` reads what was logged since (the last 4 KB of it, re-reading 14 bytes so a line written in two parts still matches). Once that holds `error audio key` ([librespot#1649](https://github.com/librespot-org/librespot/issues/1649): Spotify refused the track's key), it says "Spotify refused this account / Its tracks can't play on the Q2". It also runs `killall -9 librespot aplay`, because librespot otherwise skips through the whole list, a key refused each time, and a pause (`s`) doesn't stop it while it loads. `run`'s loop starts librespot again with no track, and for `SPOT_SIGNOUT_MS` the browser takes a missing socket for that restart (`spot_restarting`). Any track that plays later still clears it. The three labels scroll without focus or an ellipsis and restart when their text changes; `field` cuts only at a UTF-8 character boundary. It sits on the browser, so Return goes back to the list it was opened from. ponytail: the cover is sized on the UI thread, one decode a track; a worker if it stutters.

**Browser.** `spotbrowse_page` (`CTX_DYNAMIC` in `contexts.inc`, so the wheel moves its rows) is one `page_list` rebuilt per level, as Internet Radio's, its rows' clicks run from a timer. A level is a Q2SPOT/1 request ([librespot.md](librespot.md#browsing-protocol-q2spot1)): its op, its arguments and cursor (escaped, `spot_escape`) and its reply, cut in place by `spot_reply` into the header's 4 fields and 6 a row (at most 50), unescaped; a bad escape, `%00`, a NUL, a line of the wrong width or too many rows makes it malformed. IDs, URIs and cursors are sent back exactly as received. The base level asks `account`: `ok` shows the menu (Now Playing, Queue and Playback Settings only with a track), `login` the pairing prompt ("Open Spotify on your phone and choose Q2", "Both on the same Wi-Fi"), which asks again every `SPOT_PAIR_MS` (2 s) and becomes the menu once paired. Liked Songs, Playlists, Albums and Artists list 64 px rows (title over subtitle); an album, playlist or artist row opens its own level (an artist's top tracks, then its albums), and a non-empty cursor adds **More…**, a level of its own, so Back returns to the page and the row left. Up to `SPOT_DEPTH` (8) levels are kept, the oldest past the base dropped; only the top `SPOT_KEEP` (3) keep their replies (64 KB each at most), and Back to an older one fetches it again. Search lays peq_ui.c's T9 edit over the title; its OK with text opens Tracks, Albums, Artists and Playlists for the term, and empty text or the box's blur leaves the menu as it was. A track row sends `play CONTEXT POSITION URI` (`play URI` without a context; a Queue row `skip POSITION URI`) and opens Now Playing once it is `ok`, else toasts the message. Play/Pause held on a track row (`ringnav_keylong` → `spot_hold`) sends `enqueue URI`, its release latched as the radio's, and only toasts a failure message; held on a playlist row it sends `download URI`, as the open playlist's Download Playlist row does, and toasts the reply (it starts, or another playlist is already downloading). Liked Songs has a Download Liked Songs action when the backend advertises saved collections. The persistent Downloads hub, capability negotiation, ordered offline playback and storage controls follow [downloads.md](downloads.md). `caps` is asked once, after the base's `account`; a request that cancels it (any other) leaves `caps_checked` clear, and the page's 250 ms tick asks again once the menu is idle at the base, re-laying the menu with the reply only when no click is pending on its rows. A removal preview is its own act (`A_PREVIEW`), so only its reply opens **Remove Downloads?**; one dropped by Return can't turn a later action's reply into a removal. Download Progress polls `jobs` every 2 s only while the browser is on top with the screen on, and a failed poll keeps the list. With an older backend (no saved collections, but `download`), Downloads keeps **Download Status** (`downloadstatus`, its message toasted). Playback Settings shows Shuffle as a switch (`list_toggle`) and Repeat (Off, All, One); a press sends the next value and reads them again. Account shows the name and Premium, and Sign Out asks first; after `signout` the pairing prompt returns. Loading ("Loading…"), empty ("Nothing here", unless a cursor offers More…) and errors are page states: the backend's message as the title, with **Retry** for `offline`, `throttled`, `error` or no usable reply. Any `login` reply returns to pairing.

**Worker.** `request` writes `Q2SPOT/1`, the id (`wk.id`, never reused) and the op into a 64 KB buffer and starts `worker` on a pthread: an `AF_UNIX` `SOCK_STREAM` (2, MIPS numbering) to `/tmp/q2-librespot.browse.sock`, the request sent `MSG_NOSIGNAL`, the write side shut (`spot_shut`, the o32 `shutdown` syscall 4182, since demo imports no `shutdown`), then `select` 100 ms at a time and `recv` into the same buffer until EOF, `SPOT_WAIT_MS` (15 s) or a cancel; more than 64 KB fails. One request at a time: a new one, Back from a loading level or the page's close cancels the one before and joins it within 100 ms. The page's 250 ms timer takes the finished reply on the UI thread; one whose header doesn't echo the id, or for a level since left (`level.id`), is dropped. Actions (`play`, `skip`, `enqueue`, `shuffle`, `repeat`, `signout`) are never sent again by themselves. No socket within `SPOT_OLD_MS` (3 s) of librespot's start, or `SPOT_SIGNOUT_MS` (15 s) after a sign-out (librespot exits and its launcher restarts it), is librespot starting: "Loading…" (or the pairing prompt) asks again every 500 ms. After that, a state file means an old add-on: Now Playing and "Update Spotify on the card / to browse on the Q2", still asking every 2 s. Without either, "Spotify isn't running" with Retry. ponytail: a title is cut at 255 bytes by the backend and a search term at 63 by the page; nothing is cached across opens.

Checks in `test/peq.py` (host build): `spot_reply` on escapes in any case, UTF-8, a short header, every malformed shape and the row limit; `spot_escape` and its bounds. Checks in `test/patch.py`: the row and its icon, the page without librespot (nothing starts), the start once per boot from the row and never without it, saved login or not, the session prompt, `spot_take`'s calls and their order and Bluetooth's exception, `SPOT_OUT` and the sink's volume on Bluetooth and its removal on the DAC, the held timers, the art, the labels moving on from `at`, every label inside the glass, the bar's rectangles and colour, the keys on another page and their return to local music, `mclStartPlayer`'s wait and its limit, the scrub and Return; the idle stop only with the browser shut. The browser against a fake backend on the socket: the first request's bytes, the menu with and without a track, loading, errors with and without Retry, empty pages, More… on an empty page, a wrong id, a bad escape and an oversized reply, playlists by URI, tracks played in their context (with their URI) or alone and Now Playing only on `ok`, a refused key (the note, one kill, no play sent again, a line from before the play ignored, a line cut between polls found, no kill without one), More… and Back to the row left, Back while loading, an artist's and album's pages and a refetched evicted level, pairing and its polling, an old add-on and a missing librespot, search's empty, left and escaped terms, Add to Queue on hold and its failure, Queue's skip, Playback Settings and an unrepeated failure, Sign Out's confirmation, the restart after it, a lost login, and the idle stop after the browser shuts and the relaunch.

## AirPlay

Stock's receiver is `/usr/bin/shairport`, Shanling's Shairport 1.1.1 (`1.1.1-22-shanling`): AirPlay 1 (RAOP) only, with its own mDNS responder, started and stopped by demo (`airplayStartService`, `airplayStopService`) from Network service's switch (`on_airplayset_onclick`, `0x51e8f0`), with its now-playing page `airplay_page`. Streaming's **AirPlay** row (`ringnav_stream`, stock's `netservice_airplay` icon) only opens `systemset/netservice_page`. ponytail: the row doesn't toggle the service itself; stock's `airplay_switch` (`0x50ef44`) is the way in if it should. AirPlay 2 would be a shairport-sync 4.x add-on with nqptp, an add-on on the card like librespot.

**Open device check (1.4.4):** missing AirPlay title/art has not been reproduced. Both editions keep stock V1.32's `airplay_page.bin` byte for byte, including `img_playcover`, `label_title` and `label_artist`. During a phone stream, check whether the page retains the stock title/artist or shows real text without art; check the receiver's `-M /tmp/dirairplay` argument, cover files there, `/tmp/airplaypic.png` and free space in `/tmp`. This is a diagnostic follow-up, not a confirmed firmware fix.

## Internet Radio

**Internet Radio** is Streaming's last row (`ringnav_stream`, `assets/icons/stream_radio.png`, a broadcast glyph on coral, 52 px palette PNG with `list_tidal`'s inode metadata). It opens `radio_page`, a runtime window (`patch/radio.c`, `CTX_DYNAMIC` in `contexts.inc`, so the wheel moves its rows), one `page_list` rebuilt per level: the menu (Now Playing, when a station has played this boot or `/mnt/mmc/Radio/.last` exists; Favourites; Featured; Top Stations; By Country; By Genre), a country or genre list, and a station list. Rows rebuild the page from a timer (`later`), never inside their own click. Return goes up a level, from a "Loading…" or failure caption back to the list it covered, and from the menu back to Streaming. Each level keeps its selected row.

**Lists.** Favourites are `/mnt/mmc/Radio/favourites.m3u`, then every other `.m3u` and `.pls` there, read whole and parsed as one text (`playlist`): `#EXTINF:…,NAME` names the next line, `FileN=` is a URL and `TitleN=` names the one before; a line without `://` is no station. Favourites start empty: opening the page only creates the `Radio` folder. The six built-in stations (`STARTERS`, each checked against stock-like ffmpeg) are Featured, parsed from the payload. Search is the page's pull-down ([Pull-to-search](#pull-to-search)) on any level: `radio_search` lays a T9 edit (`edit_create`, shared with the EQ editor's value) over the title and focuses it for the keyboard; its OK (`EVT_VALUE_CHANGED`) fetches `/m3u/stations/byname/TEXT` (%-encoded, a substring match), and an empty result says "No stations", and Return goes back to the level it was pulled over. Empty text, or the box's blur (`EVT_BLUR`, `0x10f`) without a search, rebuilds the page as it was. An empty OK closes the keyboard with neither event, so the keyboard's OK (`EVT_IM_ACTION`, `0x13a`) runs the same handler. Return empties and blurs the box, which shuts the keyboard before the box is destroyed: destroying it under an open keyboard crashes demo. With the keyboard up, Return never reaches the page: `search_key` (`ringnav`, ahead of stock's filter) takes it for the input method's edit when that is one of `edit_create`'s, named `EDIT_NAME` (`q2_edit`): it empties it, blurs it and detaches the keyboard, so every one of them (Radio, Spotify and card-app search, the EQ value and preset name) cancels, and the next Return goes back. Stock's search dialogs and the playlist editor's search, named `search_edit`, are blurred and detached without emptying, the editor's search applying its text on that Return. The directory is [radio-browser.info](https://api.radio-browser.info)'s round-robin `all.api.radio-browser.info`, fetched by `fetch_worker` on a pthread through demo's libcurl, over verified TLS with the firmware's `/etc/scrobble-ca.pem` (ISRG Root X1 signs it), 10 s to connect, 30 s in all, at most 256 KB: Top Stations is `/m3u/stations/topvote/100?hidebroken=true`, By Country `/csv/countries?order=stationcount&reverse=true&limit=60` then `/m3u/stations/bycountrycodeexact/CODE`, By Genre `/csv/tags?…` then `/m3u/stations/bytagexact/TAG` (%-encoded, `url_escape`), each with `?order=votes&reverse=true&limit=100&hidebroken=true`. The m3u is the favourites' parser's; the CSV (`choices`) is read from each line's end, so a quoted name may hold commas. Without Wi-Fi (`get_wifisignal`) the page says "No Wi-Fi"; a failed fetch "Directory unavailable". Return while loading sets a cancel flag that the transfer's progress callback returns, so the join takes at most about a second. A list holds `RADIO_MAX` (200) entries.

**Favourites.** Play/Pause held on a station (`ringnav_keylong` → `radio_hold`, the row from the list's `_ringnav_index`, or on Now Playing the station playing) rewrites `favourites.m3u` (a `.tmp`, synced and renamed) without it when its URL is there, else with it appended, and toasts which; its release is latched as the queue menu's (`st.qm_press`), so it does not stop the radio. A file already at `RADIO_MAX` is never rewritten, so nothing past it is lost.

**Playing.** A station (`radio_play`) copies the list it is in, so the side buttons step through it later, stops a playing q2video, pauses librespot (`spot_yield`), and starts `/usr/bin/q2video -r DEVICE URL [VOLUME]` through `video_start` (books.c), Videos' own output choice: `player_stop`, the headphone output readied (`dac_take`), `plughw:0,0`, `plug:bluealsa` or `usb`, and the volume for Bluetooth's and soft USB's gain. The station goes to `/mnt/mmc/Radio/.last` as a one-entry `.m3u`. The keys and their output stay demo's: `radio_poll`, from `ringnav_sleep` every 500 ms while it runs, reads `/tmp/q2radio.state`, reaps it (`waitpid(WNOHANG)`), calls `reset_poweroptions_timer(1, 1, 0)` and zeroes a counting `g_dacoff_time`, as Spotify's poll does, and sends `v` and the volume byte on `/tmp/q2radio.sock` when `g_volume` changes, so stock's volume (wheel, dialog, screen off) reaches the soft gain. `radio_media`, next to `spot_media` after stock's key-lock filter, takes Play/Pause (stop, or play again), 222 and 223 (the previous or next station, round the ends) on any page while the radio is the active source: it played last and no local track, video or Spotify has played since. Stopping (`radio_halt`) sends `q` and waits up to `RADIO_QUIT_MS` (1.5 s), then `kill -9` on its pid (librespot also uses q2video), and removes the state file. `mclStartPlayer`'s hook, `play_video`, `spot_take` and Home's Rockbox row call `radio_stop`, which also hands the keys back. Nothing starts at boot.

**q2video -r** (`radio` in `patch/video.c`) opens the PCM and readies the DAC as Videos does (`sound_open`), then runs stock ffmpeg with `-loglevel verbose -icy 1 -reconnect 1 -reconnect_streamed 1 -rw_timeout 15000000 -i URL -vn -ac 2 -ar 48000 -f s16le pipe:4` (HTTPS works: ffmpeg's `tls_verify` is 0), the sound to the same writer thread, its stderr read a byte at a time on the main loop's poll. libavformat's http logs `Metadata update for StreamTitle: …` only at verbose; the input dump gives `icy-br` and `Stream #0:0: Audio: CODEC`. `meta` takes a line's value after `KEY :`, so `icy-notice2 : … Network Audio Server` is no codec. The state file is rewritten whole (`.tmp`, renamed) on each change: `state=` `connecting`, `playing` (once the writer has played a frame) or `error`, `title=` (the StreamTitle, which the page splits at `-` into artist and song), `codec=` (upper case), `bitrate=` (kbit/s), `error=` (when `sound_open` found no device: `open:` or `params:` and `snd_strerror`'s reason, which the page shows as "Can't play: …"; Bluetooth and USB get 50 tries, 5 s, the DAC 20, at open and again at `snd_pcm_set_params` while it is busy, since bluealsa claims its PCM there; or `write:` when a write fails past `snd_pcm_recover`, which ends that ffmpeg run, as it ends Spotify's sink; Videos drop such a write instead, so their picture goes on) and `at=` (`CLOCK_MONOTONIC` ms the sound started, for the elapsed time). When ffmpeg ends anyway it starts again after 1 s, 2 s, … up to `RADIO_TRIES` (5) starts in a row without sound, then writes `state=error` and exits. A start with no frame written after `RADIO_SILENT_MS` (20 s) means the output is stuck (the writer may be blocked inside ALSA, so it can't be joined): it writes `state=error` with `error=no sound from the output` (or the writer's), kills ffmpeg and exits, so the page never stays on "Connecting…". `q` kills ffmpeg, unlinks the state, closes the PCM and mutes the DAC. No FM: the Q2 has no tuner (the device tree's only i2c device is the axp2101, and stock hciplayer was built with `--disable-radio`).

**Page.** `radionp_page` has Spotify's Now Playing layout: "Internet Radio" above, the row's icon unscaled in the art box, the station's list name, the StreamTitle's artist and song, then the codec and bitrate ("Connecting…", "Stopped", "Can't play: REASON" or "Can't play this station" instead). Local Now Playing's heart sits at its place (x 320 in Legacy, 303 in iPod, y 176, 50 px, `play_fav` / `play_unfav` centred): filled when the station playing is in `favourites.m3u`, which is read again only when `rd.serial` changes (each start and each toggle), and a tap toggles it as the held Play/Pause does (`fav_toggle`), with the same toast and the elapsed time, refreshed on a 500 ms timer idle with the screen off. It is not in `contexts.inc`, so the wheel is stock's volume and Centre stock's screen toggle. ponytail: no station logos; the directory's m3u has none, and a logo would cost another fetch and a decode per station.

Checks in `test/peq.py` (host builds): `meta` on ffmpeg's lines; `playlist` on CRLF, BOM, radio-browser's extra tags, `.pls` keys and the limit; `choices` on both CSVs and quoted commas; `url_escape`. `test/patch.py`: the row and its icon, empty favourites, Featured, a 1.0.1 seed removed and an edited one kept, the pull-down's search box, its query, its empty result and its cancel, the pull gesture on `radio_page` in both builds, the headphone output readied before the fork, the error's reason, the save from Now Playing and its heart, the favourites from every playlist, the child's `execl` argv after `player_stop`, `.last`, the page's labels connecting and playing, the held timers, the volume datagram, the media keys and the side buttons round the ends, `mclStartPlayer`'s hand-over, the kill after `RADIO_QUIT_MS`, the error, the favourites toggle and its latched release, Top Stations' fetch and TLS options, No Wi-Fi, genres with an escaped tag, countries by code, a failed fetch and Return through every level.

## Tidal cache

Stock's Tidal stream thread (unnamed, after `tidalGetFavoritesIdsThd` at `0x4863a0`) waits for `tidalfurlstart`, which `get_tidal_trackurl` sets after copying the record's song id into `tidalsongid`. It copies that id to its own `malloc` in `$s2` (`0x48647c`), sets `tidalStreamingLevelNow` from `tidalStreamingLevelNext` (at most 3), and asks `playbackinfopostpaywall` for that quality. It then base64-decodes the manifest (`toolsBase64Decode`) and passes its `urls[0]`, a direct file URL, to `mcl_tidalStartPlayer` (`0x5ad240`, called only at `0x486d88`), which sends it to hciplayer. A skip during the fetch rewrites `tidalsongid` but not the thread's copy.

Its idle poll (the `jalr` at `0x486404`) is `ringnav_tidal_poll` (`patch/tidal.c`), which waits on a semaphore with no timer, screen on or off, whatever plays. Only `get_tidal_trackurl` (`0x483188`, store at `0x483278`) sets `tidalfurlstart`, and stock's loop polls before it reads the flag, so `ringnav_tidal_track` posts the semaphore once stock's `get_tidal_trackurl` has returned: the thread wakes once per track asked for (a queue restored at power-on, a pick, the next track), fetches it, and waits again at its next poll. A post made during a fetch is counted, so it only adds one pass that finds the flag clear. Baidu's stock thread (`baidu_pthread_playurl`, poll at `0x47f824`) calls `ringnav_baidu_poll`, which waits on a semaphore nothing posts. The thread is not exited, since a `pthread_exit` from the payload's frames has no unwind tables to walk.

`mcl_tidalStartPlayer` is hooked by `tidal_start` (`patch/tidal.c`), two instructions that pass `$s2` on as the song id; the build pins the `$s2` set-up and the call. With the extension of the URL's path (`.flac`, or `.mp4` kept as `.m4a`, which stock's file lists take), the card's `/mnt/mmc/.tidal/<id>-<quality> <artist> - <title><ext>` (`tidal_name`: the queue row with that id at `REC_TIDAL_ID` names it; FAT's reserved characters become `_`, and a long label is cut at a whole UTF-8 character) plays in the URL's place when it exists, found by its `<id>-<quality>` prefix and extension. Otherwise the URL plays, and a detached pthread downloads it with demo's libcurl to `.tmp`, flushes and syncs it, and renames it into place on HTTP 200. A skip (a `tidal_play` with another id; a re-fetch of the same id leaves its download running) or Stop aborts it from curl's progress callback, and one under 1 KB/s for 30 s gives up, so the `.tmp` is removed. Stop is inferred, not signalled: the callback reads stock's play status (`mclGetPlayStatus`, 1 stopped) and aborts once it has read stopped for `TIDAL_STOP_MS` (3 s) running, so the stopped state between queued tracks keeps the copy; ponytail: a stream that sits stopped longer than that before it plays drops its copy (cached on a later play). Only one download runs at a time: a track that starts while a skipped one's transfer is still ending leaves its URL as the job the same thread takes next, the latest track's only, and none is queued below `card_space`'s 16 MB. After each download the oldest files by mtime are removed until `.tidal` holds at most the cap chosen in [Streaming configuration](#streaming-configuration) (2 GB by default), read once per trim. Peer verification is off: the firmware's CA bundle lacks Tidal's CDN roots, and hciplayer streams the same URL unverified (ffmpeg's `tls_verify=0`). Tidal's files carry no tags, so before the rename the copy is remuxed by stock `/usr/bin/ffmpeg` (`tag`: `-c copy` to `<file>.tmp.tag.tmp`, renamed over the `.tmp` on exit 0 with a non-empty file) with the queue row's title, artist and album; a FLAC also gets the row's cover (`REC_COVER`, `+0x28`, the `/tmp/tidal/*.jpg` that `player_get_id3info` hands the cover thread as type 2), fitted to 640 px by `thumb` into `/tmp/.tidal_cover.jpg` as the job starts, since stock removes a page's covers when another opens, as an attached picture. A track played from Tidal Downloads then shows its artist and art from its own tags. ponytail: no art in an `.m4a`, as ffmpeg 4.2's mp4 muxer writes no cover; a row whose cover file is already gone (a queue restored at power-on) gets tags but no art; any ffmpeg failure keeps the untagged copy; copies cached before this stay untagged until Clear All Downloads; the remux writes the file once more on the card. Only the URL sent to the player changes; the queue record, art and Now Playing are stock's. The API request still runs first, so a cached track started from Tidal's pages needs Wi-Fi but streams nothing. **Streaming → Tidal Downloads** (`tidal_downloads` in navigation.c) lists `.tidal` through `toolsLoadDirectory`, newest file first, by the label after the prefix, and folder-plays a row as local music, so it needs no Wi-Fi; its page name starts `tidal_`, so the playback keys stay with it (`media_focused`). ponytail: oldest download goes first (demo imports no `utime` to touch a replayed file); a `.tmp` left by a power-off stays until that track is cached again, or Clear All Downloads. The list ends in **Clear All Downloads…** (`td_clear`), which, from a 0 ms timer as a row's click may not rebuild its list (`td_show`), puts **Remove Downloads?** in the list's place (the track count, Cancel, Remove), worded as Spotify's Storage Management; Cancel or Return (`td_keyup`) lists the songs again, on the Clear row. Remove (`tidal_clear`, tidal.c) unlinks every name in `.tidal` not starting with a dot: finished copies and `.tmp` ones, nothing outside the folder; `.cache-size` stays. There is no index to keep in step, as the list is `toolsLoadDirectory` of the folder, read again after. It runs under `td.lock`, drops a queued job and counts `td.cleared`; the download thread renames its finished `.tmp` into place under the same lock only if that count hasn't moved since its job began, so a download running across the clear is dropped rather than left behind. A play after it caches as usual. ponytail: on the UI thread, holding the lock, a moment for a full 8 GB cache. The confirmation is the same page rebuilt, not a pushed one: a new runtime window would need its own `contexts.inc` entry, builder allowance and close handling.

Checks in `test/peq.py` (host build): `tidal_ext` on a query string, a dot only in the query, a trailing dot and an extension too long for the buffer; `trim`'s eviction at 2 GB, 4 GB and 512 MB caps and a failed `unlink`.

## Tidal track lists

Stock keeps a Tidal track list in `stTidalTracks` (16 bytes: total, limit, offset, the record deque). `init_infodata` (`0x4d9888`), run as the album/playlist page (`tidal_albuminfo`) opens, clears the deque and sets limit 50, offset 0. `tidalPlaylistDetailThd` (`0x48b3d8`, `playlists/%s/items?...offset=%d&limit=%d`) and `tidalAlbumDetailThd` (`0x48b59c`, `albums/%u/tracks?offset=%d&limit=%d`) request one page from the struct's offset and limit into `/tmp/tidal_<thread>` (`httpRequest`; the thread's own id) and set `tidal_albuminfo_thread_run` to 2. The page's timer polls that flag, then parses the file (`parseTidalPlaylistDetailJson` `0x48b8b0` or `parseTidalAlbumDetailJson` `0x48c500`, by the pthread id it started), which appends the records and writes back `totalNumberOfItems` and the reply's `limit`, removes it and lists the deque. Neither page ever asks for another page, so a list stopped at 50. My Collection's Tracks (`tidalColTracksThd`, `0x4882d4`, `users/%u/favorites/tracks?...limit=50&offset=%d`, its offset the thread's argument, done flag `tidal_collection_thread_run`) does page, parsed the same way (`parseTidalColTracksJson`, `0x48a300`): the page's scroll handler (`0x4d68a8`) adds 50 to the offset and starts the thread again when a scroll ends near the bottom of a list with pages left. That takes a touch drag to the end; inferred from the issue's report (favourites stop at 50), not traced, the wheel's selection moves don't reach it.

The three threads are hooked (`ringnav_tidal_playlist`, `ringnav_tidal_album`, `ringnav_tidal_favs`, `patch/tidal.c`) and their done stores (`0x48b57c`, `0x48b70c`, `0x488434`, `sb $3,0($2)`) become `nop`s (`TIDAL_PAGE_DONE`). `tidal_pages` runs the stock thread and parses its reply itself with the page's parser, so the deque and total grow per page (without that parse the deque never grew, so 1.4.8's first build stopped at 50), then, while the offset plus the limit is short of the total, adds the limit to the offset (in the struct, and as the argument for favourites) and runs it again; after the last page the hook writes `{"items":[]}` over the reply and sets the done flag, so the page's own parse adds nothing (it returns 1 on an empty `items`) and lists the whole deque once. A first page that fails to parse is left for the page, so its error handling (`subStatus` 11003, a sign-in that ran out) is stock's; a later one ends the run with what loaded. Favourites leave the offset at the last page's, so the scroll handler's check (page `offset/limit + 1` against `ceil(total/limit)`) finds no page left. A run ends early when a page adds no record (a failed request: the parser leaves the deque alone) or the struct's offset is no longer the one it set (`init_infodata` reset it: the page was opened again). ponytail: `TIDAL_MAX_TRACKS` (2,000) caps a list at 40 sequential requests, the page's spinner meanwhile; a page opened again while a request is in flight can still take that page's records, as stock's single request could. Limits: still 50 a request, stock's own size (Tidal's maximum per request isn't known here); favourite albums, artists and playlists and the user's playlist list keep stock's 50 and touch paging.

## Streaming configuration

Streaming's last row, **Configuration** (`stream_config` in navigation.c, `stream_config.png`: `assets/icons/stream_config.png`, a grey disc with a sliders glyph whose strokes stay crisp at the 40px rows (stock's `system_netservice` globe frayed there), with `list_tidal`'s inode metadata, resized on iPod as the other Streaming icons), opens `streamconf_page`, a `page_list` of three rows and then a check row per service. It and Tidal Downloads are listed in `patch/contexts.inc` (`CTX_DYNAMIC`), so the wheel moves the selection and Centre presses the row, as on the other payload lists.

**Cache sizes.** Tidal Cache and Spotify Cache step through 512 MB, 1 GB, 2 GB, 4 GB and 8 GB (`512 << i` MB) on each press. Each is a file, `.tidal/.cache-size` and `.spotify/.cache-size`, holding `<MB>M` (`2048M`), librespot's `--cache-size-limit` form; the folder is made if missing and the file written with `blob_io` (a `.tmp`, synced and renamed). `stream_cache` reads it back with `state_read`; missing, or anything but those five sizes, is 2 GB. Files, not `config.ini`: `trim` reads Tidal's on the download thread, and the card's Spotify launcher reads Spotify's. The dot keeps Tidal's out of `trim`'s count and `cached`'s matches, and Tidal Downloads lists only songs. A smaller Tidal cap applies at the next download's `trim`. The launcher (q2-librespot's `contrib/shanlingq2/launcher.sh`) reads `.cache-size` before each librespot start, falling back to `2G` when it is empty or not digits with at most one trailing `K`, `M` or `G`, so a new Spotify size applies when librespot next starts. Older launchers ignore the file and keep `2G`.

**Stop Streaming Services** (`sc_click`) runs `spot_stop` (`SPOT_KILL`: the restart loop, librespot and its `aplay`, the state and `SPOT_OUT` files; `sp` cleared, so `spot_poll` idles, `spot_status` is 0 and the keys and `media_owner` are local music's), `radio_stop` (q2video `-r` quit or killed, `rd.active` 0) and, when the queue is a stock stream (`MCL_TYPE` neither 1 nor `0xf0xx`: Tidal) and hciplayer isn't stopped, `player_stop`. A local queue is left as it is; the next local track readies the DAC itself (`player_start`). It toasts "Streaming stopped" and is harmless with nothing running. Without it, a paused librespot stays the active source (`sp.active`) until a local track plays or `SPOT_IDLE_MS` passes, so Play/Pause, the side buttons, Bluetooth controls and the status bar follow Spotify, and a phone that casts again stops local music (`spot_take`). ponytail: AirPlay's receiver keeps running; its switch is on Network service.

**Shown services.** After Stop Streaming Services, a blank row (a bare `list_item_create`, no click, so the wheel passes over it) leaves a gap above one switch row per service `ringnav_stream` lists (`svc`: stock's TIDAL row, Spotify, the card apps in `addon_scan`'s order, Tidal Downloads, Internet Radio, AirPlay), each with Shanling's `switch_on` (shown) or `switch_off` (`list_toggle`, at screen x 302 as the settings rows'). Centre (`svc_toggle`) flips it and saves the hidden ones in `Q2POD STREAMHIDE` in `config.ini`, comma separated: a fixed row's upper-case key (`TIDAL`, `SPOTIFY`, `DOWNLOADS`, `RADIO`, `AIRPLAY`) or a card app's id, which is lower case, so the two never collide and a hidden app stays hidden when another app changes its place. A missing key hides nothing. `ringnav_stream` tags each row it builds with its service (`SVC_PROP`, `ringnav_svc`) and hides the saved ones with `widget_set_visible`; a toggle finds the Streaming page underneath (`scroll_view_streamsset` from `window_manager`) and shows or hides the tagged row there too, then lays the list out again, so Return shows the change. Configuration has no switch and always stays. The comma list is `csv_find`/`csv_toggle`, shared with Spotify's Remove selection; an empty key is never added. ponytail: an uninstalled app's id stays in the key (the app is hidden again if reinstalled); once the value would pass `ROOT_MAX` (200 bytes, far beyond the five fixed keys and eight app ids; not the 260-byte buffer, as stock's config line is a 257-byte `sprintf` and a 255-byte `fgets`), a toggle that hides refuses with "Could not save setting".

Checks in `test/patch.py`: the row and its icon, the page's labels, each cache row's step, wrap and file, `stream_cache` on a size not in the list, every payload list page on the wheel's allowlist, and the stop row's kill, `player_stop` for a stream queue, its toast, and radio and Spotify no longer owning the keys; the service rows' ticks, `STREAMHIDE` and the Streaming rows they hide, at once and on the next open.

## Album artists

Stock has two artist settings. `artist_type` (`0xa38acc`, `PLAYSET ARTISTTYPE`, read by `read_artistype_value`) is the artist page's own switch (`btn_artistswitch`), which picks its albums and songs by album artist (`getAlbumByAlbumArtist`, `getMusicByAlbumAndAlbumSonger`) or by artist. `g_artist_type` (`0xa38ae4`) picks `GetAllAlbumArtist` for the Artists list and the album-artist queries for an artist's albums in `load_localclass_list`, but nothing in stock writes it, so Artists always grouped by the Artist tag and a guest artist split an album. `ringnav_localclass` copies `artist_type` into `g_artist_type` before every list load, so Artists, an artist's albums, the artist page and the [Queue menu](#queue-menu) follow one setting. `GetAllAlbumArtist`'s row callback (`0x4fbc28`) fills the name at `+0x18` as `getAllArtist`'s does, and also at `+0x24`, so the rows show and expand the same way. **Audio settings → Artists** (`ringnav_audioset`, after stock's 15 rows in `scroll_view_playset`) toggles `artist_type` and writes `PLAYSET ARTISTTYPE`, as the artist page's switch does; the lists take it on their next load. Album Artist is the default: stock's read, `read_artistype_value` (`0x4f4724`, `atoi` of the key with `"0"` as its default), is replaced by `ringnav_read_artistype`, which reads a missing key as 1. Stock calls it only from `localmusic_localclass_page_init` and `localmusic_artistinfo_page_init`, so until a Library list or artist page opened, `artist_type` stayed 0 for Audio settings' row, boot resume (`memeory_startplayer`) and an artist page opened from Coverflow; `ringnav_bootmode` (`config_init`'s play-mode call, before Home restores the queue) reads it once at boot as well. Stock's `config.ini` template has no `ARTISTTYPE`, and only the two toggles write it, so a saved Artist (0) stays Artist. Stock's album-artist queries (`GetAllAlbumArtist`'s `group by albumsonger`, an artist's albums' and songs' `albumsonger like '%s'` and `albumsonger=""`, search) only match the tag, so in Album Artist mode an album without one was missing from its artist and listed under Unknown Artist. `ringnav_library_query` (the `toolsQueryDbTable` hook) rewrites every `albumsonger` in those queries to `ifnull(nullif(albumsonger,''),songer)`, as `ringnav_content_query` does on `toolsQueryContentDbTable` (`0x5c863c`, the same with a row argument and the name sort on the stack), which an artist's albums (`getAlbumByAlbumArtist`) run through, so such a song goes by its artist on both tabs; Unknown Artist keeps only songs with neither tag, its albums as its songs. `select *` records keep the real tag at `+0x24`, and Coverflow's own grouping (`ALBUM_SQL`, recognised by its `ifnull(albumsonger`) is left as is, so an untagged compilation stays one album there. Stock's libtag reads a Vorbis album artist only as `ALBUMARTIST`, so FLACs tagged `ALBUM ARTIST` (foobar2000's spelling) had an empty `albumsonger` and listed under Unknown Artist. `toolsGetMusicInfo` (`0x5c5f40`, called by the library scan, `player_get_id3info` and `get_songid3_info`) is hooked: when stock leaves its album artist (`info + 0x580`, `MUSIC_INFO_ALBUM_ARTIST`) empty, `ringnav_music_info` reads a `.flac`'s VORBIS_COMMENT block itself, seeking past the other metadata blocks, and copies `ALBUM ARTIST`, at most 255 bytes. Ogg Vorbis and Opus are not read.

**Artists rows.** Library Artists, Composers and Genres share one row template (built at `0x4b0efc`, bound at `0x4b0bc0`); each bind calls `0x4afc14` (`class_icon`), which sets `img_icon` to `default_artist_small` for Artists, a genre icon for Genres, or a `.downpic` picture. That call (`CLASS_ICON`, `0x4b0cec`) is a `jal` to `ringnav_class_icon`, which hides `img_icon` on Artists so the name takes its width (iPod's `compact_row_layout` skips hidden siblings; Stock keeps its 200 px label) and shows it again, through stock's setter, on the others.

## Charge Limit

The charger is the AXP2101 PMIC's integrated one (the device tree's `x-powers,axp2101-power-supply`, driven by `drivers/power/supply/axp2101_charger.c`, which registers `battery` and `axp2101_charger`; there is no bq25890 driver in the kernel or rootfs), set to 1000 mA of charge current, the 2000 mA USB input limit (`pmu_usbpc_cur`), a 4.35 V target (`pmu_init_chgvol` 0x10fe) and 100 mA of termination (`pmu_iterm_limit` 0x64). The fuel gauge is the CW2015 on I2C-0 (`/sys/devices/i2c-0/0-0062/cw2015_capacity`, `module_driver/cw2015.sh`). `toolsGetChargeStatus` (`0x5bca7c`) reads `/sys/class/power_supply/battery/status` (1 Charging, 2 Full, -1 Discharging or anything else, 0 unreadable); the `bq25890_1_status` paths it probes and the `echo %d > .../bq25890_1_otg_enable` helper are for the other Shanling players that share `demo`. `switch_charge_enable(on)` (`0x4f3cfc`) sets the active-low charge enable on GPIO `PE22` through `toolsGpioSetValue` (libgpio, no shell); stock uses it only for USB mode's and AirPlay's charge choice (`g_usbdac_chargeflag`; `usbmode_page_init`, `usbmode_exit`, `airplay_page_init`). With it high, charging is off and the player runs from USB while the battery holds. `get_battery_capacity` (`0x4f4afc`) keeps `g_power_capacity` (0-100) and `g_power_chargestate` (a negative status is not stored).

**System settings → Power → Charge Limit** (`ringnav_powermanager`, after stock's rows in `scroll_view_powermanager`; `Q2POD CHARGELIMIT` in `config.ini`). Every `CHARGE_POLL_MS` (5 s) `charge_poll`, from `ringnav_sleep`, stops charging at `CHARGE_STOP` (80%), stops it again if anything turned it back on meanwhile (USB mode's exit, AirPlay) while the charger reports charging, and hands it back at `CHARGE_RESUME` (75%) or when the limit is turned off: on, or `g_usbdac_chargeflag` while `usbmode_page` or `airplay_page` is open. ponytail: only while demo runs; a powered-off Q2 charges as stock.

## Low Power

**System settings → Power → Low Power** (`Q2POD LOWPOWER`). The kernel (5.10, SMP) has CPU hotplug but no cpufreq core, so the clock cannot be lowered; Low Power saves where it can without touching what you hear or see. While the screen is off and no video plays, CPU1 goes offline (`/sys/devices/system/cpu/cpu1/online`); the player decodes on one core, which plain playback, PEQ included, does not need two for. With the screen on, both cores are up, so Coverflow's software renderer and the UI never compete with hciplayer (`nice -5`, not real-time). The first offline of a boot is bracketed by `/mnt/data/q2pod-cpu1`: written and `fsync`ed before, unlinked and the directory `fsync`ed after. If a kernel stalled on it, the next boot finds the file, renames it `.bad` and never tries again (delete it to try again); a marker that cannot be synced means no offline that boot. An offline the kernel refuses stops the attempts until the next boot; a refused online is retried once a second. Off, no file is touched.

## Coverflow sort

**System settings → Display → Coverflow → Coverflow Sort** (a `pod_rows` row after Artist Albums, `POD_CF_SORT`; the library's covers had a Sort card for it, and a Refresh library card, which 1.4.2 moved to Display as Coverflow Sort and Refresh Coverflow, so nothing but albums follows the last one) cycles **Album** (stock order), **Artist**, **Year**, **Recently Added**, **Most Played** and **Random** (`coverflow_sort_step`), saved in `/mnt/data/ringnav-coversort` (an int, through `blob_io`, so Refresh keeps it); the row's label reads the file (`coverflow_sort_name`). A press sets `cf.resort`, and Coverflow's next open (`load`) reads the order again and sorts its kept list, so a Coverflow page open under Settings keeps its order until then. The stock album list is still queried and kept as before (`cf.stock`); the shown list is a sorted copy of it. Artist, Year and Recently Added rank albums by `toolsQueryDbTable(db, sql, row, 0)` with `split_row` over Coverflow's `ALBUM_SQL` grouping with an `ORDER BY`: `ifnull(max(year),0)=0, max(year), album` (oldest first) or the same with `max(year) desc` (newest first) for Artist's year order and Year, as Playback settings' **Year Order** (Q2POD `YEAROLD`, `ringnav_year_old`; a change re-sorts on the next open), undated last, and `max(time_create) desc` for Recently Added (the 0 skips stock's name sort, `0x5c8120`). Rows are matched back to the album rows by `album_cmp`, as the grouping is. Artist then sorts by artist without a leading article (`ringnav_sort_key`, as [Sorting](#sorting)), then that year rank. Most Played sums the [play counts](#play-counts) of each album's songs (`album_plays`, one pass over `getAllMusic`). Random ranks each album by `rand_below` (`patch/playback.c`) with no query, so it reshuffles whenever the list is rebuilt: choosing it, Refresh, a library change or a reboot. Ties and albums without a rank keep the stock order; the Unknown card stays last. A new order queries only the ranking. The rated albums' covers (Library → Ratings → Albums) keep their own Sort card, last, which cycles Rating, Album, Artist, Year and Most Played (`rated_sorts`, appended so saved values keep their order) in `ringnav-ratedsort`, stays centred when pressed, and queries only the ranking. An artist's page orders its albums the same way: `ringnav_artist_list` takes the artist's split albums (`artist_albums`), ranks them by `coverflow_by_year` (the same year query, or `album COLLATE NOCASE` with Playback settings' **Artist Album Order** at A–Z, Q2POD `ARTISTAZ`, matched to that short list), and restacks the grid's rows to match (`artist_order`, rows it cannot match last) before the art keys are made; the artist Coverflow gets that list as is.

## Artist Albums in Coverflow

**Display → Coverflow → Artist Albums** (`Q2POD ARTISTCF`, a `pod_rows` toggle, the sub-page's first row in both builds) set to Coverflow (the default, with no key) hands an artist's albums to Coverflow. `ringnav_artist_list` already copies the albums tab's list (class `0xff01`) and `coverflow_split`s it for the cover keys; the layout copy is filtered by album identity against the selected artist's songs (`getMusicByAlbumArtist` or `getMusicBySonger`, following `artist_type`), so another artist's same-named album cannot leak in. The unfiltered split stays available for the artwork keys.
