The filtered copy goes to a 0 ms timer, and when the tab loads on a top `artistinfo_page`, `coverflow_artist` opens `coverflow_page` over it with that list as `cf.albums` (the library's list kept aside in `cf.all_albums` and put back on close). Until then the artist page is veiled (`artist_cf_veil`): opacity 0 and its `anim_hint` cleared before its first paint, so the Artists list stays until Coverflow cuts in, as Home's card opens it, instead of the grid sliding in and showing for a frame; with the covers up, its opacity and slide come back for its own Return. In this mode (`cf.scoped`, `ARTIST_ALBUMS`) `CARDS` is 0 (no Sort or Refresh) for one album, else 1: **All albums**, last, with the placeholder art and no caption under its name. Centre on it runs `play_all`: each cover's `coverflow_album_tracks` (the cover's own album, disc then track order, as its track list) appended in the covers' order into one deque, played from its first song through `play_folder` as a library queue (`0xf001`, as Most Played's: `0xff10` resume would rebuild one album of the name), so Now Playing opens and the play mode and shuffle state go as for an album; a missing first file shows "Storage unavailable" instead. A Play/Pause hold on it opens nothing (`coverflow_album` is albums only). The List view (`text_albums`) has no card. Further, the stock list and its library generation are left alone, the last album isn't saved or restored, the fast-turn letter jump is off, and Return on the covers closes the artist page under them (`window_manager_close_window_force`, unanimated; its stock Return, `on_return_onclick`, is only `navigator_back`, its cleanup on `EVT_WINDOW_CLOSE`) before `navigator_back`, so it lands on the Artists list. The artist page that got Coverflow is remembered (`art_cf_win`), so should it come back some other way, the tab's reload leaves the stock grid. A Coverflow already open (the one a song was played from, under Now Playing's Go to Artist or Ratings' Albums, issue #73) is closed once the new page is up (`scoped_open`, both scoped openers), along with an artist page right under it (`artist_under`, as Return does), `closed` run on it at once and its later `EVT_DESTROY` ignored (its ctx is no longer `cf.page`), since the covers keep one page's state; ponytail: Return from Now Playing then skips it. An artist with no albums keeps the grid.

Go to Album opens through the same `scoped_open` as a one-album page (`ALBUM_TRACKS`): `cf.albums` is a one-record copy of the song's record and the page goes straight to its tracks with no covers under them, so Return (`keyup`) closes it with `navigator_back` instead of showing covers. Playing a track is the covers' `play`, `play_folder` at `0xff10`, so Shuffle Songs' mode comes back as from any other load (`ringnav_load`).

Set to Grid, on iPod, every tab load on a top `artistinfo_page` runs `artist_bar` first: if the hidden `view_navbar` is still hidden (Legacy's always shows), it shows and enables it and moves `tab_control` down by the navbar's height, shrinking it by the same amount. `pages`' self layout and the fixed-height `list_view_album` lose those pixels too. Stock's page code builds the navbar's children (`s_img_mulsel`, `s_btn_singersong_navbar`) and runs multi-select (`g_navbar_status`); its open handler (`0x4ae6ec`) clears that state each time the page opens. Limit: multi-select is touch only. Nothing routes the hardware Return to stock's cancel, so a Return mid-selection is expected to leave the page (this is inferred, not traced); reopening the page starts clean.

## Hold Return

**Display → Controls → Hold Return: Home** (`Q2POD HOLDRETURN`, [ipod.md](ipod.md#hold-return)) sends a hold to stock `navigator_back_to_home`, which closes windows and opens none: with a modal dialog on top it calls `dialog_quit` and goes on once the dialog is destroyed, otherwise it closes the stack from an idle. That is the call stock's own long Return made at that point, so the payload leaves `RETURN_RELEASE_LATCH` armed for stock's release filter, unlike the switch to Now Playing, which clears it. This relies on the hold's key-up reaching that filter once the windows close: the release is swallowed and the next short Return passes; clearing the latch instead would let the release act on Home (on iPod, select or open Now Playing). Over quick settings, `ringnav` leaves a Return release to that filter while the latch is armed, before its own close (`dialog_quit`), so the release cannot close the dialog and leave the latch for the next Return.

## Play counts

The Resume poll also counts listens, for every track but [Podcasts and Audiobooks](#podcasts-and-audiobooks). It adds the seconds heard: a step of 1 or 2 s between polls, so seeks, pauses and Resume's jump add nothing. Once a track longer than `LISTEN_MIN_S` (30 s) has had half its length, or `LISTEN_MAX_S` (4 minutes), heard, its path's FNV-1a hash (with a CUE track's start mixed in, as a CUE image's tracks share one path) is counted, once per play; a new key (another track, or another CUE track of the same image) or the time falling back under 2 s from past 30 s (repeat one, or a seek to the start) starts a new play. The counts are a fixed table of `PLAYS_SLOTS` (512) hash/count pairs, 4 KB, in `/mnt/data/ringnav-plays`, read on first use and written whole like Resume's ring. A counted track moves first; with no free slot, the furthest back of the lowest counts, the least recently played, is replaced. The file never grows, whatever the library's size. Keys are paths, so counts survive Update Local Music and a moved or renamed file starts again from zero.

**Most Played** (Local Music, under Shuffle) runs `getAllMusic` through `staged` as Shuffle Songs does, looks each song's path hash up in the table and lists the top `PLAYS_TOP` (100) on a runtime `mostplayed_page` (`page_list`, as Books', in 64 px `page_row_detail` rows: the title over "Artist · N plays", or the count alone when untagged, in 16 px `#AAAAAA`, stock's `s_scrlabel_gray24l` grey), most played first and, among equal counts, the most recently counted first (the table's order). A row folder-plays that ranked `stSongInfo` deque from itself and leaves the play mode alone. A Play/Pause hold opens the song menu over the same deque, as on Coverflow's tracks; for that the page is `CTX_FIXED`, so it also comes back on the last row. Nothing counted titles the page "No plays yet". The ranked deque is kept after the page closes, and a song counted while it is closed that is not in it is appended (up to 2 × `PLAYS_TOP`), so a later open ranks only those, not the library. The library is read again after Coverflow's `library_gen` moves (scan or delete), when the deque would grow past that or such a song is counted with the page open, or when a full table drops the count of a song in it, since the next best could be any song. A song first counted from stock's folder browser has that queue's record, without library tags, until then.

## Ratings

A song has 0 to 5 hearts. Five is stock's My Fav (the `myfav` table, `checkFavExist`), so Now Playing's heart, the queue menu's Favourites rows and the Loved list stay one value; 1 to 4 are kept in `/mnt/data/ringnav-ratings`, `RATE_SLOTS` (4096) words, each a song's `listen_key` (the [play counts](#play-counts)' path hash) with its low three bits replaced by the hearts, 0 for a free slot. The table is `calloc`'d and read whole on first use and written whole through `blob_io` on each change; a full table refuses a new rating with a toast rather than drop one.

**Now Playing.** `playing_page_init` is hooked in both builds (`ringnav_playing`; Legacy's does only this). Stock binds `img_fav`'s click to `0x52b154` with the window as context: for a local track it runs `manage_myfav` (`0x516cd8`, My Fav toggled for the queue's playing index) and swaps the heart's image. `rate_bind` finds that binding in the heart's emitter, takes it off and binds its own click and `EVT_POINTER_DOWN`. A press arms `RATE_HOLD_MS` (500 ms); if the pointer is still down then, the picker opens, sends the window `EVT_POINTER_ABORT` (as pull-to-search does) so the `slide_view` under the finger lets go and cannot drag pages behind the picker, and the release's click is dropped. The heart's click returns 0, as stock's does: after a child's STOP stock's `widget_on_pointer_up` skips the parents' own release, so the `slide_view` kept the window's grab, and the seek bar and More took no taps until the page closed. The queue menu's "Queue unchanged" pick returns 0 for the same reason (its list's `scroll_view`). A tap runs stock's click and then clears any 1-4 kept for the track, so a heart taken away leaves the song unrated. A label on the heart's top right shows a kept 1-4 while the heart is empty (`play_unfav`), refreshed every 500 ms with the screen on; it is insensitive, so the heart takes the taps under it.

**Picker.** A `LETTER_ALPHA` rounded panel in the stock dark surface, built once per page over its centre, holds a caption ("N of 5", "Unrated") and five 56 px images of the heart's own `play_fav` and `play_unfav`. While it shows, `ringnav` sends it the wheel (±1 within 0-5), Centre (save) and Return (close unsaved), ahead of stock and the page's own keys; tapping heart _k_ saves _k_. Closing hides the panel and makes it insensitive: stock's `widget_find_target_default` (`0x669c6c`) skips only children without enable and sensitive (flags `0x9`), not hidden ones, so a hidden panel would keep taking every touch over the cover until the page was left. Saving keeps 1-4 or nothing, and calls the heart's stock click when My Fav has to change (5 in, anything else out). **Hold Play/Pause** on local Now Playing puts **Rate** first in the queue menu, with the hearts, opening the same picker (`QA_RATE`).

**Lists.** Library's **Ratings** row (`library_into`, stock's `local_fav` icon) takes My Fav's place, whose stock row stays hidden next to it. It opens `ratings_page` with Top Rated (4-5), Loved (5), All Rated (1-5) and Disliked (1). Each runs `getAllMusic(0)` (title order) and `getAllFav` (`0x4fef24`, `select * from myfav` through the same row callback, `0x4fb8b8`) through `staged`, rates every song against the fav keys and the kept ones, and lists the matches most hearts first on `rated_page`, as Most Played's: "♥♥♥♡♡ · Artist" under the title, a row playing the list from itself as a library queue, the queue menu on a Play/Pause hold (`CTX_FIXED`). ponytail: every song against every rated one; fine for thousands rated, a sorted table if not.

**Albums.** An album has its own 0 to 5 hearts in `/mnt/data/ringnav-album-ratings`, the same `RATE_SLOTS` word table (5 included, an album having no stock favourite), keyed by `album_id` (coverflow.c): an FNV-1a hash of what `album_cmp` compares, the name and album artist ASCII-lowercased, else the name and the album folder (`album_dir`, disc folders as one, case kept); the Unknown Album card is one album. A slot whose hearts read 6 or 7 counts as unrated, and a short file reads as empty. A save writes the table whole; a failed write puts the slot back and toasts "Rating not saved", a full table "Ratings are full". The queue menu gives an album **Add/Remove from Favourites** (5 or 0) and **Rate Album** with its hearts (`qm_album_id`): a Coverflow cover's record, a Library Albums row (split as Coverflow's), or an artist's albums tab row, whose split album `ringnav_artist_list` finds as for art keys and keeps in `art_ids`. Rate Album opens the picker over that page (`rate_panel`, found by name, so Now Playing's and Coverflow's each have one); saving runs `qm_record` again and saves nothing ("Album changed") unless the page is still top with the same row or centred cover. The picker's state clears with its panel or page. A Play/Pause hold while it is up is latched and ignored. **Ratings → Albums** (after Loved) is `coverflow_rated`: `getAllAlbum`, `split`, the albums with hearts, on a Coverflow page scoped as an artist's (`RATED_ALBUMS`: no saved album, no Refresh, Return goes back) whose one card sorts by Rating (most first), Album (library order), Artist or Year (as Year Order, undated last), kept in `/mnt/data/ringnav-ratedsort`; the library's albums and Sort come back on close.

## Era

**Era** (Library, after Genre, `local_era`, a build-added copy of stock's `local_genre`) browses by decade, as Rio Karma's Year menu and Eversolo's Era do. `era_menu` runs one grouped query through `sqlite3_exec` on `/mnt/data/database.db`. Era connections use a 64-page SQLite cache and file-backed temporary sorting to reduce peak memory alongside the library records; the query includes the entire library. A query failure toasts "Era unavailable". The query groups: albums as Coverflow splits them (`ALBUM_GROUP`, now in `offsets.inc`), music only (`MUSIC_WHERE`), named (`album` not empty), each dated by its tracks' latest year (`ALBUM_YEAR`, shared with Coverflow's Year sort, taking the first four digits of full dates such as `20220510` and `2022-05-10`), then counted per `year/10*10` with `year > 0`. No decade gives the toast "No dated albums yet" and no page. `era_page` (runtime, `page_list`) has a 64 px row per decade, oldest first, titled "60s" to "90s" then "2000s" on, the album count under it. A row opens `era_albums_page`: **Play All**, **Shuffle**, then the decade's albums (`having release_year between N and N+9`, by year then name), each with "Artist • 1989" under it (album artist, else artist). The rows (album, artist, year and one track's `fileurl`) are copied into `era.v`, sized from the decade's count, and freed when the page closes. An album's record is the album name's songs (`getMusicByAlbum` through `staged_music`), the one at that path, so it splits as Coverflow does. Decade and album rows carry the `list_into` chevron (`page_row_detail_into`). Centre on an album opens `coverflow_goto_album` (its tracks alone, see [Artist Albums in Coverflow](#artist-albums-in-coverflow)). Play All appends every album's `coverflow_album_tracks` in the page's order and plays from the first song as a library queue (`0xf001`, as Coverflow's All albums); Shuffle is Shuffle Songs (`shuffle_play`, one-shot as ever) over the decade's songs found in one query (`era_shuffle_songs`: the files of `ERA_ALBUMS`' groups dated in the decade, joined on `ALBUM_GROUP`'s keys) and the library's music filtered by exact song-table IDs, since two stock queries an album took many seconds on a large decade. A missing first file toasts "Storage unavailable". ponytail: decades only (no year level, no cover view), two queries per album for Play All, and the album rows have no hold menu; a year page under the decade, the decade as a scoped Coverflow (`coverflow_rated`'s pattern) or an album route through `qm_record` are the upgrades.

## Long Songs

Stock's `songtable` has no length: its `time` and `time_end` are a CUE track's start and end seconds (`REC_CUE_START`, `REC_CUE_END`). **Long Songs** (Library, after Hi-Res, `local_longsongs`, a copy of stock's `system_time`) therefore measures files itself, once. A CUE track with both ends takes `end - start`; any other song takes its file's length, less a CUE start, from `/mnt/data/ringnav-lengths`: `{FNV-1a path hash, seconds}` pairs sorted by hash, read whole on first use and searched by bisection. The files not in it are probed on a pthread (`ls_worker`) with the TagLib demo already links: `taglib_file_new`, `taglib_file_audioproperties`, then `AudioProperties::length()` through its vtable (`+0xc`), as libtag_c's `taglib_audioproperties_length` calls it (demo imports no `taglib_audioproperties_length`); an unreadable file is kept as 0 seconds so it isn't probed again. While it runs, the page counts ("Measuring songs… n/N", a 250 ms poll) over a Cancel row; finishing, Cancel or closing the page joins the worker and merges what was probed into the table, which is written whole through `blob_io`. Then `longsongs_page` lists every song over `LONG_SONG_S` (600 s), longest first, "Artist · length" under the title, as Most Played's: a row plays the list from itself as a library queue, and a Play/Pause hold opens the song menu (`CTX_FIXED`, the list in `rt_list`). ponytail: a file edited in place keeps its first length, and removed files' entries stay; the table is a few bytes a song.

## Scrobbling

The same listen also appends a line to `/mnt/mmc/.scrobbler.log`, the card's root, in Rockbox's AudioScrobbler portable-player 1.1 format, so any `.scrobbler.log` uploader can send it to Last.fm or ListenBrainz. A missing file (`access`) gets the `#AUDIOSCROBBLER/1.1`, `#TZ/UTC` and `#CLIENT/Q2 Pod` header first. Each line is artist, album, title, track number, the length in seconds, `L` (listened), the start time and an empty MusicBrainz ID, tab-separated, from the record's `REC_ARTIST`, `REC_ALBUM`, `REC_TITLE` (the tag's; without one, `REC_NAME`, the file name, less its extension) and `REC_TRACK` (left empty when 0); tabs and newlines in tags become spaces. The start time is `time()` less the seconds heard, UTC since the clock fix keeps the system clock in UTC. Artist and album are the player's parsed tags once it has parsed the track (`now_tag`, see [Now Playing](#now-playing-ipod)), so a next folder's untagged records still log. A track with no artist, or a listen while the clock reads before 2020 (never set), is not logged, as scrobblers reject both. With `[SCROBBLE] ALBUM_ARTIST=1` in `.scrobble.ini` (`scrobble_flag`, read at each listen), the artist column is the album artist instead, when the song has one: the player's parsed one (`ID3_ALBUM_ARTIST`, `g_play_id3_info + 0x984`, which `player_get_id3info` fills through `toolsGetMusicInfo` for every non-CUE track), else the record's (`REC_ALBUM_ARTIST`, `+0x24` `albumsonger`, which the song row callback `0x4fb8b8` fills for library rows; folder-queue records have none, and the player copies none for a CUE track). The log stays in the 1.1 format, so any uploader sends the same artist. The file is only appended to, about 100 bytes a listen, by `scrobble_append` in `patch/scrobble.c` under the upload's lock.

With `[SCROBBLE] AUTO=1` (`scrobble_flag`), uploads also start by themselves (`scrobble_auto`, every UI loop pass). Each logged listen counts in RAM, with the time of the first since the last upload; once that first is `scrobble_auto_ms(listens)` old, `SA_MAX_MIN` (180) less `SA_STEP_MIN` (4) per listen, never under `SA_MIN_MIN` (60) minutes, it reads the setting and Wi-Fi (`get_wifisignal() > 0`, never turning it on) at most once a minute and starts `scrobble_start`'s thread, joined silently by the same pass: no toasts. Success, or Upload Scrobbles' success, clears the count; a failure tries again `SA_RETRY_MIN` (30) minutes on. Listens from before the boot, or logged while an upload runs, are not counted, so the loop never reads the card for them; they stay in the log and go with the next upload. None starts while USB mode's page is open (a computer has the card). ponytail: one already uploading when the cable goes in still rewrites the log as it ends; `ringnav_usbmode` could join it if that ever bites.

**Upload Scrobbles** (Local Music, index 2, after Most Played, with a build-added copy of stock's 52 px `wifiset_wifi` as `local_scrobble`) is created only when `/mnt/mmc/.scrobble.ini`, read with `toolsReadConfig`, has `[LISTENBRAINZ] TOKEN` or all four of `[LASTFM] USER`, `PASSWORD`, `API_KEY` and `API_SECRET`. A press with `get_wifisignal` (`0x50e428`, the status bar's bars, -1 unless connected) at 0 or below says "Connect to Wi-Fi first". Otherwise `scrobble_start` reads the accounts again and starts one pthread, and a 500 ms timer (`upload_wait`) polls it and joins it, then toasts the count sent, "Nothing to upload" or "Upload failed, N sent". The thread reads the log, takes complete lines in batches of 50 (Last.fm's limit) and skips comments, lines not rated `L` and lines without artist, title or time. It sends a batch through demo's own libcurl imports: ListenBrainz `submit-listens` as `import` JSON with `Authorization: Token`, and Last.fm `track.scrobble`, form-encoded and signed (`api_sig`, the MD5, from demo's libcrypto `MD5_*` imports, of the sorted names and values followed by the secret), with a session key from `auth.getMobileSession` fetched once per upload, before the first batch. A batch counts as sent only when every configured service answered 200 (and Last.fm's reply has no `error`). The first failure stops the upload. A service that took a batch the other refused gets it again next time. Then, under the lock, the bytes of every sent batch go to `.scrobbler.log.sent` (comments dropped) and the log is rewritten, to a `.tmp` then renamed, as the header plus what follows them. Listens appended during the upload are therefore kept. Uploads always enable `SSL_VERIFYPEER` and `SSL_VERIFYHOST`; `/etc/scrobble-ca.pem`, packaged in the firmware, supplies `CAINFO` because stock has no CA bundle; no certificate file is required on the card. It contains the ListenBrainz and Last.fm trust roots from [Mozilla’s CA store converted by curl](https://curl.se/docs/caextract.html) (MPL-2.0), checked against both services when updated. Archive writes must flush and sync successfully before pending listens are removed, and storage failures are reported to the UI.

## Battery

The kernel has no cpufreq core (no governor to set) and stays stock; six rootfs drains change instead.

PEQ's steady bypass skips its sample loop; the filter still requests float PCM, so bypass doesn't remove every format conversion in the player. ponytail: the format is picked once per track, at the filter's `af_reinit` (`control(0x10000100)`, from `af_add` at each file load); nothing on our side reinits mid-track, so a native-PCM bypass would leave EQ, the visualizer and the outgoing crossfade dead until the next track. Native bypass needs a mid-track reinit (re-sending stock's EQ-on, unverified) or int PCM converted in `play()` (needs the vendor's format codes and stock's rounding); the filter is only in the chain once EQ, Crossfade or the visualizer has been used.

- **drop_caches.** `check_mem_thd` (`0x512050`, started by `platform_init`) writes `3` to `/proc/sys/vm/drop_caches` every sixth second for the life of the player, throwing away the page, dentry and inode caches: the library database, the card's directories, the playing file's readahead and squashfs assets are read again. `toolsGetSysMemFree` has no callers, so nothing depends on it. Its first two words become `jr $ra; move $v0,$zero` (`DROP_CACHES` in `tools/build.py`), so the thread exits as soon as it starts: no write, and no one-second wake. `platform_init` detaches it and nothing joins it.
- **UI loop.** `main_loop_sleep_default` sleeps at most 8 ms (its expected-sleep argument can only shorten that), so AWTK's loop runs at 125 Hz, screen on or off. `ringnav_sleep` first sleeps `SCREEN_OFF_SLEEP_MS` (100 ms, `patch/offsets.inc`) with `sleep_ms` (`0x724928`) while `g_backlight_status` is clear; stock then finds its 8 ms gone, sleeps 0 and updates its own timestamps. The loop runs at about 10 Hz with the screen off, and a key pressed then waits at most 100 ms longer. Once nothing plays (`media_playing`: local, Spotify, radio or video; no AirPlay or Bluetooth receiver) and `check_dacoff_state` has powered the DAC off (`g_dacoff_time` -1, about 31 s after playback stops), it sleeps `DARK_IDLE_SLEEP_MS` (250 ms) instead, about 4 passes a second: a Q2 left dark without Standby otherwise keeps waking both cores for nothing. The first key then waits at most 250 ms; for `WAKE_MS` after a dropped Wake press the pass stays at 100 ms, so the second press is timed as before. With the screen on, once `IDLE_MS` (2 s) pass without input (`ringnav_input` times every event) or a window animation, each pass idles `IDLE_SLEEP_MS` (25 ms), about 30 a second instead of 125; the first input wakes it within that. A game's 33 ms frame timer (`games_live`) keeps stock's pace too: Vortex's raw wheel posts no event to time. Now Playing (`playing_page` on top) keeps stock's pace only while its visualizer repaints (`visualizer_live`), its album marquee moves or its lyrics glide (`lyric_glide`), whose 40 and 50 ms timers and per-pass steps an idle pass would make late; its 250 ms progress and lyric follow keep up. The first pass with the backlight back on calls `widget_invalidate_force` on the window manager once, so the whole screen repaints on wake rather than only the regions that change next.
- **hciplayer's loops.** Stopped, the player's main thread (`0x42d994`) polled its play flag (`0xb10e34`) with `usleep(10000)`, 100 wakeups a second, more than everything else on a dark, idle Q2 together; paused, MPlayer's loop (`0x42fff0`) checked for a command every 20 ms. Both sleeps are futex waits now (`IDLE_WAIT` and `PAUSE_WAIT` in `tools/peq.py`, `patch/peq_player.c`): the stopped loop's on the flag, the paused loop's on a count, `cmd_gen`. Every command reaches the player through the command thread's handler (`0x42be20`), which `hci_command` wraps (`CMD_DONE`). When the handler set the flag (`set-songurl`, `set-nexturl`) it wakes the stopped loop; when it changed anything the paused loop reacts to (the next and stop flags `0xb10e6c` and `0xb10e70`, or the MPlayer context's state, which `mcl-play` and `mcl-pause` set) it moves `cmd_gen` and wakes that. demo's status queries, several a second, change none and wake nothing. A count that moved while the loop checked returns at once. Each wait times out after a second (`WAIT_S`) as a fallback, so a paused or stopped Q2 wakes the player about once a second. `WAIT_S` cannot grow much: the command thread clears a heartbeat (`0xb10e68`) each second, which both loops set after a wait, and exits hciplayer after six checks find it clear (`0x42d95c`); a `_Static_assert` keeps it at 2 s or less. `test/hci_wait.py` runs the built player's waits and the wrapper under Unicorn.
- **Player scheduling.** `on_wm_idle_status`, stock's idle callback, runs every UI pass and calls `player_mainscheduling` (`0x514c70`), whose `mclAutoChange` (with Gapless on and the player connected) asks hciplayer for `player-status` and waits for the answer on the UI thread: 40 round trips a second on an idle lit screen, up to 125 while it animates, each waking demo's reply thread and hciplayer's command thread, and 12% of the UI thread's samples asleep waiting. `MAINSCHEDULING_CALL` in `tools/build.py` turns its `jalr $t9` (`0x4e9d28`) into `jal ringnav_mainscheduling`, which runs it every `MAINSCHEDULING_MS` (100 ms), or every `DARK_POLL_US` while dark and quiet, as the status sync above. A lyric line and the play-state notifications follow within that. From a second before the playing track's end (`player_playtime_and_length`'s cached times, a CUE track's own end, no round trip) until `MAINSCHEDULING_END_MS` after, it runs every pass as stock did, so a change without Gapless (`mclAutoChange` → `mclNextSong`), a CUE track's change, a folder's rollover and Now Playing's new track are not delayed; paused there it stays paced. Gapless changes are hciplayer's own. ponytail: a length that overstates the audio (a VBR MP3 without a Xing header) ends outside the window, up to 100 ms late; `mclAutoChange`'s own end flag would catch it.
- **Stock pollers.** `mclSyncPlayerStatus`' 200 ms loop sleeps through `ringnav_dark_usleep`: at least `DARK_POLL_US` (1 s) while the pass finds the Q2 dark and quiet, as above. `parse_albumcovertask_thd`'s 50 ms and `player_parsecover_thd`'s 100 ms waits sleep through `ringnav_dark_cover_usleep`: at least 1 s whenever the screen is off, playing or not, since they only feed what is painted (`DARK_POLLS` in `tools/build.py`). ponytail: a cover parsed while dark, or a thread still in its long sleep at wake, shows up to 1 s late; wake the threads at the first lit pass if that shows.
- **Painting.** Stock's screen-off (`screen_action`, `0x4f77d4`, with 0) turns the backlight off and blanks the framebuffer (`disable_fb`, `0x4f3bcc`), but nothing in the UI loop reads `g_backlight_status`: `main_loop_simple`'s step paints every pass (`window_manager_paint` at `0x68b278`), and Now Playing's 250 ms timer (`0x52bffc`) runs its page update (`0x52b6f4`) whatever the screen. So a Q2 left dark on Now Playing kept drawing its times and bar into a blanked framebuffer. `ringnav_wm_paint` returns `RET_OK` without painting while the backlight is off, unless a window animation runs (`window_manager_is_animating`): AWTK steps it from the paint, so it would otherwise wait for the screen. Invalidated regions keep, and the first lit pass repaints the whole screen as above.
- **MQA side chain.** hciplayer ran every probed FLAC and PCM stream through the MQA decoder; the build runs it only for MQA ([MQA detection](#mqa-detection)).
- **Wi-Fi power save.** The RTL8733BS driver loads with Leisure Power Save on (`rtw_power_mgnt` 2, `PS_MODE_MAX`, in the module's `.data`), but once Wi-Fi connects, `network_checkstatus` (from home's 500 ms timer) runs `rtwpriv wlan0 pm_set lps=0` (`0x50e3d4`) and nothing turns it on again: the radio stays awake for the whole connection. The build makes that `lps=2` (`WIFI_LPS` in `tools/build.py`, the byte at file offset `0x37133d`), which the driver applies at its next 2 s watchdog. That alone would keep little: `networkGetWifiSignal` (`0x5afb48`), which the status bar (`get_wifisignal`) and the network monitor thread (a `bal`) each call about once a second, asks with `SIOCGIWSTATS`, which the driver answers through `get_station`, whose TX report (`rtw_get_sta_tx_stat`) leaves LPS every time; only the watchdog enters it again. So `ringnav_wifi_signal` takes the function's entry and reads `signal_strength` from `/proc/net/rtl8733bs/wlan0/rx_signal` instead, at most every `WIFI_SIGNAL_MS` (1 s): the same percentage `get_station` reports less 100 as dBm, from a read that touches no hardware. The one difference is 100%, 0 dBm, which stock's unsigned level wrapped to -256 (one bar) and is 0 here (four). Unreadable, it is 0, as stock's failed ioctl. While Bluetooth A2DP streams with Wi-Fi on 2.4 GHz, the driver's coexistence keeps LPS off anyway (its PS-TDMA cases on this chip force it). Why Shanling turned LPS off is unknown.
- **Watchdog.** `platform_init` starts `checkappprocess.sh`, which forks `pgrep` every 2 s and reboots if `demo` is gone. Its `sleep 2` becomes `sleep 10` (`WATCHDOG` in `tools/build.py`), so a crashed player reboots within 10 s.

## ALSA startup lock

The stock ALSA 1.1.6 library can deadlock with an ioplug behind `plug`. Preparation locks the wrapper while the ioplug callback unlocks and relocks its slave, leaving a slave mutex owned by the setup thread, so the writer blocks at its first write. `q2video` sets `LIBASOUND_THREAD_SAFE=0` in its own process before opening ALSA. Its PCM calls are sequential: setup precedes the writer, and drop, prepare and close follow joining it. BlueALSA's locks, the local player, the codecs and the services are unchanged.

To reproduce the old hang and verify the fix against the stock MIPS library, run `python3 test/alsa.py BUILD QEMU_MIPSEL`. The regression covers one and two plug layers and rate conversion.

## Audio writes and the queue checkpoint

The shared audio writer keeps partial ALSA writes and retries the remaining frames after an underrun, instead of discarding unwritten samples. Native tests exercise partial writes, zero progress, recovery and lost outputs. This is independent of the ALSA startup lock workaround.

Queue and traversal changes commit the full queue snapshot, for queues up to `SAVE_LIMIT` (999) tracks; a larger one keeps no snapshot (it would be rewritten whole on every track change), so a reboot resumes it as stock does. Version 3 snapshots carry Play Next's pending list after the history (count, then indices). A version 2 file, written before the list existed, restores its one run (`forced`, `force_end` in its header) as the list, in order; the next save writes version 3. Elapsed-only changes atomically replace the 16-byte `ringnav-queue-elapsed` checkpoint, which applies only when it matches the snapshot's checksum and position. The UI loop writes it every 30 s while the same track plays on and within 5 s of a pause, seek or track change, and retries a failed write every 30 s (a full snapshot rebuilt and synced each time, so a failing card isn't hammered every 5 s); power-off (`ringnav_savequeue`) writes the exact second. A crash mid-track resumes up to 30 s early. The progress check itself runs at most every five seconds (`playback.poll_stamp`), so UI passes between checkpoints do not repeatedly take the playback-time mutex.

**Reboots.** Stock writes Memory Playback's record (`save_memoryplay_info`, `0x516344`: play class, track, second and the album or folder the queue came from, through `write_memory_to_file`) only from `into_poweroff`, the power-off page's. `device_reboot` (`0x4f7874`: Power's Restart, a long power-key press on the power-off page, and `on_wm_idle_status`'s Bluetooth recovery) and the firmware update never call it, so after a TF card update or Network Update the Q2 resumed the queue and second of the last power-off, however long ago. A queue without advanced play keeps no snapshot of its own, so nothing else had its place. Both now save first (`ringnav_reboot` and `ringnav_checkfw` in `patch/playback.c`), through the hooked `save_memoryplay_info`, so the snapshot's exact second is written too. The update saves when its dialog opens (`dialog_checkfw_dialog_init`, `0x4985a4`, after stock's confirmation, both rows'), since that init stops the player before its thread (`0x4982b8`) runs `update_firmware` and the countdown's `device_reboot`. A stopped player (`mclGetPlayStatus` 1) saves nothing, as stock's save skips it, so that later `device_reboot` leaves the saved place alone and the snapshot keeps its second.

## M4B recognition and audit

Stock has two audio extension checks: folder enumeration at `0x5c2054` and recursive scanning at `0x5c3da4`. Both also feed playlist loading. `ringnav_audio_extension` accepts `.m4b` case-insensitively and otherwise calls the original `strstr` with its original format list. The builder asserts the original import load and call bytes before replacing each call; `audio_extension_calls` records both edits in the manifest and packaging audit. The installed TagLib already sends M4B to the MP4 constructor, and the decoder probes the original MP4 container. Neither needs a binary dispatch change. Cover extraction has a separate extension dispatcher in `libtag_c.so.2.0.1`, which stock recognizes as MP4 artwork only for M4A/MP4. `patch_m4b_cover` in `tools/build.py` checks the whole library hash and adds a case-insensitive M4B comparison that reaches the existing MP4 image extractor. Its 48-byte appended segment is read/execute only, uses relative branches and GP-relative data, and preserves the other formats. Now Playing retains stock’s external folder-cover fallback when embedded extraction fails; Coverflow retains its folder-image fallback. Files and paths are preserved. `test/m4b.py` executes the native metadata and cover dispatchers at two load addresses, while `test/patch.py` covers the patched checks, short-book persistence and shared resume/count exclusions.

## Headset controls and volume

`patch/headset.c` restores the AACP handshake and AVRCP input from `6799d59`, without the battery state or connection card. One generation-tagged worker uses nonblocking socket/input I/O and one `select` over both descriptors, up to 1 s a pass, so a still connection wakes it once a second. It rescans for the AVRCP input each pass for the first 10 passes, then every 2nd, and retries AACP every 5th. It drains the AACP socket each pass (AirPods send other reports between ear reports), validates complete ear packets, retries discovery throughout a connection, queues only key presses, and closes descriptors on disconnect. The UI loop discards stale generations and dispatches commands every 100 ms. No player API runs on the worker.

The internal dispatcher chooses video, radio, Spotify, then the stock local/Tidal player, independently of the visible page. Explicit play/pause operations and session identities support ear detection; the number of buds originally worn (counted, since the report's primary/secondary slots swap when the primary leaves an ear), a manual-command epoch and the player/session identity gate automatic resume. Recent AVRCP pauses are coordinated with ear reports, and duplicate commands are suppressed. Player starts, track changes, manual controls and disconnects invalidate pending resume. Video retains its commands and adds idempotent `a`/`r` pause/resume, an atomic pause-state file and a separate `g` gain command. Radio resumes the current station by restarting it.

Bluetooth output remembers `PLAYSET BT_VOLUME` independently of wired/USB's stock `PLAYSET VOLUME`. The `write_int_config` hook routes volume saves to the active output's key; the `mclSetOutputWay` hook saves the departing level and restores the incoming one before stock switches output, clamped to the volume limit, then applies it to the mixer. A missing or invalid saved level inherits the current volume. Stock boot volume behavior keeps the wired key. While receiving Bluetooth audio from a phone, the gear's Audio settings and its submenus accept normal wheel navigation; the receiver player and volume dialog keep stock volume control.

**Bluetooth → Control Device Volume** (`Q2POD BTVOLLINK`, `bt_vol_link`, a `pod_rows` switch right after Resume on Connect in `ringnav_btrows`) is Off by default, as stock: the Q2's volume is stock's software gain alone and the device keeps its own volume, never read or written (`bt_audio_ready` is 0, so `ringnav_btvol` passes the levels straight to stock, `headset_gain` is `g_volume` and `bt_volume_poll` stops), and Rockbox's launch leaves the device's volume alone too. A device whose own volume tracked the Q2's turned down with it, so a low Q2 volume played thin through a speaker's weak amplifier stage. On, the rest of this section applies. A switch while Bluetooth plays resets the sync and re-applies the volume at once (`headset_volume_reset`, `device_set_volume`): off, stock's gain comes back; on, the next poll writes the Q2's level to the device.

With it on, volume starts by writing the Q2's saved volume to the headset, unless the headset's level already reads as it (128 headset steps map onto 101, so a step that rounds to the same volume is left alone). Polling then consumes headset changes, rounds between 0–127 and 0–100, enforces the configured limit, saves Q2 state and updates the visible volume slider. Remembering the accepted absolute value prevents feedback loops. Confirmed headset volume uses full helper gain except at zero; unsupported or failed reads/writes restore software gain. Spotify, radio and video consume the same effective gain. The AAC 48 kHz capability fix remains in place.

`test/patch.py`'s headset cases run routing, ear counts and slot swaps, single-bud listening, first reports, manual cancellation, player/session changes, duplicate commands, malformed reports and volume limits against the MIPS payload. `test/headset_io.py` runs the actual worker with simulated kernel I/O, including late input discovery, handshake, draining queued reports, release/repeat filtering, cleanup and stale reconnect reports.

## Resume on Connect

Two paths start playback when a headset connects. Stock's `checkBluetoothStatus` (`0x4f2cfc`), once a headset's A2DP comes up with the Q2 as the source, runs `player_save_stop` (saves `mclGetPlayStatus` unless stopped, then `mclStop`), `config_outputchannel(2, 3)`, `device_set_volume` and `player_save_start(0)` (`0x4f358c`). That restarts the saved local or Tidal queue unpaused, so a paused queue plays, and a stopped queue with a stale saved status starts over Spotify or Internet Radio (`mclStartPlayer`'s hook yields them). AirPods also send AVRCP PLAY as the link comes up, which `headset_command` turns into a start for whichever player is active.

**Bluetooth → Resume on Connect** (`ringnav_btrows`, hooking stock's list builder `0x4bee90`, which rebuilds the whole list on every search and link change; `Q2POD RESUME` in `config.ini`; On, 1, is stock's behaviour) is Off by default. With Bluetooth on, the row goes right after stock's fourth row, Bluetooth Headset Volume Control (`bt_adjvol`, before Scan now), text only and 78 high like stock's rows; with Bluetooth off (just the switch) it isn't added. The jalr at `0x4f358c` becomes `jal ringnav_bt_connect_start`, which with the setting On calls `player_save_start(0)` as stock; Off, it lets a playing queue go on, reloads a paused one paused (`player_save_start(1)`, as stock's disconnect does) and starts nothing for a stopped queue or while a stream owns playback. Off, `headset_command` also drops PLAY (200, 207, or 164 while nothing plays) within `CONNECT_HOLD_MS` (5 s) of the link, which covers the worker's AVRCP discovery (about a second) and the headset's PLAY after A2DP setup; a press after that plays. Ear detection is a separate path: the first ear report after a connect never resumes, and putting worn buds back resumes only what removing them paused, with the setting either way. AirPlay and DLNA keep their senders' control (stock skips the restart while AirPlay plays).

## Wi-Fi transfer

Stock's Wi-Fi transfer page (thttpd on port 8888, `/usr/share/web`: `index.html`, `js/index.js` on jQuery File Upload 5.40.1 and five CGIs) is patched in place by `patch_web` (`WEB` in `tools/build.py`: exact replacements, each checked against the audited stock file, and no Chinese may be left). Every string is in English. The extension filter (client-side only; `upload.cgi` takes any file) adds the formats Q2 Pod's pages read: M4B, MP4, M4V, MKV, AVI, MOV, MPG, TXT and EPUB.

**Folders.** **Upload Folder** is a second file input with `webkitdirectory`, sharing the upload widget (`fileInput`). A picked folder's files carry `webkitRelativePath` (`Album/CD1/01.flac`), a dropped folder's (the plugin walks it; patched to call `readEntries` until it returns none, as Chromium gives at most 100 entries a call) `relativePath` (`Album/CD1/`); either way the file goes to the current folder plus that path, and its dot files (`.DS_Store`, macOS's `._` copies) are skipped silently. Stock's `upload.cgi` only moves the part into `path` (`mv "%s" "%s" -f`), so `_mkdir` first makes each folder with stock's `create` (`mkdir "%s"`, no `-p`), parents first, once per batch (`_made`, cleared when the batch stops): one that exists fails harmlessly, and a folder that really couldn't be made shows as its files' upload errors. A file's row shows the upload panel as it is added and can be cancelled while its folders are made; the panel hides (and `_made` clears) only once no row is left, since the plugin's `stop` fires whenever no upload is in flight, also between files that wait on a folder. ponytail: the stock CGIs run `mkdir` and `mv` through `system()` inside double quotes, so a name holding `"`, `` ` `` or `$` can't be uploaded, as a plain file can't on stock; replacing the CGIs would lift it.

Checks in `test/build.py`: the three files in the image are `patch_web` of stock's, listed in the manifest's changed assets.

## Quick-settings radio holds

In both variants, holding Wi-Fi or Bluetooth for `RATE_HOLD_MS` opens `systemset/wifi_page` or `systemset/bluetooth_page`. `ringnav_quick_settings` adds pointer-down handlers to the stock images. Release, movement over 10 pixels, pointer abort, keys, window close, background or destruction cancels the one-shot timer. A successful hold aborts the native press before closing the dialog and suppresses any late click, so it cannot also toggle the radio. Ordinary taps keep their stock click handlers.

## Local playlist editing

`ringnav_playlistsmore` extends stock `localmusic_playlistsmore_page_init` (`0x4b3918`) in both editions. Its context is the index into `p_deque_playlist`; the container's `REC_DISC` (`cd_num`) fixes the editor's destination. Stock Rename and Add to Playlist routes remain in use, while New Playlist's allocator and Delete are replaced (`ringnav_playlist_create`, `ringnav_playlist_delete`); the More page's Delete row is relabelled Delete Playlist, as it deletes the whole list; the label is set as its translation key (`widget_set_tr_text`; no language has it, so it reads as given), which a re-translation keeps. The added Edit Playlist row reuses the Rename icon and the stock rows' list item, 335px button, 52px icon, 20px label and spacing. The runtime `playlistedit_page` uses the shared `page_open` / `page_list` helpers, its own staged records and a local song picker. Touch/Centre song actions include adjacent movement and confirmed removal; Add Songs leaves the picker open after each stock batch addition, checking a fresh load before reporting success. Add Songs' first row opens the same T9 box as Internet Radio's pull-down (`edit_create`, named `search_edit` so `search_key` closes the keyboard before Return destroys it); the picker filters case-insensitively on tagged title, artist and album, and keeps the query while several songs are added. Holding Play/Pause on a playlist row opens `localmusic/playlistsmore_page` with the playlist index from the row's `img_more<n>` dots, as the dots themselves do (the list's row index also counts Import and Export, and a row without dots keeps stock's hold); the shared `playlist_page` add picker is excluded by `PLAYLIST_ADD_MODE`. Stock duplicate checking runs before addition. The picker ticks a song the playlist already holds (same path and CUE start, matched against the staged entries in memory, no query per row) with stock's `select` check at x 340, the caption shortened to stop before it; a successful add ticks its row in place. A private one-entry libcstl selection vector lives for demo’s lifetime, so adding never rewrites a covered stock picker’s global batch selection. Done/Back exits; destroy cancels pending work and releases both deques.

Playlist creation (`ringnav_playlist_create`) allocates a free header ID from 0–99 under `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, independent of the displayed name order. Stock's allocator assumed ID order and could reuse an occupied ID after name sorting. Duplicate names keep stock's `-2` result; database failures roll back and show “Could not create playlist”. Delete Playlist asks for confirmation, then removes the header and its entries in one transaction. It sets stock's reload flag and shows “Playlist deleted” only after a successful commit; failure keeps the playlist and shows “Could not delete playlist”. Music files and the current playback queue stay intact.

Stock `getMusicByPlayList` (`0x500a28`) selects `playlist` records with `id > 100` and the destination `cd_num`. `getAllPlayList` (`0x4fefb0`) lists the playlists themselves with `select * from playlist where id <= 100`; `ringnav_library_query` appends `order by name collate nocase, id` to exactly that query, so Library → Playlists and Add to Playlist list them by name (stock Rename updates `name` and `filename` together). It also adds explicit `order by id asc` to that exact stock query, including its export (`saveExternM3uFile`) and later playback callers. `ple_mutate` opens the existing SQLite library, takes `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, validates both entry IDs, playlist membership, path and CUE bounds, then swaps IDs through a verified free ID above the table maximum. Every update/delete checks `changes() == 1`; errors and failed commits roll back. Removal uses entry ID, so duplicate paths and CUE segments remain distinct. Only the chosen entries change; records omitted from a view keep their IDs. UI reload and selection movement follow successful commits. No live playback queue is rewritten, and no schema is added.

Previous/Next press, repeats, native delivery, long press and release are consumed while the editor is open, including under a success/error toast; movement requires the editor to own the top window. One press moves once; a release latch survives page closure. The wheel retains ordinary row selection. `test/patch.py` exercises the MIPS editor in both builds with SQLite transactions at the import boundary, including rollback, stale selections, ignored writes, duplicate/CUE entries, isolation, limits, picker behavior, touch actions and selection retention. Hardware reboot, stock export/import round trips and subsequent playback still require device validation before release.

## 1.3.5 audio and input fixes

Swap L/R (`Q2POD SWAPLR`, default 0) follows Mono Audio in settings. `crossfade.c` publishes `/tmp/q2swap` using the existing atomic shared-int pattern. The float PCM filter swaps after EQ and mono, before Crossfade and the visualizer; q2video swaps S16 after soft gain and mono for video, radio and Spotify’s helper. Spotify uses that helper on the headphone DAC when either switch is on, with the existing sink-restart limitation. Neither option changes DSD/DoP or AirPlay.

The stock title sorter queries `toolsQueryDbTableAndSort` (`0x5c8274`), now scoped through `ringnav_sorted_query` just like the other Library queries. Folder loads and stored playlist records retain their scope. Bluetooth absolute volume keeps the larger requested channel gain on the headset and normalizes each software channel against it. Reconnect/read-back uses `device_set_volume` to retain stock Balance rather than applying equal gains. `bt_volume_poll` reads the headset's volume back every 400 ms lit; dark, every `BT_VOLUME_LIMIT_MS` (1 s) while a volume limit has to be held on the headset's own keys, else `BT_VOLUME_SLOW_MS` (2 s). A headset that refuses absolute volume, or can't report it (a read above 127, as before its AVRCP is up), gets the 2 s pace lit too, so its reconnect `device_set_volume` isn't repeated 2.5 times a second. ponytail: dark, the headset's own keys can pass the limit for up to 1 s before it is pulled back.

`ringnav_input` handles the disconnected USB confirmation before stock input routing. During a Now Playing animation it applies fresh wheel volume directly and consumes the event; held list-navigation ticks are discarded. Once the animation ends, normal stock feedback resumes.

Stock startup and wallpaper-list validation call `lockscreen_access` only at their two audited access sites (`0x4fa41c`, `0x4fad60`). Valid built-in names succeed; other paths use normal access checks. The list wrapper no longer writes a repaired choice back to config.ini. Factory reset still selects stock’s default.

## Network Update

**System settings → System Update** keeps stock's page (`systemset_fwupdate_page_init`, hooked by `ringnav_fwupdate` in `patch/netupdate.c`). Stock's row builder (`0x4c3140`) names each row's button by its index, and stock's click (`0x4c2fac`) runs `atoi` on the name: 0 is the TF card update (`0x4c2994`), 1 Shanling's plain-HTTP server (`openwin.shanling.com:9000`, `/tmp/versioninfo`, `download_ota_thd`). The hook renames the second button "2", which stock's click ignores, and adds its own click (`online_click`).

The click says "Connect to Wi-Fi first" when `get_wifisignal` is 0 or below. Otherwise one pthread (`worker`) runs two transfers through demo's libcurl, both verified against `/etc/scrobble-ca.pem` (`SSL_VERIFYPEER`, `SSL_VERIFYHOST` 2):

1. A header-only request for `https://github.com/DiamondBond/q2-pod/releases/latest` (`Q2POD_REPO`, another repo's with `tools/build.py --update-repo`), without following the redirect. The tag is what follows the last `/` of `CURLINFO_REDIRECT_URL` (`netupdate_tag`, `.../releases/tag/TAG`). A tag equal to `Q2POD_TAG` (`VERSION` in `tools/build.py`; empty in `--dev` builds) says "Latest firmware installed", as stock's updater would refuse that tag anyway.
2. `releases/latest/download/update.tar` (iPod) or `update-legacy.tar` (Legacy), following the redirect to GitHub's asset host, into `/mnt/mmc/update.tar.part`, flushed and `fsync`ed, then renamed over `update.tar`. curl fails a body shorter than its `Content-Length`, and a transfer under 1 KB/s for 30 s ends. Any failure deletes the `.part` and keeps the old `update.tar`: "Can't reach GitHub" (curl or no redirect), "No update for this build" (not 200), "Can't write to the card".

A 500 ms timer (`wait_done`) shows "Checking…" until the first bytes, then "Downloading NN%" in the row's label (or "Cancelling…"), from the transfer's progress callback, calls `reset_poweroptions_timer(1, 1, 0)` so standby and auto power-off wait (the screen still sleeps), and joins the thread. Presses between the thread's end and that join do nothing. A second press sets `nu.cancel`, which the progress callback returns, so curl stops ("Download cancelled"); a cancel that lands after the transfer finished still deletes the `.part`. The header request has no progress callback, so a cancel there waits for it (30 s at most), then skips the download. Leaving the page drops the label (`EVT_DESTROY`) and cancels, as the card may be unmounted or shared over USB next. A tag not newer than the build's (`strcmp`, as tags are single-digit `x.y.z`) is "Latest firmware installed", so an older release is never offered; `--dev` builds take any tag. After the rename the card's root directory is `fsync`ed (demo imports no `sync`), so the new entry is on the card before stock flashes. When it finishes on the page, `fwupdate_tf` runs stock's TF card update: its battery check, its confirmation and the flash. Elsewhere it says "Update downloaded: use TF card update".

`tools/release.py` uploads each variant's `update.tar` again under those fixed names (`FIXED`), because `releases/latest/download/` needs a name without the version. `github.com` chains to USERTrust ECC (Sectigo E46) and the asset host to ISRG Root X1; both roots are in the bundle.

ponytail: only releases that carry the fixed-name assets can be fetched, and a CA change at GitHub needs a bundle refresh. The card needs room for the new tar (about 55 MB) beside the old one. TLS and curl's length check are the only integrity checks before stock's updater checks its MD5s. Plugging in USB storage mid-download unmounts the card under the write (the card-corruption risk of any card write); the `.part` then fails and is deleted. Leave USB unplugged until it finishes.

## Card apps

Card apps (`patch/addon.c`, apps on the card that Q2 Pod knows only by their folder: q2-deezer, q2-soulseek, q2-filebrowser) draw their pages over Q2SPOT/1, as Spotify's browser does ([librespot.md](librespot.md)). Each row's kind sets what a press does: `list` opens a level (its uri the op, its context the argument), `action` sends its uri with the context and toasts the reply, `search` opens the T9 box, `remote` the QR code page, `local` plays a song or a folder from the card (context `album`: in track order, by name), `info` nothing; `position` is the op a held Play/Pause sends, and a reply message `refresh` asks again every 2 s.

**More….** A level keeps its list's own arguments apart from a page's cursor (`lv_t.base`, the bytes of `args` before it), and More… asks for the list's arguments and the new cursor alone, the op's last argument as Q2SPOT/1 has it. Up to 1.4.1 each More… appended its cursor to the page's arguments, cursor included ("albums 30 60"), which q2-deezer read as the second page again; q2-deezer v3.3 reads the last argument either way.

**Stop and Retry.** A page that can't show the app's rows ends in **Stop <name>** whenever the app may be running (launched, a restart pending, or its loop's pid file there): an error page under its Retry, and a level loading or starting for `SLOW_MS` (3 s; a frozen app holds a request up to `ADDON_WAIT_MS`, 15 s). It ends the app as Stop Streaming Services does, a TERM to its loop (`app_stop`), so a frozen app, which can't answer a stop op of its own, stops too; then it toasts and leaves the page. Retry on "<name> isn't running" while the loop's pid file is there (the app running, its socket gone) restarts it: the loop is asked to end, the page waits as for a start (a pending `restart` counts as starting), and `poll_one` starts the app again once the pid file is gone, or after `RESTART_MS` (20 s) regardless; q2-soulseek's launcher kills an app still there 15 s after its TERM. Otherwise Retry asks again, starting the app first if it isn't running.

**Stream mode.** An app that plays as well (q2-deezer's Stream mode) writes `/tmp/q2-<id>.play`, "<seq> <folder>": a hidden card folder (under `/mnt/mmc/.`, no `..`) it fills with a list's songs named in play order ("01 - Title [id].flac"), each renamed into place once whole. `stream_poll`, every `ADDON_POLL_MS` while the app runs, follows it: a new one is played from its first song once one is there (a 0 ms timer: the folder's songs by name, `app_album_songs`, through `play_folder` as a local album, Now Playing opening); while the queue playing is still the folder's (its first song's path in it), songs named after the last one queued join its end through `playback_queue_insert`, as Add to Queue; and the path of the one playing goes to `/tmp/q2-<id>.playing` (`blob_io`), from which the app fetches a couple ahead and deletes the ones long played. Once another queue plays, the session is left; a new seq starts again. The folder is listed only when its mtime changes (`stream_newer`, `sm.seen`), not every poll; an mtime within a second of now is scanned again on the next poll, as a file could land later in that second. A song that is added and then rejected (no `stSongInfo` for it yet) is picked up only at the folder's next change. Songs are queued by name after the last one queued, so a song that finishes downloading before an earlier one is never queued. The library scan skips hidden folders (`SCAN_DIRS`: the folder walk's `"."` test is `ringnav_scan_dir`, which skips any name starting with a dot, as Folder view's list does), so streamed songs never join the Library.

## Q2 Shelf

**System settings → App Store** (titled **App Store**; just above System Update, both variants; `shelf_open` in `patch/netupdate.c`, the `apps_page` runtime window) installs, updates and removes the apps in [`sources.list`](../sources.list) from their GitHub releases. The firmware names no app but its own three. The list is fetched from `raw.githubusercontent.com/DiamondBond/q2-pod/main/sources.list`, which doesn't count against the API's rate limit, and kept as `/mnt/mmc/.q2/sources.list` so the page works offline. With no list yet, the page has one row, **App list**, which fetches it when pressed with Wi-Fi.

Each line is `owner/repo [Name]` (`#` starts a comment), and `shelf_sources` reads up to 16 (`SH_MAX`). The owner is `A-Za-z0-9-`, the repo also allows `._`, and the name defaults to the repo's. A line is skipped when any of these fail or its id repeats an earlier one's. The id is the repo's name less `q2-`, and must be 1 to 15 of `a-z0-9-`, addon.c's rule, since it goes into shell commands. A card app's folder is `.<id>`, its binary `.<id>/q2-<id>`, and nothing in it is kept from unzip.

Q2 Pod's own apps are matched by the repo's name, so a fork is the same app; another repo whose id would be one of theirs is skipped:

- `q2-rockbox` → `rockbox`: binary `.rockbox/rockbox`, keeps `.rockbox/config.cfg`.
- `q2-rockbox-themes` → `themes`: folder `.rockbox`, no binary, keeps the same file.
- `q2-librespot` → `spotify`: binary `.spotify/librespot`, keeps `.spotify/cache/*` and `.spotify/.cache-size`.

The rows follow the list's order. Each row shows its app's state as the row's detail:

- **Installed** with a tag in `/mnt/mmc/.q2/apps/<id>.tag`.
- **Update** when that tag differs from the latest release's (string equality, not order), or when the app's binary is there without a tag (a manual install).
- Otherwise **Install**.

Rockbox Themes is listed only while `.rockbox/rockbox` is a regular file, and **Erase Spotify Data** comes after Spotify only while `.spotify` exists. The rows are built when the page opens, and again when a job's end changes which (`sh_rows`). Themes says "Install Rockbox first" without Rockbox.

ponytail: a card app keeps nothing from an update. Its settings survive only because its release zip doesn't ship them; an app whose zip ships its settings file overwrites the user's copy on every update. The fix belongs in that app's release, or in a `keep` list here if one ever needs it.

See [the user guide](manual/guide.html#apps) for install, removal, confirmation and cancellation controls.

One pthread runs one job at a time, all transfers through netupdate.c's `fetch` (the same CA bundle and TLS checks, `User-Agent: Q2Pod`, progress and cancel through its transfer callback). Opening the page with Wi-Fi checks: it fetches the list, then every app's latest release. This is skipped if every release was checked in the last 10 minutes (`SH_STALE_MS`, kept until power-off): GitHub allows 60 unauthenticated API requests an hour, and an install fetches its own release anyway (`releases/latest` of `api.github.com`: `tag_name`, and the first asset whose name ends in `.zip`, its `browser_download_url` and `size`, `shelf_release`). The worker parses the fetched list into `sh.next`, adds back any app it dropped that still has a tag (so it can still be removed), and writes the result to the card. The UI thread adopts it when the check ends, so the rows never change under a job. A list that fails to fetch or parses to nothing leaves the old one in place. An install:

1. Fetches its release again, deletes any leftover `/mnt/mmc/.q2/staging` (an interrupted install is retried, never resumed), and needs the zip's size plus 64 MB free (`statfs`).
2. Downloads the zip to `.q2/staging/<id>.part`.
3. Lists it with `busybox unzip -l -qq` (stock busybox 1.31.1), restricted as the unpack is: the app's folder only (`.rockbox/*` or `.spotify/*`), never `*/._*` or the kept paths (`.rockbox/config.cfg`; `.spotify/cache/*` and `.spotify/.cache-size`). `shelf_unpacked` rejects an absolute path or a `..` component and a total over 1 GB, and needs at least one entry; the card then needs that total plus 64 MB free.
4. Unpacks with `busybox unzip -o -qq -d .q2/staging/<id>` and the same patterns, so `INSTALL.txt`, `__MACOSX/` and macOS `._` files stay off the card. busybox's unzip matches with `fnmatch` without `FNM_PATHNAME` (so `*` spans `/`), and strips a leading `/` and `../` from names itself before listing and unpacking.
5. Stops Spotify (`SPOT_KILL`) again before touching `.spotify`, in case it was started during the download, lists the staged files with `find <dir> -type f`, and renames each over its target on the card (the same file system), making folders as needed; the app's binary goes last, and only when every other file moved, so `.rockbox/rockbox` is the old file or the new one, never part of one, and never new beside a half-moved set. The list becomes `.q2/apps/<id>.files`, then `sync`, then the tag through `blob_io`. Staging is deleted after every install, whatever the outcome.

Every Spotify job starts and ends with `spot_stop` on the UI thread, so spotify.c forgets the librespot it started and Streaming → Spotify starts the new one (or says it is missing).

**Remove** unlinks the app's binary first (a remove cut short still boots Q2 Pod), then each path in `<id>.files` that no other app's list holds (two fonts are in both Rockbox's and the themes' zips), then each folder left empty, up to and including the app's own, then the list and tag. Removing Rockbox removes the themes first. Removing Spotify stops it and keeps `cache/` and `.cache-size`, which were never installed by it; **Erase Spotify Data** stops it and deletes those two.

A 500 ms timer refreshes progress and holds standby and auto power-off during app jobs; `sh_wait` joins the worker and reports its result wherever the user is, including after leaving the page.

ponytail: TLS and curl's length check are the only checks on the zip, and unzip's CRCs the only ones inside it; there is no signature. Cancel stops the download at once, otherwise it lands after the step it was pressed in (the API request, the listing or the unpack); once files move it is ignored. A power cut while files move leaves those moved so far beside the old ones, with the old tag and list; the next install starts over. Staging left by a power cut stays on the card until the next install. A release that drops a file leaves the old one on the card and off the new list. The card is FAT32 or exFAT, which hold no symlinks or special files: busybox fails on one, so nothing checks for them.

### Card apps

Every other app in the list is a card app: it installs through the same rows and runs from **Streaming** through one generic host, `patch/addon.c`. It lists up to 8 card folders `.<id>/` (id: 1 to 15 of `a-z0-9-`, since it goes into shell commands) holding `q2app` (`name=`, `search=`, `qr=1`), `run` and `q2-<id>`, and optionally `icon.png` (52 px, Legacy's rows) and `icon-40.png` (40 px, iPod's): Streaming's row shows the edition's size 1:1, else the other scaled down, else the shared `stream_app` (`addon_icon`); in the Minimal theme it greys as Streaming's own icons do (`addon_is_icon` in `ringnav_image_add`), and Classic keeps its colours. The launcher's loop leaves its pid in `/tmp/q2-<id>`; a TERM to it stops the app. `/tmp/q2-<id>.browse.sock` serves the page over Q2SPOT/1, and `/tmp/q2-<id>.state` holds `hold=1` (standby kept off) and the QR page's `addr`, `pin`, `qr`, `qrsize`, `note`. USB storage (`usbmode_page_init`'s hook, `addon_card_away`) asks every app to end at once and waits up to 3 s in all, then starts each again once the card is back. No app starts while the Apps page installs or removes its folder (`shelf_busy`). A stop removes a pid file whose loop is gone, but it trusts the pid inside: a loop that died without removing it, its pid since reused, would TERM that process (a `flock` in `run` would close this). The QR page draws up to 49 modules (`QR_HEX`).

## 2038

The rootfs is glibc 2.28 on 32-bit MIPS, so stock's `time_t` is 32 bits, and the root certificates in `assets/scrobble-ca.pem` expire between 2035 and 2046.

**TLS.** Every verified libcurl handle (Network Update, Q2 Shelf, Internet Radio, scrobbling, lyrics and covers) sets `CURLOPT_SSL_CTX_FUNCTION` to `tls_untimed` (`patch/peq.h`). It sets `X509_V_FLAG_NO_CHECK_TIME` in the `SSL_CTX`'s `X509_VERIFY_PARAM`, which each connection inherits, so chains still have to reach a pinned root, but no certificate's dates are checked. demo doesn't import `SSL_CTX_get0_param` or `X509_VERIFY_PARAM_set_flags`, so the callback writes OpenSSL 1.1.1f's fields itself (`SSL_CTX->param` at `0x100`, `->flags` at `0xc`). `tools/build.py` checks that both functions in the rootfs's `libssl`/`libcrypto` still load those offsets. ponytail: an expired or retired server certificate is accepted too, and there is no revocation check. That is the price of working with a firmware nobody can update. Tidal's downloads already run unverified, as its stream does.

**Clock.** Linux 5.10 keeps 64-bit time, and glibc 2.28's `time()` truncates it to 32 bits. `wall_time` reads that unsigned, which is right until 2106. Scrobble timestamps use it. The iPod status bar takes it back in 28-year steps, a span over which dates and weekdays repeat, before stock's 32-bit `localtime`, which is right until 2100. ponytail: stock code stays 32-bit. Its clock screens and its network time sync (`ntpdate`, then `hwclock -wu`) will show or write wrong dates after January 2038, and NTP's 2036 era rollover depends on stock's `ntpdate`. Stock's own HTTPS calls skip verification. Card apps' folder poll (`addon.c`) compares a 32-bit `st_mtime` with `time()`; both wrap together, so only the second of the wrap misjudges a change. librespot carries its own fix (DiamondBond/q2-librespot).
